The fix follows the `commandStateRevision` precedent already established for exactly this class of problem: `WindowCoordinator.commandStateRevision` (`WindowCoordinator.swift:75`) is bumped by `commandStateDidChange()` on tab-switch/key-window-change, and read by `canPerformMarkdownEditingCommand`/`canPerformJSONFormatting` to establish an Observation dependency before doing a real `NSApp.keyWindow?.firstResponder === textView` check. `keyFolderSelection` gains the same first-responder check (Folder Duplicate is enabled only when the sidebar, not the editor's text view, is first responder); a new `canPerformOccurrenceSelection` (same pattern, inverted) gates a new editor-scoped Cmd-D binding for Select Next/All Occurrence. Both bindings stay declared with the same shortcut; AppKit/SwiftUI resolves the enabled one by the responder chain at the moment of the keystroke, exactly as `canPerformMarkdownEditingCommand`'s siblings already rely on today for their own disambiguation. Confirmed before implementing that the underlying event-driven invalidation this fix depends on already exists and already fires on exactly the triggering gesture: `DocumentWindow.sendEvent(_:)` calls `commandStateDidChange()` on every `leftMouseDown`/`rightMouseDown`/`otherMouseDown`/`appKitDefined` event (i.e., every click anywhere in the window, sidebar-to-editor included) — this fix only needs `keyFolderSelection` and the new `canPerformOccurrenceSelection` to actually *read* `commandStateRevision` and perform the real check, not any new invalidation plumbing.

**A second, same-shaped conflict found while tracing this one, fixed under the same change rather than deferred:** `keyFolderSelection`'s fix is applied at its own definition (not just at the "Duplicate" button's call site), since `WorkspaceCommands.swift`'s "Rename" button reuses the identical `coordinator?.keyFolderSelection == nil` gate and is bound to a plain, unmodified Return (`.keyboardShortcut(.return, modifiers: [])`) — a key the editor's own E10 list/blockquote-continuation assists depend on. Before this fix, pressing Return in the editor while a sidebar file happened to be selected would have hit the identical bug Cmd-D had (a live, disclosed finding, not a hypothetical): "Rename" would consume the keystroke instead of inserting a newline. Fixing `keyFolderSelection` at its source instead of only where Cmd-D collides closes both conflicts with one change; "Move to Trash" (Cmd-Delete, no editor collision found) is unaffected in practice but shares the same, now-correct focus-awareness for consistency across all three Folder commands.

**Select Next/All Occurrence semantics, decided here before implementation** (J1/§17's own literal requirement — "Press Cmd-D on a repeated key name to select the next occurrence, repeat to select all three occurrences" — describes the incremental, one-at-a-time convention, not an immediate jump to two occurrences on the first press): matches Sublime Text/VS Code's own established Cmd-D behavior, the only real precedent for this exact gesture. `selectNextOccurrence()`: from a bare caret, selects the word (`CharacterSet.alphanumerics` + `_`, matching `TextSearchEngine`'s own `wordCharacters` definition — duplicated rather than imported, since `EditorCore`/`TextSearch` are sibling targets with no dependency between them, mirroring `TextSearchEngine`'s own documented reason for duplicating scalar-boundary helpers rather than depending on `EditorCore`) at/touching the caret and stops there — it does *not* also jump to a second occurrence on that same press, matching the literal walkthrough's own three-press count for three occurrences. From an existing selection, searches for the next occurrence of that selection's own literal text (case-sensitive, substring match — not restricted to whole-word boundaries for this step, again matching Sublime/VS Code: a word selected by the first press can still match as a substring inside a longer identifier on a later press) starting just after the current primary's end, wrapping to the document start, and skips any occurrence already present in the selection set — so a further press once every occurrence in the document is already selected is a correct, disclosed no-op rather than a re-add or a crash. `selectAllOccurrences()` shares the same text-to-match derivation but replaces `selectionSet` wholesale with every occurrence in one call, usable standalone from a bare caret or an existing selection, not only as a continuation of `selectNextOccurrence()`. Bound to ⌘⇧L (confirmed collision-free against every existing shortcut in the app before choosing it, matching the same background-research discipline Add Cursor Above/Below's own binding used) — the same convention VS Code itself uses for "Select All Occurrences," chosen for recognizability over inventing a novel binding.

**E10 assist interaction (not yet covered by §7.2/§7.3's existing text):** `EditorTextSystem+EditingAssists.swift`'s `performMarkdownCommand` and `EditorView.Coordinator`'s `shouldChangeTextIn`/`doCommandBy` call `MarkdownEditingAssistEngine.outcome(..., selection: system.selectedRange, ...)` — singular only — and `applyAssistOutcome(_:)` calls `textView.setSelectedRange(...)` (singular) in both its `.selection(range)` and `.edit(edit)` branches. Re-running a full per-caret assist pass for every selection in a multi-selection would risk N divergent, order-dependent outcomes for list continuation/blockquote/auto-pair assists that were never designed with more than one caret in mind. Slice 3 therefore draws the line at **typing and structural editing** (§7.2's multi-cursor typing flow: plain character insertion, delete, and paste all route through `EditorEditTransaction` and apply identically to every selection) while **Markdown-specific editing assists remain single-selection-only for this slice**: `MarkdownEditingAssistEngine.outcome` is called only when `!selectionSet.isMultiple`, and multi-selection input that would otherwise trigger an assist (e.g. Return inside a list item, or Tab/Shift-Tab's existing `.insertTab`/`.insertBacktab` assist actions) falls through to the plain multi-cursor transaction path instead — a real, disclosed scope line, not silently dropped functionality, and one later slice-4-or-beyond issue can extend if the owner wants multi-caret-aware assists specifically.

**Scope correction against this contract's own first draft:** an earlier version of this section (and of §17's Slice 3 entry) listed "indent/comment-toggle" as part of what Slice 3a routes through `EditorEditTransaction`. Neither exists as a general, non-Markdown-assist command anywhere in this codebase yet — comment-toggle is explicitly a Slice 4 deliverable (§17's Slice 4 goal: "the ten built-in transforms... case/indent/comment-toggle"), and general indent is bound up with Slice 4's `LanguageEditingProfile` work, not a pre-existing mechanism Slice 3 could route multi-selection input into. The only indent-adjacent behavior that exists today is Tab/Shift-Tab's `.insertTab`/`.insertBacktab` E10 assist actions, which are covered by the single-selection-only scope line directly above like any other Markdown assist. This is corrected here, before implementation, rather than silently implemented differently from what was documented.

**Architecture decision:** `EditorTextSystem` gains `selectionSet: EditorSelectionSet` as its actual selection source of truth, replacing raw AppKit `selectedRanges` reads as the thing every other component (status bar, outline/preview sync, assists) consults — exactly as already specified in §7.1. `selectedRange` is **not removed**; it becomes computed as `selectionSet.primaryRange`, so every existing single-selection consumer (`EditorTextSystem+DocumentReplacement`, `TextFilterCoordinator`, session restore, Slice 2b's status bar and Go to Line) keeps compiling and behaving identically. `EditorView.Coordinator.textViewDidChangeSelection` becomes the single point that reads `textView.selectedRanges` and reconstructs `selectionSet` via `EditorSelectionSet(selectedRanges:)`; `EditorView.onSelectionChange` keeps its existing single-`NSRange` signature (per the epic's explicit "preview/outline tracking continues to follow only the primary selection"), fed `selectionSet.primaryRange`, so nothing outside `EditorTextSystem` needs to become selection-set-aware to keep working.

**Escape-collapse:** a new `EditorView.Coordinator` handling in `doCommandBy:` for `cancelOperation:` — when `selectionSet.isMultiple`, collapses to `selectionSet.primaryRange` (dropping every other caret) and consumes the key event (`return true`, no beep); when already single, falls through to AppKit's existing default handling unchanged (so Escape's other native behaviors, e.g. dismissing a completion panel if one is ever added, are never shadowed by this feature).

**Sub-slice split, decided at the point this slice began** (same `EPIC_STANDARD.md` "detailed contract fixed when the slice starts" convention already used for Slice 2's 2a/2b/2c split, §17): multi-cursor has no precedent anywhere in this codebase and spans three genuinely separable interaction categories — a foundational data-model change every later part depends on, native mouse/keyboard interaction (the largest, riskiest AppKit surface: `NSEvent` handling, drag tracking, modifier-key state), and occurrence-based selection plus the Cmd-D routing fix (self-contained, depends only on the foundation). Bundling all three into one PR would repeat the exact "monolithic change, differently-risky deliverables" failure mode Slice 2's split was written to avoid.
- **Slice 3a** — Foundation: `EditorTextSystem.selectionSet`/computed `selectedRange`, `EditorView.Coordinator`'s selection-set-aware `textViewDidChangeSelection`, Escape-collapse, and multi-range typing/delete/paste routed through `EditorEditTransaction` per §7.2 — the piece every later part of Slice 3 (and Slice 4's multi-cursor-aware transforms, per its own stated dependency on `EditorEditTransaction`) needs to exist first.
- **Slice 3b** — Native interactions: Option-click add/remove caret, Add Cursor Above/Below, rectangular/column selection via Option-drag — the novel, precedent-free AppKit event-handling surface, sequenced once 3a is merged and proven so it can be built and tested against a real, working multi-selection data model rather than against a stub.
- **Slice 3c** — Occurrence selection: Select Next/All Occurrence bound to Cmd-D while the editor is first responder, plus the `keyFolderSelection`/`canPerformOccurrenceSelection` first-responder disambiguation fix described above — self-contained, depends only on 3a's `selectionSet`, sequenced last because it is the lowest-risk remaining piece and benefits from 3a/3b's multi-selection plumbing already being proven in real use.

**Non-negotiable invariants this contract exists to enforce (§4 restated for this slice specifically):** one user-visible command (a keystroke, a paste, an Option-click) that touches N selections produces exactly one undo group and exactly one document publication — never N of either, per `EditorEditTransaction`'s own existing contract, reused rather than reinvented. IME/marked-text sessions are never intercepted by any multi-cursor logic (`shouldChangeTextIn`'s existing marked-range guard, extended to also gate the new multi-selection branch, so a session with an active input method falls open to native single-caret AppKit handling exactly as it does today). No new actor: `EditorSelectionSet` stays a plain `Sendable` value type mutated only on the main actor inside `EditorTextSystem`'s existing edit/selection call sites, per §8.

**Tests:** per issue #112's own adversarial-requirements list — overlapping/adjacent selections normalize correctly on every mutation (already covered by `EditorSelectionSet`'s Slice 1 suite; Slice 3 adds integration-level coverage that `EditorTextSystem.selectionSet` reconstructs correctly from real AppKit `selectedRanges` callbacks); duplicate/identical cursors; emoji/CJK/combining-character selection boundaries; CRLF boundaries; first-line/last-line edge selections; multi-cursor paste (a single clipboard string applied identically to every selection, and the documented-but-not-yet-decided N-strings-to-N-selections case explicitly deferred if raised, since issue #112 does not require it); undo/redo restoring the full selection set, not just the primary range; auto-pair/comment/indent applied identically across every selection for 3a's scope; a real, AppKit-driven (not synthetic) external full-document replacement while a multi-selection is active, confirming `clamped(toLength:)` prevents any out-of-bounds range from reaching AppKit; IME marked-text safety (marked-text session active ⇒ multi-cursor logic never engages, native single-caret path handles it); a 100+-cursor stress case against the §11 performance budget. Slice 3b adds real, mounted-window `NSEvent`-driven tests for Option-click add/remove, Add/Remove Cursor Above/Below at document boundaries, and rectangular selection across wrapped lines and tab stops. Slice 3c adds a live-mount test confirming Cmd-D routes to Select Next Occurrence when the text view is first responder and to Folder Duplicate when the sidebar is, plus the existing occurrence-selection adversarial cases (no match, single match, overlapping matches, case sensitivity per whatever default the issue specifies).

**Architecture correction, found during Slice 3a's own implementation (empirical, not theoretical):** this contract's own §7.1 diagram, and the sub-slice split above, both assumed `NSTextView.selectedRanges` can hold multiple simultaneous ZERO-LENGTH ranges (i.e. multiple bare carets with no selected text) the same way it holds multiple genuine, non-zero-length selections. Directly probing live AppKit (a real mounted `EditorTextView`, `swift test`, not documentation) during 3a's implementation disproved this:
- Two non-zero-length, non-touching selections round-trip through `textView.selectedRanges` correctly (`[{0,3},{4,3}]` in, `[{0,3},{4,3}]` out) — this is the case Cmd-D/Select-All-Occurrence (Slice 3c) actually produces, and it works exactly as this contract assumed.
- Two *touching* non-zero-length ranges (e.g. `{1,1}` and `{2,1}`) are silently MERGED by AppKit into one (`{1,2}`) the moment they are assigned — never two distinct, adjacent selections.
- Two or more ZERO-LENGTH ranges (bare carets), at any spacing — adjacent, one character apart, or far apart (`{3,0}`/`{7,0}`/`{11,0}`) — are collapsed by AppKit down to just the FIRST one the moment they are assigned, via both the `selectedRanges` property setter and the explicit `setSelectedRanges(_:affinity:stillSelecting:)` method. This is not a bug in this codebase's own code; it is `NSTextView`'s own native behavior, unrelated to `EditorTextView`'s Slice 2c invisibles override (confirmed with a plain, unmodified probe).

**Implication:** genuine multi-*caret* editing — Option-click to add a bare caret with nothing selected, Add/Remove Cursor Above/Below — cannot be represented via `textView.selectedRanges` at all, at any point; there is no alternate stock AppKit API that behaves differently (`setSelectedRanges(_:affinity:stillSelecting:)` was tried directly and shows the identical collapse). Rendering and tracking more than one simultaneous bare caret therefore requires state and drawing kept **entirely outside** `textView.selectedRanges` — analogous in kind (though not necessarily in size) to Slice 2c's own custom, non-AppKit-native invisibles drawing — including a decision for how `EditorTextSystem.selectionSet`'s cache invalidation behaves once it holds carets AppKit itself cannot corroborate, and how click/arrow-key handling distinguishes "the user moved the primary caret" from "the user's action should also affect every stored-but-unrendered caret." Multi-*selection* editing (multiple real, non-zero-length, non-touching ranges — exactly what Slice 3c's occurrence-selection feature needs) is unaffected and fully achievable with the design already in this contract.

The same collapse also shows up on the **output** side of an otherwise-achievable multi-selection edit: replacing three real selections with new text via one `EditorEditTransaction` naturally produces three resulting zero-length carets (`EditorEditTransaction.resultingCaretRanges` computes all three correctly), but only the first survives once installed into `textView.selectedRanges`, for the identical reason. This is a real, disclosed limitation of Slice 3a's own achievable scope, not a bug: a single "type once to replace every selected occurrence" command works correctly end to end, but the surviving caret state afterward cannot durably represent all three positions for a hypothetical second keystroke to continue affecting all of them — that would need the same Slice-3b-or-later custom caret tracking as bare-caret input does.

**Scope correction applied to 3a as a result:** 3a ships exactly what is achievable and empirically verified — `selectionSet`/computed `selectedRange`, Escape-collapse, and multi-*selection* (non-zero-length, non-touching ranges) typing/delete via `EditorEditTransaction`. It does **not** attempt bare-caret multi-cursor typing/delete; that scenario cannot be constructed against real AppKit state in the first place, and a test that only sets up an unrealizable precondition would give false confidence, not coverage. **Slice 3b's scope (Option-click add/remove caret, Add/Remove Cursor Above/Below) is flagged here as needing a fresh design pass before its implementation begins** — its current one-line description in this contract and in §17 understates the work: it is a custom caret-tracking-and-rendering feature, not a thin layer over `EditorSelectionSet`/`textView.selectedRanges` the way 3a's own multi-selection work is. This is recorded here as a narrowly-scoped architecture flag for review, per this epic's own "stop and record, do not silently redesign around it" discipline, rather than pushed through as an undisclosed scope expansion inside 3a.

### 6.10 True multi-caret editing (Slice 3b) — design pass, baseline and contract

**Why this section exists before any Slice 3b code:** §6.9's architecture-correction note found, empirically, that `NSTextView.selectedRanges` cannot hold more than one simultaneous bare (zero-length) caret via any known API. Slice 3b's original one-line scope ("Option-click add/remove caret, Add/Remove Cursor Above/Below, rectangular/column selection via Option-drag") assumed it could. This section is the flagged design pass, done before writing any Slice 3b code, per this epic's own "stop and record, do not silently redesign" discipline.

**Baseline, verified against live master (`9090cc8`) before writing this contract:** zero `mouseDown`/`mouseDragged`/`NSEvent`-handling code exists anywhere in `EditorCore` — Slice 3b has no precedent to extend, unlike almost every prior slice in this epic. `EditorTextSystem+Scroll.swift`'s own doc comment already records a directly relevant prior finding: `NSTextView.characterIndexForInsertion(at:)` "can misreport a point inside a correctly laid out fragment as past the END of the document," which is why `topVisibleUTF16Offset` uses `layoutManager.textLayoutFragment(for:)` instead — the same reliable, already-proven technique Slice 3b's own click-to-character-index lookup must reuse rather than reaching for `characterIndexForInsertion(at:)` fresh. `EditorSelectionSet.addRange(_:makePrimary:)`/`removeRange(at:)` (Slice 1) already have the exact API shape a caret-add/remove interaction needs; `EditorTextSystem.selectionSet` (Slice 3a) already caches a caret set that survives a read-after-write as long as nothing else changes `textView.selectedRanges` — a property that turns out to matter a great deal here, since Slice 3b is the first feature whose own carets are NOT reflected in that AppKit array at all.

**Architecture decision:** every caret beyond the primary one is stored ONLY in `EditorTextSystem.selectionSet`, never pushed into `textView.selectedRanges` (which continues to hold just the primary caret/selection, for AppKit's own native purposes — blinking, IME, scroll-to-caret, accessibility). `EditorTextView` gains a second custom-drawing pass, alongside Slice 2c's invisibles pass, that paints a non-blinking vertical bar (the same `insertionPointColor` AppKit uses for the real caret) at each non-primary caret's position — using the identical viewport-bounded fragment-enumeration technique the gutter, invisibles, and scroll APIs already established. Non-blinking is a deliberate simplification, not an oversight: synchronizing a second timer to AppKit's own private blink cadence would be real, novel complexity for a purely cosmetic property; a solid indicator still unambiguously shows where each caret is.

**Sub-slice split — Slice 3b's original scope is too large for one PR once its real shape is known, for the same "don't bundle differently-risky changes" reason every prior slice in this epic was split:**
- **Slice 3b-i — Rendering foundation.** The custom multi-caret drawing pass described above, with no new interaction yet: a test constructs a `selectionSet` with several carets directly (mirroring how `EditorTextViewTests` already constructs invisibles fixtures directly) and confirms each one draws at the expected position, viewport-bounded, none drawn for a caret scrolled out of view. This is the safe, visually-verifiable increment every later part of 3b needs first.
- **Slice 3b-ii — Option-click add/remove.** `EditorTextView.mouseDown(with:)` override: `event.modifierFlags.contains(.option)` and a plain (single) click ⇒ resolve the click point to a UTF-16 offset, then `addRange(_:makePrimary: false)` if no existing caret sits there, or remove it (toggle) if one does; anything else (no Option) falls through to `super.mouseDown(with:)` unchanged — including that not calling `super` for a handled Option-click means AppKit's own internal drag-tracking loop never starts, so an Option-*drag* does nothing beyond placing the initial caret until Slice 3b-iv implements it separately, which is the correct minimal scope here, not a gap. Point-to-offset resolution uses `NSTextInputClient.characterIndex(for:)` (screen-space), confirmed empirically against this exact TextKit 2 stack (a direct probe: `firstRect(forCharacterRange:)`'s known-correct offset→point mapping round-tripped back through `characterIndex(for:)` for 7 offsets spanning a line wrap, all exact) — not the fragment-only lookup `topVisibleUTF16Offset` uses (that one only ever needed fragment granularity, not a precise character position within a line).
- **Slice 3b-ii-b — Add/Remove Cursor Above/Below.** Split out from 3b-ii once that slice began: Option-click (mouse-driven, self-contained within `EditorCore`/`EditorTextView`) and Add/Remove Cursor Above/Below (keyboard-driven, needs app-target menu/shortcut wiring — a different layer and risk profile, mirroring why Go to Line's Slice 2b work touched the app target while the same slice's pure-`EditorCore` line-index work didn't) are different enough in kind to split, per the same "don't bundle differently-risky changes" reasoning already used throughout this epic. Given the primary caret's current line/column (`EditorLineIndex`), adds a caret at the same column on the line above/below, clamped to that line's actual length — the standard "sticky column" caret-vertical-movement convention, not novel to invent, but real column-clamping logic needs its own dedicated tests (a shorter line above/below, first/last line, CJK/emoji columns).
- **Slice 3b-iii — Synchronized caret movement.** The single largest open question this design pass surfaces: once more than one caret exists, does pressing an arrow key move all of them together (the universal convention in every real multi-cursor editor), and if so, which movement commands are in scope? Intercepting AppKit's entire movement-selector surface (there are dozens: `moveRight:`, `moveWordForward:`, `moveToEndOfParagraph:`, `moveToBeginningOfDocument:`, and their `AndModifySelection:` counterparts, etc.) in one PR would itself be a "monolithic change." **Decision, made here rather than deferred:** Slice 3b-iii's own first cut covers exactly the plain single-character and single-line arrow keys (`moveLeft:`, `moveRight:`, `moveUp:`, `moveDown:` — the four `NSResponder` selectors, no modifiers) applied identically to every caret via `EditorLineIndex`; every other movement/selection-extension selector is explicitly, disclosedly out of scope for 3b-iii and continues to move only the primary caret (collapsing the others is NOT correct either — they should stay put, matching "an unhandled command does nothing to what this feature doesn't yet own," not "an unhandled command silently drops the feature's own state"). Broadening this list is a natural, separately-scoped follow-up once the pattern is proven for the smallest real case.
- **Slice 3b-iv — Rectangular (column) selection via Option-drag.** `mouseDragged(with:)` tracking a rectangle between mouse-down and the current drag point, converted to one selection range per line the rectangle spans (per-line fragment geometry, mirroring the gutter/invisibles per-line iteration pattern), each column-clamped to that line's own length — genuinely independent of 3b-ii/3b-iii's caret-movement work, since it produces ordinary `EditorSelectionSet` ranges (which Slice 3a's typing/delete/paste mechanism already handles) rather than bare carets, so it can be built and merged in any order relative to those two once 3b-i's rendering foundation exists.

**Non-negotiable invariants this contract exists to enforce (§4 restated for this slice specifically):** the primary caret/selection is always exactly what `textView.selectedRanges` reports — no Slice 3b interaction ever writes a non-primary caret into that array, so every existing single-selection consumer (status bar, outline sync, E10 assists, Slice 3a's own `applyMultiCursorInsert`/`DeleteSelection`/paste) continues to see exactly what it already expects without becoming aware synthetic carets exist. Typing/delete/paste while synthetic (non-primary, zero-length) carets are active is explicitly **not** promised by 3b-i/ii/iii/iv alone: Slice 3a's mechanism already requires every range in a `count > 1` `selectionSet` to be non-empty (checked, not assumed, per its own `allSatisfy` guard) — extending typing/delete/paste to a mix of real selections and bare synthetic carets is a distinct, not-yet-scoped follow-up, and until it exists, that `allSatisfy` guard is expected to correctly decline and fall through to native single-caret handling whenever synthetic carets are present, rather than silently mishandling them.

**Tests:** 3b-i's direct-construction rendering tests as described above, extended with the adversarial fixtures §15 already calls out (100+ simultaneous carets against the §11 performance budget, carets on wrapped lines, light/dark theme). 3b-ii adds real, `NSEvent`-driven mounted-window tests for Option-click add at a fresh location, Option-click remove on an existing non-primary caret, and Add/Remove Cursor Above/Below at document boundaries (first/last line, a shorter adjacent line, CJK/emoji column positions). 3b-iii adds tests confirming plain arrow-key movement shifts every caret by the same logical amount (not the same UTF-16 offset, where CJK/emoji/combining sequences differ) and that an out-of-scope movement command (e.g. Cmd-Right) leaves every non-primary caret exactly where it was. 3b-iv adds rectangular-selection tests across wrapped lines, tab stops, and a drag that starts or ends past either end of a line.

### 6.11 Core text commands + language profiles (Slice 4) — baseline and contract

**Baseline, verified against live master (`645edc8`) before writing this contract:** `LanguageEditingProfile` (§6.4) is exactly what that section's own doc comment already disclosed — a real, `Sendable`/`Equatable` value type with `lineComment`/`blockComment`/`pairedDelimiters`/`indentAfterTrailing`/`defaultIndentWidth`, plus a `.plainText` all-nil default — but a repo-wide grep confirms it is consumed **nowhere**: no per-format table exists, nothing constructs a non-default instance, and `EditorTextSystem`/`MarkdownEditingAssistEngine`/`DocumentEditorSplitView` never reference the type. The single existing gate this slice must generalize is `DocumentEditorSplitView.swift`'s `config.editingAssists = document.format.id == "markdown" ? Self.assistConfiguration(from:) : .disabled` — every non-Markdown format gets NO editing assists at all today, not even generic pairing/indent.

None of the ten built-in transforms (Duplicate/Delete/Move/Join/Sort/Dedupe/Trim Line, case conversion, indent-adjust, comment-toggle) exist anywhere in the codebase — confirmed by grep across `EditorCore` and the app target. The closest existing precedent for HOW to build them is `EditorTextSystem+MultiCursor.swift`'s `applyMultiCursorInsert`/`applyMultiCursorDeleteSelection` (Slice 3a): build `[TextReplacement]` from the current `selectionSet`, validate via `EditorEditTransaction.validate(_:documentLength:)`, compute the resulting selection via `EditorEditTransaction.resultingCaretRanges(for:)`, then `apply(_:)` for one undo group and one publication — every transform in this slice is expected to follow this exact shape, extended with per-line geometry from `EditorLineIndex` where a transform operates on whole lines rather than raw selection ranges.

The fenced-code/front-matter classifier (`FencedRegionClassifier`, named already in §7.3's dispatch diagram and §16's file list) is genuinely new work with no reusable prior art: `MarkdownEngine/FrontMatter.swift`'s `FrontMatterExtractor` is a whole-document, single-pass scan with no incremental/local-scan API, and the `Diagrams`/`DiagramsGraphviz`/`DiagramsD2` fence scanners all walk the debounced swift-markdown AST via `SourceMap` — both architecturally disqualified by §2.1's own binding E10 rule ("no full Markdown parse on the hot path... read local ranges from live text storage"), which this classifier inherits verbatim. Only the delimiter definitions themselves (` ``` `/`~~~` fences, `---`/`...` front-matter markers) carry over as reusable constants, not any scan strategy.

`FileFormatRegistry.defaultFormats` (`FileCore/FileFormat.swift`) has 16 entries (markdown, html, json, yaml, toml, javascript, typescript, python, ruby, css, swift, c, bash, sql, xml, plaintext); `FileFormat` itself carries no comment-syntax field, and no comment-delimiter table exists anywhere in `Highlighting` (the package's `.scm` query files classify syntax-highlighting tokens, not comment delimiters) or `FileCore`. Slice 4 must build this table from scratch — there is nothing to generalize from, only a green field to populate for all 16 formats.

**Architecture decision:** a new `LanguageEditingProfileRegistry` (name chosen here; `EditorCore`, since profiles are pure editor mechanics with no `Highlighting`/`FileCore` dependency needed beyond the `FileFormat.id`/`highlightLanguageID` string key already used to look up grammars) holds one hand-authored `LanguageEditingProfile` per registered format — reusing `PairedDelimiter.structural`'s existing bracket/quote table for every format that wants it (nearly all of them; `plaintext` stays `.plainText`), adding format-specific `lineComment`/`blockComment`/`indentAfterTrailing` per language's own well-known conventions (e.g. `//` for swift/javascript/typescript/css/c, `#` for python/ruby/bash/yaml/toml, `<!-- -->` for html/xml/markdown, none for json/sql-standard/plaintext). `DocumentEditorSplitView`'s gate changes from "Markdown or disabled" to "every format gets `EditingAssistConfiguration` built from ITS OWN profile's pairing/indent-after-trailing behavior; Markdown additionally layers its existing symmetric-delimiter/list/blockquote/heading extras on top, gated exactly as today." This is the generalization §6.4 already promised, made concrete.

Per §7.3's already-recorded dispatch diagram, `FencedRegionClassifier.classify(text:atUTF16Offset:)` returns `.prose | .fencedCode(languageID: String?) | .frontMatter` via a bounded backward scan from the caret to the nearest preceding unmatched fence/`---` delimiter pair — never forward, never whole-document, matching every other "local, synchronous" precedent this codebase has already established (`EditorLineIndex`'s incremental rescan, `EditorTextSystem+Scroll.swift`'s viewport-bounded fragment lookups). Inside a classified fenced-code region, Markdown-specific assists (list continuation, blockquote, symmetric-delimiter completion) do NOT run; the fence's own tagged language's profile (if registered) drives general pairing/indent instead, falling back to `.plainText` for an untagged or unregistered fence language. Comment-toggle inside a Markdown document specifically consults this classifier to pick the fenced language's own comment delimiter rather than Markdown's (which has no conventional line-comment syntax of its own) when the caret/selection is inside a fence.

**Sub-slice split, decided at the point this slice began** (same convention as every prior multi-part slice, §17): the foundational profile-registry/gate generalization, the genuinely novel fenced-code/front-matter classifier, and the ten built-in transforms are three differently-risky, largely independent deliverables — bundling them would repeat the same "monolithic change" failure mode this epic has split around at every prior opportunity:
- **Slice 4a** — `LanguageEditingProfileRegistry` + generalizing `DocumentEditorSplitView`'s gate so every registered format gets a real, profile-driven pairing/indent-after-trailing engine, while Markdown's own extras stay exactly as gated today. Foundational: 4b and 4c's comment-toggle both depend on real per-format profiles existing.
- **Slice 4b** — `FencedRegionClassifier` + gating Markdown-specific assists behind it, closing the E10 inherited-debt item (§2.3). Depends on 4a for the correct non-Markdown fallback behavior inside a fence.
- **Slice 4c** — The ten built-in transforms via `EditorEditTransaction`, multi-cursor aware. Nine depend only on already-complete Slice 1/3 machinery; comment-toggle specifically also depends on 4a (per-format delimiters) and 4b (fence-aware delimiter selection inside Markdown). Whether 4c itself needs a further split (e.g. line-reordering transforms vs. text-transform/comment-toggle) is deferred to when 4c actually begins, per this epic's own "detailed contract fixed when the slice starts" convention — not pre-committed here.

**Tests:** per §17's own line — the fenced-code/front-matter adversarial corpus already itemized in §15 (caret moving into/out of a fence via arrow/click/programmatic jump, pasted fence, blockquote-nested fence, list-nested fence, front matter immediately followed by a fence, CRLF-delimited fences, a bounded-scan-must-not-degrade case on a large document) for 4b; format-neutral pairing/indent tests across every one of the 16 registered `FileFormat`s for 4a; per-transform correctness plus multi-cursor-simultaneous-application tests, using the same undo-step/publication-count assertions Slice 3a's own multi-cursor tests already established, for 4c.

### 6.12 `FencedRegionClassifier` (Slice 4b) — baseline and algorithm, decided before implementation

**Baseline, verified against live master (`deb1ec7`) before writing this contract:** confirmed again (Slice 4a's own baseline already established this) that no incremental, local fence-detection API exists anywhere in this codebase — `MarkdownEngine/FrontMatter.swift`'s `FrontMatterExtractor` and the `Diagrams*` package fence scanners are both disqualified for the reasons already recorded in §6.11. `MarkdownEditingAssistEngine+LineHelpers.swift` already has the exact line-boundary primitives this classifier needs (`lineStart(of:in:)`, `lineContentEnd(of:in:)`, `character(at:in:)`), all `internal`, so `FencedRegionClassifier` (a new type, same module) reuses them directly rather than re-deriving line-boundary math a third time.

**The core tension a "bounded, local, backward-only" scan must resolve, decided here explicitly rather than glossed over:** whether a UTF-16 offset sits inside a fenced code block is, in the general case, a question about the PARITY of fence-delimiter lines between the document's own start and that offset — fences do not nest (CommonMark), so each one simply toggles "inside/outside" going forward. A scan that stops early (anywhere short of the document's actual start) without having reached a point of *known* state cannot, in principle, report a parity it hasn't actually established. The decision: scan backward from the caret's own line, counting fence-delimiter lines crossed, up to a generous but finite cap (`maximumFenceLinesScanned`, currently 5,000 lines — reduced from an initial 20,000 during Slice 4b's own hostile-review fix round, once per-keystroke cost at the cap was measured at ~0.5ms/1,000 lines and pinned by its own test) — for the overwhelming majority of real documents this reaches offset 0 (a document's own start is unconditionally "outside any fence," the one free known-state anchor available without parsing anything), giving an EXACT answer via full parity from a real baseline. If the cap is reached before the scan reaches offset 0, `classify` does NOT special-case that outcome — it simply reports whatever parity it has accumulated so far from the partial scan (odd → `.fencedCode`, even → `.prose`), which is only a correct answer if that partial parity happens to match the true, full-document parity. This is a disclosed approximation for a single pathological case (a caret buried more than `maximumFenceLinesScanned` lines past the last fence boundary in an enormous document with no closer for that long) — an independent review confirmed this exact gap by constructing a counter-example (an unclosed opener more than the cap's own distance before the caret, with intervening prose past the cap) that classifies as `.fencedCode` — not a claim of correctness at unbounded distance, and not the "always falls back to `.prose`" behavior an earlier draft of this paragraph incorrectly described. This is the same class of disclosed tradeoff as `EditorPerformanceTests`' own "budget, not perfection, for the pathological case" precedent already established elsewhere in this epic.

**Fence-delimiter-line detection is a practical, disclosed approximation of CommonMark's own fence-matching grammar, not a claim of full conformance:** a line qualifies as a fence delimiter here if, after up to 3 leading space characters, it has 3 or more of the same character (backtick or tilde) — full stop. Real CommonMark additionally requires a closing fence to be at least as long as its opener and forbids a backtick in a backtick-fence's own info string; neither rule is enforced here. This is adequate for gating editing assists (the actual purpose of this classifier) and is explicitly not a second Markdown parser competing with `MarkdownEngine`'s own real one. The reported `languageID` for a `.fencedCode` classification is the fence's info string, trimmed and reduced to its first whitespace-delimited token, lowercased — matched directly against `LanguageEditingProfileRegistry`'s own format-id keys (e.g. an info string of `swift` or `python`); an unregistered or absent tag falls back to `.plainText`, the same graceful-degradation convention the registry itself already uses.

**Front matter is detected independently of the fence-parity scan, not folded into it:** a document opens with front matter only if its very first line is exactly `---`; the block's own end is found by scanning FORWARD from there (bounded by a much smaller cap, since a real front-matter block is conventionally a handful of lines, not the whole-document scan E10's hot-path rule forbids — that rule is about scans proportional to document size, not a small, definitionally-bounded prefix) until a line that is exactly `---` or `...` is found, or the smaller cap is exhausted (treated as "no front matter" — a malformed, never-closed `---` at line 1 does not hang this classifier). Any offset inside that span is unconditionally `.frontMatter`, short-circuiting before the fence scan ever runs — front matter's own YAML syntax has no Markdown fences of its own to consider.

**Wiring point, matching §7.3's own dispatch diagram exactly:** rather than touch either of `MarkdownEditingAssistEngine`'s call sites (`EditorView.swift`, `EditorTextSystem+EditingAssists.swift`), the classification and the resulting configuration/profile substitution both happen INSIDE `MarkdownEditingAssistEngine.outcome(for:text:selection:configuration:profile:)` itself, once, immediately after the existing `configuration.isEnabled` guard — every existing call site is completely unaffected. When `configuration.isMarkdownFormat` and the classifier reports `.fencedCode`/`.frontMatter` for the caret's own location, an adjusted configuration (Markdown-specific flags forced off, `isMarkdownFormat` forced false so a nested nothing-special-happens) and the fenced language's own profile (or `.plainText`) are substituted for the rest of that one call — never persisted, recomputed fresh on every action. `.markdownCommand` (the explicit Bold/Italic/Heading menu commands) is deliberately **not** gated by this classifier in this slice: those are a different interaction model (a deliberate, explicit user action, not an ambient auto-assist), and §7.3's own diagram is specifically about the ambient typing/Return/Tab assists this classifier's own name describes. Extending fence-awareness to the menu commands too, if ever wanted, is an explicitly out-of-scope, separately-decided follow-up, not silently bundled here.

### 6.13 The ten built-in transforms (Slice 4c) — design pass, further split, decided before implementation

**Baseline, verified against live master (`75b78e8`) before writing this design pass:** re-confirmed §6.11's own original baseline claim — a repo-wide grep for every transform's own obvious name (`Duplicate Line`, `Move Line`, `Sort Lines`, `Dedupe`, `Trim Trailing`, `toggleComment`, case-conversion helpers) finds nothing in `EditorCore` or the app target; none of the ten exist anywhere. Two pieces of real, reusable machinery already exist and change this slice's actual scope, found only by reading (not assuming) the current Tab/Shift-Tab implementation:
- `MarkdownEditingAssistEngine+Indentation.swift` already has a complete, tested, per-line indent/outdent engine (`indentSelectedLines`/`unindentSelectedLines`/`transformSelectedLines`, plus the exact-UTF-16-delta selection remapping `remappedSelection`/`remappedLocation` it depends on) — but it is reachable ONLY through the single-selection E10 assist dispatch (`EditorView.swift`'s `doCommand(by:)`, which calls `MarkdownEditingAssistEngine.outcome(for:text:selection:...)` with `system.selectedRange`, a single `NSRange`). With multiple selections/carets active (`EditorTextSystem.selectionSet.ranges.count > 1`), pressing Tab/Shift-Tab today only ever indents/outdents the PRIMARY selection — the other active selections are untouched, exactly the "not multi-cursor aware" gap §17's own Slice 4c goal line names for "indent-adjust." This means indent-adjust is NOT a from-scratch algorithm: it is a new, explicit command that calls the SAME existing per-line transform once per active selection (independently, since each selection's own line range is disjoint from every other's by construction) and combines the N resulting replacements into one `EditorEditTransaction` — mirroring `EditorTextSystem+MultiCursor.swift`'s own established "N replacements, one transaction" shape rather than inventing a new one. It is deliberately a SEPARATE command from the Tab key itself (bound to Cmd-]/Cmd-[, see below), not a change to Tab/Shift-Tab's own single-selection-only E10 assist scope (§7.2's already-recorded "Markdown assist commands stay single-selection-only; multi-selection input falls through to the plain transaction path" line) — keeping the existing, already-shipped Tab-key behavior completely untouched and avoiding a second, riskier change to E10's own dispatch for this slice.
- `LanguageEditingProfile.lineComment`/`blockComment` (Slice 4a) and `FencedRegionClassifier` (Slice 4b) are both already real and landed — comment-toggle's two stated dependencies are satisfied; nothing new needs to be built for it besides the toggle command itself.

The other eight transforms (Duplicate/Delete/Move Up/Move Down/Join/Sort/Dedupe/Trim/case) have no existing implementation or reusable per-transform logic beyond the general `EditorEditTransaction`/`EditorLineIndex` primitives every transform in this slice is built on, confirming §6.11's original "nine depend only on already-complete Slice 1/3 machinery" framing for those (indent-adjust is the one exception just described, and is still Slice-1/3-only in its dependencies, just not zero-new-code).

**Per-transform scope, decided here rather than left to each sub-slice to improvise differently:**
- **Duplicate Line** — for each active selection/caret, the full logical line-block it touches (`EditorLineIndex.utf16Range(ofLine:in:)` for every line from the first to the last line the selection spans) is duplicated immediately after itself, including its own trailing terminator; the resulting caret/selection lands on the duplicate, at the same column(s) as before — matching Xcode/VSCode/Sublime's own shared convention.
- **Delete Line** — the full line-block touched by each selection/caret is removed entirely, including its own terminator (except a document's actual last line, which has none to remove); the resulting caret lands at the start of whatever line now occupies that position.
- **Move Line Up / Move Line Down** — the touched line-block swaps position with the single adjacent line immediately above/below it; a no-op (not an error) when the block already touches the document's first/last line in that direction, matching every other "clamp at the edge, don't wrap or crash" precedent already established in this epic (e.g. Add/Remove Cursor Above/Below's own first/last-line no-op).
- **Join Lines** — the touched line-block's lines are merged into one: each internal terminator is replaced with a single space (never simply deleted), and the joined-in line's own leading whitespace is stripped first, matching BBEdit/Xcode's shared "Join Lines" convention (`"foo\n  bar"` → `"foo bar"`, not `"foobar"` or `"foo  bar"`).
- **Multi-selection conflict rule for the four line-reordering transforms above:** each active selection's touched line-block is computed independently, then overlapping or merely-touching blocks (e.g. two carets on the same line, or two carets on vertically adjacent lines for Move Line Down) are merged into one group before the transform runs — never left to silently interact by application order. This is the same "normalize before building replacements" discipline `EditorSelectionSet` itself already applies to raw selection input, applied here one level up at the line-block granularity these four transforms share.
- **Sort Lines** and **Dedupe Lines (Remove Duplicate Lines)** — each requires a real, non-empty selection spanning 2 or more lines to be enabled at all (mirroring `canPerformMarkdownEditingCommand`/`canPerformJSONFormatting`'s existing "the menu item disables itself rather than silently doing something surprising" convention) — deliberately NOT "no selection sorts/dedupes the whole file," since that is a surprising, hard-to-undo-by-accident default this codebase has no precedent for. With multiple active selections, each qualifying selection's own line-block is sorted/deduped independently (N replacements, one transaction, same shape as the reordering transforms) — a selection that doesn't qualify (empty, or a single line) disables the WHOLE command for that keystroke, exactly `applyMultiCursorInsert`'s own `allSatisfy` precedent, rather than silently skipping just that one selection. Dedupe removes a later exact-duplicate line while keeping the first occurrence's own position, case-sensitively (no normalization) — the simplest, least-surprising definition of "duplicate."
- **Trim Trailing Whitespace** — deliberately NOT selection-scoped: applies to the WHOLE DOCUMENT unconditionally, every time it's invoked, matching BBEdit/Sublime's own shared convention for this specific command (a one-shot cleanup action, not a per-selection edit) — always enabled for any non-empty document, trivially "multi-cursor aware" by having no selection dependency to be unaware of.
- **Case conversion** (Uppercase / Lowercase / Capitalized Case, as a 3-item submenu, matching Xcode's own "Convert Case" wording) — requires real, non-empty selected text for every active selection (same `allSatisfy` convention as Sort/Dedupe above); N non-empty selections each get their own content's case transformed independently, N replacements in one transaction — no new selection-processing shape beyond `applyMultiCursorInsert`'s own established one, just `text.uppercased()`/`.lowercased()`/a per-word capitalization helper instead of a fixed inserted string.
- **Increase Indent / Decrease Indent** — see the baseline note above: calls the existing `indentSelectedLines`/`unindentSelectedLines(text:selection:width:)` once per active selection (a bare caret counts as its own one-line "selection" for this purpose, matching Tab/Shift-Tab's own existing collapsed-caret behavior), using each selection's own effective profile/width exactly as `tabOutcome` already resolves it (`profile.defaultIndentWidth ?? configuration.indentationWidth`) — combining the N per-selection edits into one `EditorEditTransaction` is the only genuinely new code; the per-line indent logic itself is reused unchanged.
- **Toggle Comment** — for each active selection/caret's touched line-block, "smart toggle" semantics (VSCode/Sublime/Xcode's shared convention): if every touched line already starts (after its own leading whitespace) with the effective profile's `lineComment` prefix, remove it from all of them; otherwise add it to every touched line that doesn't already have one. Falls back to wrapping the whole block with `blockComment` if the profile has `lineComment == nil` but a `blockComment` (e.g. a hypothetical line-comment-less format); disabled entirely (menu item off) if the profile has neither. Inside Markdown, "effective profile" is resolved exactly the way `MarkdownEditingAssistEngine`'s own `effectiveConfigurationAndProfile` already resolves it for typing (§6.12): the fenced language's own profile when the selection's own anchor line is inside a fence (Markdown itself has no line-comment syntax of its own to toggle), `.plainText`'s otherwise. Each active selection's own line-block is toggled independently using ITS OWN effective profile (a selection spanning both a fence and surrounding prose is scoped to whichever profile its own anchor line resolves to — not a mixed per-line profile within one selection, which would make the smart-toggle "already commented?" check itself ambiguous).

**Command list and shortcuts, fixed here to avoid inventing a different one per sub-slice — checked against every `keyboardShortcut` already declared anywhere in `WorkspaceCommands*.swift` for conflicts:**

| Command | Shortcut |
|---|---|
| Duplicate Line | ⌘⇧D |
| Delete Line | ⌘⇧K |
| Move Line Up | ⌘⌥↑ |
| Move Line Down | ⌘⌥↓ |
| Join Lines | ⌃J |
| Sort Lines | *(menu only)* |
| Dedupe Lines | *(menu only)* |
| Trim Trailing Whitespace | *(menu only)* |
| Convert Case ▸ Uppercase / Lowercase / Capitalized Case | *(menu only)* |
| Increase Indent / Decrease Indent | ⌘] / ⌘[ |
| Toggle Comment | ⌘/ |

None of these collide with an existing shortcut (⌘D/⌘⌦ are Folder Duplicate/Move to Trash and Select Next Occurrence/Cmd-Delete; ⌘⇧L is Select All Occurrences; ⌃⌘↑/⌃⌘↓ are Add/Remove Cursor Above/Below; ⌘⇧J is Reveal Active File; ⌃G is Go to Line; ⌘⇧⌥D is Debug-only) — verified by grep across every `WorkspaceCommands*.swift` file before picking these, not assumed free.

**Further split, decided at the point this sub-slice began** (same "don't bundle differently-risky deliverables" convention as every prior multi-part slice, §17): the four line-reordering transforms share one real risk (the multi-selection line-block merge rule above, genuinely new logic with no existing precedent to lean on); the five block-content transforms share a simpler, lower-risk shape (each selection's own block is rewritten in place, independently, no cross-selection position-shifting to reason about, and three of the five reuse or trivially extend already-existing engine code); comment-toggle is its own smallest, most self-contained unit with its own distinct dependency (the fence/profile resolution above) and no interaction with either other group:
- **Slice 4c-i** — Duplicate Line, Delete Line, Move Line Up, Move Line Down, Join Lines (the multi-selection line-block merge rule, shared by all four).
- **Slice 4c-ii** — Sort Lines, Dedupe Lines, Trim Trailing Whitespace, Convert Case, Increase/Decrease Indent.
- **Slice 4c-iii** — Toggle Comment.

Exact per-file organization (new `EditorCore` extension file(s) per group, new `WorkspaceCommands`/`WindowCoordinator` bridge file(s), following the established one-feature-per-file convention) and the precise test corpus for each are fixed at the point each of 4c-i/4c-ii/4c-iii actually begins, matching this epic's own "detailed contract fixed when the slice starts" convention — not pre-committed here. Slice 4c-i begins next.

### 6.14 Current-document Find/Replace (Slice 5) — design pass, baseline and contract, decided before implementation

**Baseline, verified against live master (`3d24c7d`, immediately after all of Slice 4c landed) before writing this design pass:** `TextSearchEngine.matches(in:query:options:)` (`TextSearch` package, Slice 1) is already fully implemented and tested — literal search (case-sensitive/insensitive, whole-word, Unicode case-folding, CRLF, a 100K-char stress case) and regex search (compile-failure throwing, whole-word via a `\b(?:...)\b` wrap, zero-width-match termination) both work today; this is NOT a stub Slice 5 needs to build from scratch. Two of `SearchOptions`' own fields are real but currently inert: `wraps`' own doc comment already states outright that `matches` itself ignores it — *"wrap behaviour is a stepping/navigation concern the Slice 5 UI layer applies on top of the full match list"* — and `searchesSelectionOnly` is identically inert, both confirmed by a repo-wide grep to have zero consuming logic anywhere yet. Consuming these two fields for the first time IS this slice's own job, not a pre-existing gap to route around.

Find/Replace today is 100% stock `NSTextFinder`: `EditorFind.swift` is a thin, currently-uncalled-by-the-app-target wrapper around `NSTextView.performTextFinderAction:`, and `WorkspaceCommands+TextFormatting.swift`'s own doc comment confirms `.textEditing` (the `CommandGroup` that owns ⌘F/⌘G/⌘⇧F) is deliberately left untouched, so those shortcuts reach AppKit's own built-in find bar purely through the default responder chain. There is no existing custom Find UI, no existing match-highlighting code, and nothing routes through `TextSearchEngine` for actual document search today — this slice is a from-scratch UI build, not wiring up dormant scaffolding.

**Architecture decision — an inline docked bar, not a floating panel, deliberately departing from this epic's own two existing UI precedents:** Go to Line (`GoToLinePanel`/`GoToLineView`, Slice 2b) and Command Palette (`CommandPalettePanel`/`CommandPaletteView`) are both floating `NSPanel`s, and both establish a real, reusable "origin-window-capture" idiom (resolve `NSApp.keyWindow` once, at invocation, before the panel itself becomes key, so a later action can't silently target the palette instead of the document the user meant) that a Find bar would still need for multi-window correctness if it followed the same shape. But Find is invoked far more frequently, stays open far longer, and is interleaved with actually reading/scrolling the document in a way neither existing precedent's own usage pattern matches — every comparable editor (Xcode, VS Code, Sublime, BBEdit) docks Find inline at the top of the editor, not as a floating window that obscures content and requires refocusing. `DocumentEditorSplitView.swift` already uses a SwiftUI `.overlay(alignment:)` for at least one other purpose (`PreviewBusyIndicator`), so an inline overlay is architecturally available there, not something to invent from nothing. Command Palette's own *interaction* shape (stays open, live-updates on every keystroke, multiple sequential actions — arrow-key navigation, repeated invoke — before an explicit dismissal) is still the right behavioral template to follow, even though its *presentation* shape (a floating panel) is not; Go to Line's "submit and vanish" shape is the wrong template for either.

**New stateful component — `EditorFindModel`** (exact file TBD at 5a's own start; `EditorCore` or the app target, decided then): `TextSearchEngine` itself is pure and stateless (it recomputes the full match list from scratch on demand), but a Find bar needs to remember which match is "current" across repeated Find Next/Previous presses, matching every real editor's own expected behavior — this state does not belong on `TextSearchEngine` (a Slice-1 type multiple future consumers, including folder search, must be able to treat as pure) and does not belong on `EditorTextSystem` either (Find-bar state is UI-session state, not document-model state the way `storedSelectionSet`'s cache is). It is a new, dedicated observable model, owned by the Find bar's own SwiftUI view, holding the current query/`SearchOptions`/computed `[SearchMatch]`/current-match index, recomputing matches on every query/option/text change, and exposing `findNext()`/`findPrevious()`/`replaceCurrent()`/`replaceAll()`/`selectAllMatches()` — each of which calls into the associated `EditorTextSystem` to actually scroll to, replace, or multi-select the live text, never mutating text itself.

**Match highlighting is new, from-scratch drawing code, following the invisibles pattern, not the secondary-caret pattern:** `EditorTextView` has no existing generic "draw N highlighted ranges" helper, but it has two directly relevant, already-solved precedents to choose between. Secondary carets (`drawSecondaryCarets()`) resolve each of a small, already-known offset set directly via `firstRect(forCharacterRange:actualRange:)`, with no viewport pre-filtering — justified there because a human's realistic simultaneous-cursor count is inherently small. Match count is NOT inherently small (potentially every occurrence in a 10MB document), so match highlighting must instead follow invisibles' own viewport-bounded `NSTextLayoutManager.enumerateTextLayoutFragments(from:options:)` walk (stopping as soon as a fragment falls outside the dirty rect), respecting the same §4 invariant #3 "no whole-document layout" budget invisibles and the gutter already respect — and reusing the exact `textContainerInset`-vs-text-container coordinate-space conversion invisibles' own doc comment already flags as a past hostile-review-found bug (PR #127), rather than risking that exact mistake a second time in new code. The current match is visually distinct from the others (a different fill, drawn in a second, smaller pass over just that one range).

**Replace/Replace All route through `EditorEditTransaction` unchanged — no new mechanism needed:** that type's own doc comment already describes exactly this shape — *"N simultaneous, non-overlapping ranges... N == 1 is a valid, and encouraged, use of this type"* — so Replace All (N matches, each replaced, one undo group, one publication) and single Replace-current-match (the N==1 case) are both already fully supported. `EditorEditTransaction.resultingCaretRanges(for:)` (already used by Slice 3a's own multi-cursor work) is directly reusable for computing where each replaced match's own resulting caret lands. Per §4 invariant #10 ("one user command remains one undo group... a multi-cursor edit affecting N ranges is one undo step, not N"), Replace All must build ONE transaction covering every replacement, never N separate ones.

**§4 invariant #6's file-conflict-safe-write model is explicitly OUT of scope here, confirmed by the doc's own words, not assumed:** §9/invariant #6 (*"Replace-in-folder target changed since search: that file is skipped and reported... never silently overwritten"*) governs racing an ON-DISK file against an external change during a multi-file operation — that is Slice 7's own folder-wide concern, named explicitly in that slice's own goal line. Slice 5's Replace/Replace All operates purely on the currently-open, in-memory buffer; any eventual disk write still goes through the document's ordinary Save path, already governed elsewhere and untouched by this slice.

**"Select All Matches" reuses `EditorSelectionSet(ranges:primaryIndex:)` directly, fed by `TextSearchEngine.matches` — it does NOT touch or reuse `selectAllOccurrences()` (Cmd-Shift-L, Slice 3c):** that method is different, already-shipped, word/selection-based functionality (`allOccurrenceRanges(of:in:)`, a private literal tiling scan local to `EditorTextSystem+OccurrenceSelection.swift`) with its own separate keyboard shortcut and its own separate UX contract; Slice 5's own "select all matches" is a new call site using the identical general `EditorSelectionSet` constructor Slice 3c's own feature already validated, driven by the Find bar's own current query/options instead of "the word under the caret." No shared code between the two beyond that one already-general-purpose constructor.

**Sub-slice split, decided here rather than attempted as one PR** (same "don't bundle differently-risky deliverables" reasoning as every prior multi-part slice): the foundational live-search-and-navigate experience (a first-ever custom Find UI, a first-ever "highlight N ranges" drawing pass, and the `wraps`/`searchesSelectionOnly` consumption) is the highest-risk, most novel piece; Replace/Replace All is lower-risk once 5a's UI exists, since it reuses `EditorEditTransaction` exactly as already proven; Select All Matches/search-selection-scope are the smallest, most self-contained additions layered on top:
- **Slice 5a** — The Find bar itself: inline docked UI, `EditorFindModel`, live literal/regex matching via `TextSearchEngine`, Find Next/Previous navigation (consuming `wraps` for the first time), and viewport-bounded match highlighting.
- **Slice 5b** — Replace and Replace All, via `EditorEditTransaction`, depending on 5a's own UI and match list.
- **Slice 5c** — Select All Matches (→ `EditorSelectionSet`) and "search selection only" (consuming `searchesSelectionOnly` for the first time).

Exact per-file organization and the precise adversarial/performance test corpus for each (§17's own line: search adversarial corpus; literal-search-in-10MB performance budget) are fixed at the point each of 5a/5b/5c actually begins, matching this epic's own "detailed contract fixed when the slice starts" convention — not pre-committed here. Slice 5a begins next.

### 6.15 Workspace index + Quick Open + recents (Slice 6) — design pass, baseline and contract, decided before implementation

**Baseline, verified against live master (`fdbb783e`, immediately after Slice 5 closed) before writing this design pass — and correcting this section's own stale framing before acting on it:** §17's Slice 6 goal line still reads "`WorkspaceFileIndex` real implementation," inherited unedited from before Slice 1 began. That work already shipped, in Slice 1 (PR #123), not as the "empty shape" Slice 1's own goal line originally described: `WorkspaceFileIndex` (`TextSearch` package) is today a fully working `actor` with a real `state` machine (`.empty`/`.building`/`.ready(count:)`/`.failed`), a generation-counter-guarded `rebuild(root:excluding:) async` running a recursive `Task.detached` walk, a pure in-memory `query(_:limit:) -> [IndexedPath]` scored via `FuzzyPathScore`, and its own `DirectoryWalker` with a `visited: Set<PhysicalFileIdentity.FileObjectID>` symlink-loop guard — already covering recursive indexing, default-directory exclusion, hidden-file exclusion, fuzzy ranking, symlink-loop non-hang, and symlinked-directory traversal correctness in `WorkspaceFileIndexTests.swift`'s existing 9 tests. `FuzzyPathScore.score(query:path:basename:)` (also Slice 1) is likewise already fully implemented and tested (exact > prefix > fuzzy-subsequence ordering, contiguous-run and early-match-position rewards, case/diacritic folding) — genuinely a novel-but-solved algorithm already proven, not something this slice invents. As with `TextSearchEngine.matches` before Slice 5, both are currently unused-by-the-app capability: `FuzzyPathScore` has exactly one call site anywhere (`WorkspaceFileIndex.query` itself) and `WorkspaceFileIndex` has zero references anywhere in the app target. **This slice's real remaining work is wiring already-solved engines to real UI and real workspace state, plus the one genuinely missing piece of engine-side evidence (the 100k-path performance budget) — not building a search/scoring engine from scratch the way Slice 5 had to.**

**Unmet baseline commitment being closed here:** §2.1's own baseline already commits `WorkspaceFileIndex` to consuming `FileTreeModel.root`/`.rootAccessURL` "rather than inventing a second bookmark store," but no code anywhere connects the two today — `WorkspaceFileIndex` is a bare, unowned type with no registry or store deciding who creates or holds an instance. Closing this is Slice 6's own foundational deliverable, not a pre-existing gap being routed around.

**Ownership granularity — one `WorkspaceFileIndex` instance per window, mirroring `FileTreeModel`'s own per-window scope, not `RecentFolderRoots`' app-wide scope:** no existing precedent settles this outright (`EditorFindModelStore`/`EditorTextSystemStore` are per-*tab*-within-a-window caches, a different granularity entirely; `RecentFolderRoots` is a single app-wide instance; `FileTreeModel.root` is per-window). A workspace index is inherently scoped to "the one folder tree this window has open," exactly like `FileTreeModel.root` itself, not to "the app" — an app-wide index would have to silently pick one of several simultaneously open folder roots to search, an ambiguity every comparable multi-window editor (Xcode, VS Code) avoids by scoping its own equivalent per-window. Decision: `WindowController` gains a `workspaceFileIndex: WorkspaceFileIndex` property alongside its existing `fileTreeModel`, constructed at the same point `fileTreeModel` is. It rebuilds via the same observation mechanism that already reacts to `fileTreeModel.root` changing (opening/switching/closing a folder), including once at window construction if a root is already set (e.g. from session restore) — kept warm in the background as soon as a folder is open, rather than built lazily on Quick Open's first keystroke, matching every comparable editor's own indexing behavior and avoiding a visible stall on first invocation for the common case. A window with no folder root open has `workspaceFileIndex.state == .empty` and nothing to index; Quick Open's own guard (below) handles that case explicitly rather than the index silently no-oping.

**`QuickOpenPanel`/`QuickOpenModel` mirror `CommandPalettePanel`'s exact ownership/origin-capture idiom, app-wide-scoped like the palette, not single-document-scoped like `GoToLinePanel`:** the epic's own Slice 6 goal line already calls for mirroring the palette, and the baseline research confirms why that idiom (not Go to Line's) is the right template — Quick Open, like the command palette and unlike Go to Line, is meaningful to invoke from any window state and stays open across a live-filtering, possibly-multi-keystroke session before an explicit choice, not a single "submit and vanish" like Go to Line's line-number field. Concretely: `NSPanel` + `NSWindowDelegate`, `weak coordinator` + `weak originController` captured once at `NSApp.keyWindow` resolution time (never re-read from `NSApp.keyWindow` later), a single strong-owning `quickOpen: QuickOpenPanel?` property on `WindowCoordinator` released via `windowWillClose` → `quickOpenDidClose(self)` exactly like the palette's `commandPaletteDidClose`, the same toggle-to-close behavior, the same deferred-activation dance for `@FocusState` plus post-deferral identity re-check, and the same `removeController(_:)` force-close-if-orphaned handling for the origin window closing out from under it. Filtering is `WorkspaceFileIndex.query(_:limit:)` directly — no separate fuzzy-matching code, and no attempt to unify this with the command palette's own plain-substring `filteredRows` filtering, which issue #117 (deferred to Slice 9 per §17) explicitly owns.

**Quick Open's own "nothing useful to do" guard, the parallel to Go to Line's "no live editor" guard:** `toggleQuickOpen()` refuses to open if `originController.fileTreeModel.root == nil` (no folder open in the origin window) — there is nothing to index or search, and opening an empty, permanently-unusable panel would be worse than not opening at all, matching Go to Line's own precedent of refusing to open rather than opening into a guaranteed-useless state.

**Recent Files — a new store mirroring `RecentFolderRoots`' own bookmark/MRU/cap/staleness-refresh shape exactly, not a novel persistence mechanism, and not AppKit's built-in `NSDocumentController` recent-documents feature, which this app has never opted into:** a repo-wide check confirms zero use of `NSDocumentController`/the standard Recent Items mechanism anywhere in this codebase — this app manages its own document lifecycle already (`WorkspaceModel`/`TabStore`/`WorkspaceSession`) and adding AppKit's parallel, independent recents mechanism now would be a second, uncoordinated source of truth for "files this app has open," not a shortcut. `RecentFolderRoots` (`FileTree` package) already solves the identical shape one level up (folders instead of files): de-duped-by-`PhysicalFileIdentity`, MRU-ordered, capped at 10, security-scoped-bookmark-backed, single app-wide instance owned by `WindowCoordinator`, persisted via a `FileTreePreferenceStoring`-style protocol over plain `UserDefaults`. A new `RecentFileDocuments` type (new, small, `FileTree` or a new package location decided at 6c's own start) follows the identical shape for individual document URLs instead of folder roots, recorded on every successful document open (the existing open-file code path already funnels through one place per window/tab open, regardless of entry point — Open panel, folder browser, Quick Open itself, session restore is explicitly excluded from recording to avoid every relaunch re-promoting the same already-open documents to the top). Menu wiring mirrors `WorkspaceCommands.swift`'s existing "Open Recent Folder" `Menu`/`ForEach`/`Divider`/"Clear Menu" shape exactly, as "Open Recent File."

**The one genuinely new engine-side deliverable: the 100k-path performance budget §11/§17 already commit to but that has no test evidence today.** `WorkspaceFileIndexTests.swift`'s existing `limit:` test caps at 20 files, nowhere near the committed budget ("Quick Open top-result refresh, 100k paths, after indexing: < 30 ms — package benchmark, `WorkspaceFileIndex` directly"; "100k-path index build: off-main, visible progress/ready state — integration test + manual Release observation"). This slice adds both: a package benchmark constructing (or synthesizing without real disk I/O, decided at implementation time based on which is actually faster/more reliable in CI) a 100k-entry index and measuring `query`'s own wall-clock time against the 30 ms budget, and an integration test confirming `rebuild`'s `state` transitions through `.building` before `.ready` without blocking the calling actor/main thread — the same "first-run failure is evidence, not noise" discipline `RELEASE_HARDENING.md` §12 already establishes applies here as much as anywhere else: if 100k paths cannot meet 30 ms on real hardware, that is a finding to act on, not a threshold to quietly loosen.

**Sub-slice split, same "don't bundle differently-risky deliverables" reasoning as every prior multi-part slice:**
- **Slice 6a** — Engine-side foundation, no UI: `WorkspaceFileIndex` wired to `FileTreeModel.root`/`.rootAccessURL` via the new per-window `WindowController.workspaceFileIndex` property and its rebuild-on-root-change observation; the 100k-path performance benchmark and off-main-build integration test.
- **Slice 6b** — `QuickOpenPanel`/`QuickOpenModel` UI, Cmd-P menu/shortcut wiring, depending on 6a's own live, kept-warm index.
- **Slice 6c** — Recent Files (`RecentFileDocuments`, recording hook, "Open Recent File" menu) — the smallest, most self-contained addition, with no dependency on 6a/6b beyond both belonging to the same slice's overall goal.

Exact per-file organization and the precise adversarial corpus for each (§17's own line: 100k-path indexing/query performance budgets; symlink-loop safety — the latter already covered by Slice 1's existing tests, confirmed above, not repeated here) are fixed at the point each of 6a/6b/6c actually begins, matching this epic's own established convention. Slice 6a begins next.

#### F18 directory-read failure amendment — 9 November 2026

At `ffe692fe`, a recursive `DirectoryWalker` catches a failed directory
listing with `try?` and returns whatever paths it collected before the error.
The index then reports `.ready`, even if a child folder was unreadable, so
Quick Open and Folder Search can treat an incomplete search as complete. The
existing post-walk root check only catches a missing or unreadable *root*.

Keep the existing `TextSearch` ownership, detached walk, generation guard and
`.failed` presentation. A directory-listing error anywhere in the walk must
throw to `WorkspaceFileIndex.rebuild`; the current generation drops its old
paths and reports the existing localized folder-read failure. A superseded
generation may not publish that error. A genuinely empty readable root stays
`.ready(count: 0)`. The test seam injects a failing child enumeration after
a successful walk and proves that neither the partial result nor the previous
snapshot remains actionable. This changes no indexing budget, symlink policy
or per-keystroke disk behavior. An actual installed-app unavailable-folder
journey remains for the native acceptance pass.

#### F18 child-metadata failure amendment — 9 November 2026

Even after a directory listing throws on failure, a listed child's
`resourceValues(forKeys:)` read could still be caught with `try?` and silently
skipped. A scan with an earlier indexed file would then publish a partial
`.ready` snapshot. Propagate a failed primary child-metadata read through the
same current-generation `.failed` path, clearing old and partial results.
Inject the read failure after a previously indexed child to prove that the
index never presents an incomplete snapshot as complete. This preserves the
existing symlink, traversal-budget and disk-free query policies; it does not
establish installed-app workspace acceptance.

#### F18 symlink-target metadata failure amendment — 9 November 2026

A symlinked directory is intentionally traversed and indexed under its
lexical name. After the primary child metadata read is fixed, its separate
target metadata lookup can still throw and be suppressed, making that linked
subtree disappear while the index reports `.ready`. Use the same checked
metadata reader for that lookup and propagate a failure to the current
generation's `.failed` state. A deterministic test first proves the linked
file is indexed, then fails only the target lookup and proves the earlier
snapshot and partial paths are no longer actionable. Link traversal and loop
limits remain as before; installed-app behavior is still unverified.

#### F18 finite-index budget amendment — 9 November 2026

The recursive walk has explicit path and directory limits so a linked
workspace cannot expand without bound. Reaching either limit while more
eligible work remains previously returned a partial array that the index
published as `.ready`. The walker now throws at the first extra file or
directory; the current generation clears its old and partial snapshot and
reports `.failed`. Hitting the exact limit with no additional work still
produces a complete ready snapshot. Controlled path and directory cases
cover the failure and recovery after the workspace is reduced again. This
does not establish live mutation freshness or installed-app presentation.

#### F18 index-read atomicity amendment — 9 November 2026

Quick Open previously queried paths and then read index status in a second
actor call; Folder Search read status and then requested paths in a second
call. A rebuild could complete between those awaits, pairing paths and
status from different generations. The TextSearch index now returns state
and the selected paths in one actor-isolated read: ranked `querySnapshot`
for Quick Open and `allPathsSnapshot` for Folder Search. The latter still
inherits the prior complete snapshot during `.building`, as the existing
index contract permits, and returns the cleared paths with `.failed`.
Controlled rebuild interleavings test both old mixed-pair orders and one
captured search input. This only makes the initial read coherent; a row
already displayed when the index later changes still needs an action-time
validity gate under F18/F19, followed by the installed-app journey.

#### F18/F19 Quick Open action-time admission — 9 November 2026

Each published index path set now has a token that changes on success,
failure and clear. Quick Open keeps that token with its displayed rows. A
click or Return action reaches the index actor again and opens only when its
state is ready, the token still matches and the selected visible path remains
in that publication. Even a new successful rebuild containing the same
relative path rejects an old row. The panel also captures the window's root
request and lexical/access roots, checks them before and after the index
await, and supplies the same guard to the document-open path's later awaits.
This closes the source-level stale-row opening path without changing index
refresh policy, filesystem symlink handling or installed-app acceptance.

### 6.16 Folder search + safe replace (Slice 7) — design pass, baseline and contract, decided before implementation

**Baseline, verified against live master before writing this design pass:** `TextSearchEngine.matches(in:query:options:)` (Slice 5, `TextSearch` package) is already a complete, pure, synchronous single-buffer literal/regex matcher — its own doc comment already names its intended second consumer: "used directly by current-document find on a bounded buffer, and per-file by `WorkspaceSearchEngine` (folder search) inside its own cancellable off-main task." `WorkspaceFileIndex` (Slice 1/6) already holds every indexed `(relativePath, basename)` pair under a window's folder root, rebuilt on root change. `FileStore.write(_:to:encoding:bom:expectedRevision:)` (`FileCore`, pre-existing) already throws `.fileChangedDuringRead` when an on-disk file's revision no longer matches one captured at read time — the exact conditional-publication primitive invariant #6 requires Replace in Folder to reuse rather than inventing a second write path.
