None of `WorkspaceSearchEngine`, a replacement-plan type, or any folder-search UI exist yet — this is a genuinely new slice, not wiring an already-built engine the way Slice 6 was.

**UI placement — a new persistent sidebar section (`SidebarSection.search`), not a floating `NSPanel` like Quick Open/Command Palette/Go to Line:** `SidebarSection` (`Workspace` package) is already a plain `CaseIterable` enum (`.folder`, `.outline`) whose own `reconcile(_:)` already appends any case missing from a persisted order, so a third case is a clean, already-supported addition, not a new persistence mechanism. The floating-panel idiom every prior E22 panel uses (§6.14's Find bar is the one prior exception, for the same reason) fits a query that is asked once and dismissed, or navigated through briefly; folder search is asked once and then *browsed* — results grouped by file, expanded/collapsed, previewed, individually accepted or skipped during a replace — for a session that can run far longer than a Quick Open lookup. This is exactly the reasoning §6.14 already used to depart from the floating-panel precedent for current-document Find ("Find is used far more frequently and for far longer per invocation"), applied one level further: folder search is used for even longer, richer sessions than a single-document find bar. A sidebar section, alongside Folder and Outline, is also where every comparable editor (VS Code, Xcode) places the equivalent feature, not a floating utility panel.

**Engine-side types, new in `TextSearch` (`WorkspaceFileIndex`'s existing package), per §5.1's own already-committed ownership boundary:**
- `FolderSearchFilter`: include/exclude glob-style patterns plus a binary-file skip flag, distinct from `FileTree`'s own `FileTreeFilter` (a sidebar-listing concern — hidden files, supported-extensions-only, folders-first — not a search-scoping one). Defaults to excluding the same `WorkspaceFileIndex.defaultExcludedDirectoryNames` set, not a second, silently-divergent exclusion list.
- `WorkspaceSearchEngine`: an `actor` (mirroring `WorkspaceFileIndex`'s own concurrency shape) that, given a root, a `SearchQuery`/`SearchOptions` (reusing Slice 5's existing types verbatim — no second query-options type), and a `FolderSearchFilter`, streams `FolderSearchMatch` values (file URL + the file's own `[SearchMatch]` from `TextSearchEngine.matches`) to a caller-supplied callback, one file at a time, checking `Task.isCancelled` between files so a superseded or dismissed search stops promptly instead of running to completion in the background. Reads file bytes through `FileCore`'s existing decode path (the same one `FileStore.readSnapshot` uses), so a binary/non-decodable file is skipped, counted, and reported — never force-decoded or crashed on — and a symlink loop is guarded the same way `DirectoryWalker`'s own `visited` set already guards indexing (reusing that guard, not re-deriving a second one). Bounded accumulation (issue #112's own "Folder search streams first results and has bounded accumulation," §11) means the engine itself caps total *matches* returned (not just files visited), stopping and reporting explicit truncation once hit — never silently dropping the fact that more existed.
- `ReplacementPlan`/`ReplacementFileOutcome` (data only, per §5.1: `TextSearch` must not depend on `Workspace`'s document-model layer, so it never calls `FileStore` itself) — a plan is a per-file list of `(SearchMatch, replacementText)` pairs plus the file's `FileRevision` as read for the plan; an outcome is `.replaced` / `.skippedChangedSinceSearch` / `.skippedUnreadable` / `.failed(FileStoreError)`. The app-target orchestrator (below) is the only thing that ever calls `FileStore.write`.

**Safe replace — reusing `FileStore`'s conditional-publication path exactly, per invariant #6, never a second write mechanism:** for each file in a plan, the orchestrator re-reads the file's current revision immediately before writing (not the revision captured when the search first ran, which could be stale by the time the user confirms Replace All), and calls `FileStore.write(_:to:...:expectedRevision:)` with that fresh revision. A mismatch throws `.fileChangedDuringRead`, mapped to `.skippedChangedSinceSearch` in the outcome — exactly issue #112's own "that file is skipped and reported in the summary... never silently overwritten," reusing `WorkspaceModel+Saving`'s already-established `.fileChangedDuringRead`/`.conditionalPublicationRecoveryRequired` handling shape rather than inventing a second conflict-reporting convention. A currently-*open* document in this or another window is written the same way as any other match — `FileStore`'s own revision check is the single source of truth regardless of whether a tab happens to have it open; a follow-up decision at 7c's own start is whether an open, dirty tab whose buffer diverges from disk should be excluded from the plan entirely up front (folder replace should not fight an in-memory edit the user hasn't saved) rather than only caught at write time — leaning toward excluding it up front, confirmed when 7c begins.

**Cancellation and root-change safety, mirroring Quick Open's own generation-counter discipline (§6.15/6b):** the sidebar's search view model bumps a generation counter on every new query and on the folder root changing underneath it (the same `WindowController.setFileTreeRoot`-driven signal `closeQuickOpenIfOrigin` already reacts to), cancelling the in-flight `WorkspaceSearchEngine` task and discarding any late-arriving streamed results tagged with a superseded generation — the identical shape already proven correct (and hostile-review-tested) for Quick Open's own query races, not a new pattern invented here.

**Sub-slice split, same "don't bundle differently-risky deliverables" reasoning as every prior multi-part slice:**
- **Slice 7a** — Engine-side foundation, no UI: `FolderSearchFilter`, `WorkspaceSearchEngine` (streaming, cancellation, binary/symlink/permission-error safety, bounded accumulation with explicit truncation reporting), reusing Slice 5's `SearchQuery`/`SearchOptions`/`TextSearchEngine` verbatim. Adversarial coverage per issue #112's own list: empty query, zero-width regex, invalid regex, slow-regex cancellation, Unicode case folding, CRLF/mixed EOL, huge single lines, binary/non-decodable files, permission errors, symlink loops, root replacement during search, result caps.
- **Slice 7b** — UI: the new `SidebarSection.search` panel/view model, live streamed results grouped by file, click-to-open-and-select-match (reusing the existing document-open path, per §6.15's own "open via `WindowCoordinator.openDocument`" precedent rather than a third opening mechanism), depending on 7a's own engine.
- **Slice 7c** — Replace in Folder: preview UI, per-file accept/skip, the app-target orchestrator's `FileStore`-backed safe write, and the open-dirty-tab exclusion decision flagged above, resolved before this sub-slice's own implementation begins.

Exact per-file organization and the full adversarial corpus for each sub-slice are fixed at the point each of 7a/7b/7c actually begins, matching this epic's own established convention. Slice 7a begins next.

### 6.17 Encoding and line-ending controls (Slice 8) — design pass, baseline and contract, decided before implementation

**Baseline, verified against live master before writing this design pass:** `FileStore.readSnapshot` decoded strictly (UTF-8 with optional BOM, or UTF-16 with BOM) and refused anything else with `.encodingDetectionFailed`; `FileEncodingMetadata` already carried `(encoding, bom)` and collapsed inconsistent pairs; `write(_:to:encoding:bom:expectedRevision:)` already did conditional publication. But every *verification* read (baseline revision check, post-write verification, external-change probe, reconcile-on-save) went through the same strict decode, so a document opened with any non-automatic encoding could not be saved conditionally, probed, or reloaded without spuriously failing or marking itself dirty. Slice 8 therefore starts with a FileCore foundation, not with menus.

**Decode policy (8a):** `FileDecodingPolicy { .automatic, .explicit(String.Encoding) }`. Automatic stays strict and never guesses (invariant #5). `FileEncodingMetadata.isAutomaticallyDetectable` is derived — true only for (utf8, none), (utf8, utf8 BOM), (utf16LE, LE BOM), (utf16BE, BE BOM) — so no persisted flag is needed, and `decodingPolicy` is `.automatic` for those and `.explicit(encoding)` otherwise. Every consumer that re-reads a bound document (monitor probe, reconcile read, `ExternalFileController`) uses the document's own policy. Explicit decoding is **lossless-or-fail**: legacy encodings must re-encode to exactly the bytes read, else `.decodingFailed` with a diagnostic offset; explicit UTF-16 rejects the opposite-endianness BOM; odd-length UTF-16 payloads are rejected in both paths.

**Revision reads never decode (8a):** `FileStore.readRevision(from:)` reads bytes under the stable-metadata bracket and returns a `FileRevision` only. Baseline checks, conditional publication and post-write verification (size + sha256 of the bytes written) use it, so publication is independent of what encoding the file happens to be in.

**Representability (8a):** `FileStore.canRepresent(_:encoding:bom:)` is public; `encodedData` refuses a non-Unicode encoding whose scalar round trip differs from the input (Foundation silently composes NFD "e + U+0301" into a single Latin-1 byte, which would reopen as different scalars — a silent normalisation). A failed check happens before any file is touched.

**Catalog (8a):** `FileEncodingCatalog.curated` (UTF-8, UTF-16 LE/BE, Latin-1/2, Windows-1250/1251/1252/1253/1254, Mac Roman, Shift-JIS, EUC-JP, ISO-2022-JP, Big5, GB18030, EUC-KR, KOI8-R) and `.all` (curated first, then every `String.availableStringEncodings` by localized name); `isSupported(rawValue:)` gates metadata validation. Display names with BOM variants live in the app layer with String Catalog entries (8b).

**Sub-slice split:**
- **8a** — FileCore foundation, no UI (this design's baseline items above).
- **8b** — `FileDocument` reopen-with-encoding (explicit decode; failure leaves the document, its dirty state and its revision untouched) and save-with-encoding (verify representability first; metadata and status update only after successful publication; deliberate BOM policy), the two menu/palette commands, actionable status-bar encoding item, distinct error for unrepresentable text, String Catalog entries.
- **8c** — `LineEndingProfile` (LF/CRLF/CR/Mixed) detection preserved across normal open/save, Convert Line Endings commands as one undoable edit, actionable status-bar EOL item.

Carried to Slice 10 rather than widened here: search/replace engines still decode automatically, so `.skippedCannotRepresent` stays unreachable until they can read legacy encodings.

## 7. State and data flow

### 7.1 Selection: editor → outline/preview (extends existing single-range flow)

```
NSTextView (native, multi-range via selectedRanges)
  → EditorView.Coordinator.textViewDidChangeSelection
    → EditorTextSystem.selectionSet: EditorSelectionSet  (NEW — replaces raw selectedRange as the source of truth)
      → EditorTextSystem.selectedRange (COMPUTED, = selectionSet.primaryRange, kept for source compatibility with any code not yet selection-set-aware)
      → EditorView.onSelectionChange(selectionSet.primaryRange)  (UNCHANGED signature and call sites: OutlineController continues to follow only the primary selection, per the epic's explicit "Preview/outline tracking continues to follow only the primary selection")
```

`EditorTextSystem.selectedRange` is **not removed** — it becomes a computed property over `selectionSet.primaryRange` so every existing consumer (`EditorTextSystem+DocumentReplacement`, `TextFilterCoordinator`, session restore) keeps compiling and behaving identically for the single-selection case, which remains the overwhelmingly common one.

### 7.2 Multi-cursor typing (new)

```
User types a character with 3 active selections
  → EditorView.Coordinator.textView(_:shouldChangeTextIn:replacementString:)
    → guard !hasMarkedText/no marked-range intersection (existing IME gate, reused verbatim)
    → guard selectionSet.isMultiple else { return true /* fall through to native single-range handling, unchanged */ }
    → build one EditorEditTransaction: one TextReplacement per selection range, all with the same replacementString
    → EditorTextSystem.apply(transaction)  (one undo group, one publication — §6.3)
    → return false (suppress the native single-edit path; we already applied it)
```

**Architecture correction, found during Slice 3a's own implementation:** the diagram above, drawn before implementation began, assumed the interception point would be `EditorView.Coordinator`'s existing `NSTextViewDelegate` hook (`shouldChangeTextIn`, or its array-based sibling `shouldChangeTextInRanges:replacementStrings:`). Empirically, against a real mounted `NSTextView`, neither delegate method is ever invoked by ordinary typing/backspace regardless of how many `selectedRanges` are active — AppKit acts only on the primary selection for a plain keystroke and never fans a delegate callback out across every selection on its own. The real interception point that was found to actually fire is a set of `NSTextView` method overrides on `EditorTextView` itself: `insertText(_:replacementRange:)`, `deleteBackward(_:)`, `deleteForward(_:)`, and (found only once a real, pasteboard-backed test was written) a dedicated `paste(_:)` override — `NSTextView.paste(_:)` was found to perform its edit as two separate `insertText` calls (delete the selection, then insert the pasted text), and the first call's own multi-range delete collapses the selection to one caret before the second call runs, so `paste(_:)` needed its own override reading the pasteboard directly rather than relying on `insertText`'s interception to catch it. Each override calls a same-shaped `EditorTextSystem.applyMultiCursorInsert(_:)`/`applyMultiCursorDeleteSelection()` method (guarding IME/in-progress-edit state internally, exactly as the diagram's pseudocode describes) before falling through to `super` unchanged for the single-selection case. The resulting behavior matches this contract's intent; only the mechanism differs from what was planned before implementation started.

### 7.3 Fenced-code/front-matter gating (new; extends existing E10 dispatch)

```
Return/Tab/typed-character hook (EditorView.Coordinator, existing entry points)
  → existing: format check (document.format.id == "markdown")
  → NEW: FencedRegionClassifier.classify(text: NSString, atUTF16Offset: caretOffset) -&gt; .prose | .fencedCode | .frontMatter
       (bounded backward scan from caret to nearest preceding unmatched fence/`---` delimiter pair;
        never touches MarkdownEngine/SourceMap; matches E10's "local, synchronous, no full parse" rule)
  → if .fencedCode or .frontMatter: Markdown-specific list/blockquote/delimiter assists do NOT run;
     LanguageEditingProfile-driven general pairing/indent DOES run (using the fenced language's own
     profile where the fence carries a language tag, else `.plainText`)
  → if .prose: existing Markdown assist path, unchanged
```

## 8. Concurrency and cancellation

- `EditorLineIndex`, `EditorSelectionSet`, `EditorEditTransaction`, `LanguageEditingProfile`: plain `Sendable` value types, mutated only on the main actor inside `EditorTextSystem`'s existing edit call sites. No new actor.
- `WorkspaceFileIndex` (TextSearch): an `actor`, built/rebuilt via `Task.detached` off the main actor, exactly mirroring `FileTreeModel.contents(_:)`'s existing `Task.detached { try self.reader.contents(of: url) }.value` shape. A generation counter (mirroring `FileTreeModel.generation`) invalidates any in-flight build whose root has since changed.
- Current-document regex search: runs off-main for anything above a small buffer-size threshold (exact threshold decided in Slice 5, informed by the performance budget in §11), with a query-generation counter so a stale query's results are never published over a newer one's — the same "stale generation guard" pattern `FileTreeModel.reload`/`OutlineController` already use.
- Folder search: cancellable per-search-session `Task`, streams results incrementally (not one batch at completion), bounded accumulation (explicit per-file and total caps per the epic's required behaviour list).
- Replace-in-folder: search produces immutable per-file snapshots (already-read `FileSnapshot`s, per §2.1's `FileStore` guarantee) before any write begins; each write is independently revalidated at publication time via `expectedRevision`, exactly like a normal document save — no global lock across files, no assumption that the whole batch succeeds atomically.

## 9. Failure model

- **Invalid regex** (current-document or folder search): surfaced as a visible, localized inline diagnostic (`SearchQueryError.invalidRegex`) — never an empty-result state indistinguishable from "no matches."
- **Pathological/slow regex:** cancellable; a cancelled search leaves the document/results pane in its last-good state, never a partial/corrupt result set.
- **Multi-cursor edit where one range would produce invalid UTF-16 (e.g. splitting a surrogate pair):** the whole transaction fails closed (no partial application) — `EditorEditTransaction`'s Debug-fatal/Release-drop validation (§6.3) is the boundary; a Release build never applies a corrupt subset.
- **Reopen Using Encoding… fails to decode:** current document completely untouched (invariant #7); the error is surfaced, no metadata is updated.
- **Save With Encoding… would be lossy:** the write does not happen; disk and document metadata are both unchanged (mirrors `FileDocument.saveAs`'s existing lossless-check-before-write discipline, generalized to plain save).
- **Replace-in-folder target changed since search:** that file is skipped and reported in the summary, exactly like a normal save's `.fileChangedDuringRead`/`reconcileSaveConflict` handling — never silently overwritten, per invariant #6.
- **Symlink loop during workspace indexing:** the walker's own visited-physical-identity guard (§2.1, new — no existing precedent) terminates that branch; the index build completes with the rest of the tree, not a hang or crash.
- **`.tex`/`.latex` with no available highlighter:** falls back to plain-text-styled rendering with correct format identity (never Markdown, never a crash) — see §9.6's evidence-based decision.
- **Snippet body referencing `${clipboard}` with an empty/non-text clipboard:** expands to an empty string at that position, never a crash or a placeholder token leaking into the document.

## 10. Security and trust boundary

- Folder search/replace reads/writes only within an already-user-granted folder root (`FolderAccessScope`), using the same access grant the sidebar already holds — no new capability is requested, no telemetry, no document content ever leaves the process (unchanged product invariant, `RELEASE_HARDENING.md` §1.1).
- Snippets are plain, versioned JSON in Application Support, atomic writes (mirroring `WorkspaceSessionStore`'s existing atomic-write pattern) — no shell interpolation, no executable content, per the epic's explicit exclusion.
- `Reopen Using Encoding…`/`Save With Encoding…` operate only on already-open, already-permitted files; no new file-system capability.
- The `.tex`/`.latex` format addition does not enable TeX *compilation* (explicitly out of scope) — it is source-text editing only, so it introduces no new subprocess/execution trust boundary.

## 11. Resource and performance budgets

Restated from the epic issue, with the evidence layer each is measured at:

| Budget | Target | Evidence layer |
|---|---|---|
| Gutter/status caret update | &lt; 8 ms main-actor work | package unit benchmark (synthetic keystroke loop) |
| 100-caret edit, 1 MB document | &lt; 50 ms excluding highlighter debounce | package integration test, mirroring `EditorPerformanceTests`' existing shape |
| Literal search, 10 MB text | &lt; 200 ms off-main | package benchmark, `TextSearchEngine` directly |
| Quick Open top-result refresh, 100k paths, after indexing | &lt; 30 ms | package benchmark, `WorkspaceFileIndex` directly |
| 100k-path index build | off-main, visible progress/ready state | integration test + manual Release observation |
| Folder search | streams first results, bounded accumulation | integration test with a large synthetic tree |
| Gutter draw | visible line numbers only, never whole-document layout | extends `EditorPerformanceTests.open10MBLazy`'s fragment-count assertion pattern to the gutter's own draw path |

**E05 inherited debt (§2.1, binding on this epic, not merely inherited-and-restated):** the existing `HighlightPerformanceTests` must be corrected to actually run and assert against Release-configuration numbers, not restate an unverified claim. This requires either (a) adding a Release-configuration package-test CI step (mirroring the "Build app + CLI (Release)" step's existing precedent, extended to `swift test -c release` or equivalent for the affected suite), or (b) if a Release package-test run proves infeasible in CI for a concrete, documented reason, recording a real local-Mac measurement with the exact command used, per `EPIC_STANDARD.md` §3.11's "unit/package benchmark vs. complete app-path Release measurement vs. manual observation" distinction — but the *current* state (an 8-second Debug ceiling standing in for an unenforced 50 ms/500 ms/8 ms claim) is not an acceptable release-evidence disposition and must change materially, not just be re-documented.

## 12. Accessibility and localisation impact

- All new UI (gutter, status bar, Quick Open panel, folder-search results, encoding/EOL menus, snippet editor) ships with accessibility labels/identifiers from the same slice that introduces the UI, per `EPIC_STANDARD.md` §3.12 ("feature epics own their basic accessibility... not a dumping ground for E15/E16").
- New user-facing strings land in the existing `Localizable.xcstrings` app-target catalog in the same slice as the UI that introduces them (per the epic's own §"Settings/localisation" note) — this repo's established String Catalog pattern (see `planning/epic-16-implementation.md`) is reused unchanged; no new catalog infrastructure is needed since the app target's catalog and resource-bundle wiring already exist.
- Because E16's *final* string freeze is deferred until after E22/E23/#115 (per the 2026-09-21 sequencing), E22 does not need to request a localisation re-verification pass mid-epic — but must not leave any new string un-cataloged, since the eventual post-#115 delta-extraction pass (§13) will audit for exactly that.
- Current-line highlighting (§2.1/§2.2) must not be the *only* indicator of the active line (existing acceptance criterion: "distinguishable without relying on color alone") — the implementation must also consider a stronger gutter-number weight/style for the current line, decided concretely in Slice 2.

## 13. Export and interoperability

- Line-number gutter, status bar, invisibles, and multi-cursor are editor-only presentation; none of them affect the durable document text or any export path.
- EOL/encoding conversions are explicit user actions that change the *saved bytes* exactly once, at the moment of the action — they do not retroactively alter Export, which already reads the live in-memory text (unaffected by these changes) and writes via its own, unrelated `ExportService` path.
- `.tex`/`.latex` files get no preview/export capability beyond what any other syntax-highlighted, non-Markdown, non-previewable source format already has (plaintext-style editing) — this is a deliberate exclusion (§ "Explicitly out of scope" in the issue: no TeX compilation), not an oversight.
- Snippets and general-format editing profiles have no export-time representation — they are editor-only conveniences.

| Critical UI journeys (multi-cursor, Quick Open, folder search, Go to Line) | genuinely executed XCUITest evidence where a runnable macOS GUI environment is available this session; otherwise honestly disclosed as unverified, matching this session's established practice for `MacDown2UITests` throughout E15/E16, never inferred as passed |
| Existing green suites remain green | full package + app-target regression run before every slice merges |

## 15. Adversarial corpus

Restated and made concrete from the epic issue's own "Adversarial requirements" section — this document does not weaken any of it:

- **Multi-cursor:** overlapping/adjacent selections, duplicate cursors at the same offset, emoji/CJK/combining-character boundaries, CRLF boundaries, first/last line, paste, undo/redo, interaction with auto-pair/comment/indent, an external full-document replacement arriving mid-multi-cursor-session, IME marked text, 100+ simultaneous cursors.
- **Search:** empty query, zero-width regex match, invalid regex, a deliberately slow/catastrophic-backtracking regex (cancellation must actually free the main actor), Unicode case folding, CRLF/mixed-EOL documents, a single very long line (no line-based assumption may break), binary/non-decodable files encountered during folder search, permission-denied files, symlink loops, the folder root changing mid-search, a target file mutated between search and replace, and result-cap truncation.
- **Encoding/EOL:** UTF-8 with/without BOM, UTF-16 LE/BE, Windows-1252, MacRoman, a representative CJK legacy encoding Foundation supports, an unrepresentable-on-save character, malformed bytes, a BOM that contradicts the selected encoding, mixed EOL within one file, no-final-newline fidelity, and an external edit landing concurrently with an encoding conversion.
- **Gutter/status/invisibles:** wrapped lines, a very long single line, a 10 MB file, empty document, first/last line, theme switch, light/dark/increased-contrast, multiple selections spanning many lines, CJK/emoji column counting, hidden/shown toggling, and confirmation that invisibles never trigger the legacy TextKit 1 fallback path.
- **Fenced-code/front-matter (new, from §2.1's E10 follow-up):** caret moving into/out of a fence via arrow keys vs. click vs. programmatic jump, a pasted fence, a blockquote-nested fence, a list-nested fence, front matter followed immediately by a fence, CRLF-delimited fences, and a very large document (the classifier's bounded backward scan must stay bounded, not degrade to a document-length scan).

## 17. Implementation slices

Per the owner's explicit technical-dependency-order instruction, slices are ordered foundations-first rather than strictly following the epic issue's own scope-area numbering (which interleaves foundation and UI within each scope area). Each slice is one or more independently reviewable PRs; a slice does not merge until its own tests/lint/build evidence is green and a hostile review of that slice's diff has run and its findings are resolved.

### Slice 1 — Pure foundations (no UI, no AppKit wiring)

**Goal:** land `EditorLineIndex`, `EditorSelectionSet`, `EditorEditTransaction`/`TextReplacement`, the `TextSearch` package skeleton (`SearchQuery`/`SearchOptions`/`SearchMatch`/`TextSearchEngine` + an empty `WorkspaceFileIndex`/`WorkspaceSearchEngine` shape), fuzzy-scoring foundations, and `LanguageEditingProfile` (data type only, not yet consumed) as fully tested, unintegrated types.

**Dependencies:** none beyond current master.
**Ownership:** `EditorCore` + new `TextSearch` package only. No changes to `DocumentEditorSplitView` or any app-target file. `EditorView`/`EditorTextSystem` themselves are otherwise untouched, with one narrow, already-reconciled exception: `EditorTextSystem.isApplyingMultiRangeTransaction` and the corresponding one-line guard addition to `EditorView.Coordinator.textDidChange`, both required for `EditorEditTransaction.apply(_:)` — one of this slice's own stated deliverables — to publish its SwiftUI binding once per transaction instead of once per range. This was discovered while implementing the slice, is documented in full in §6.3, and does not widen the slice into Slice 2's UI-integration territory (no gutter/status-bar/invisibles wiring landed here) — it is the minimum change needed for a Slice 1 deliverable to be correct at all.
**Tests:** full unit coverage per §14's first six rows, run as pure package tests (`swift test`) — no app-target/XCUITest dependency for this slice.
**Verification:** `swift build && swift test --no-parallel` for `MacDownKit`; `swiftformat --lint`/`swiftlint --strict`.
**Stop condition:** if any of these types cannot be built without touching `EditorTextSystem`'s existing public API beyond the one exception above, in a way not anticipated by §6, stop and reconcile this document before proceeding — do not silently widen scope into Slice 2's territory.

### Slice 2 — Line index + editor chrome integration

**Goal:** wire `EditorLineIndex` into `EditorTextSystem`'s edit path; ship the line-number gutter, status bar, Go to Line/Column, and real TextKit-2-safe invisibles (making `showsInvisibles`/`EditorChrome.invisibles` genuinely consumed).
**Dependencies:** Slice 1.
**Tests:** the gutter/status/invisibles adversarial corpus (§15); the viewport-laziness performance budget (§11) as an automated assertion.

**Split into two sequential PRs, decided at the point this slice began** (per `EPIC_STANDARD.md`'s "detailed contract fixed when the slice starts" convention, same as §6.4/§6.5): a fresh baseline read of `EditorTextSystem`, `EditorView`, and the (nonexistent) gutter/status-bar/Go-to-Line code (recorded in §6.6 below) showed the line-index wiring alone has real correctness subtlety — it must update once per atomic edit even inside an `EditorEditTransaction`'s N-range loop (which `EditorView.Coordinator.textDidChange`'s existing multi-range publication suppression would otherwise hide N−1 of), it must handle E10 assist's edit-interception (a vetoed outer edit whose nested internal edit is the one that actually lands), and it must do all of this without a full-document string copy per keystroke — while the remaining three deliverables (status bar, Go to Line, invisibles) are comparatively independent, self-contained UI additions. Bundling five non-trivial, differently-risky changes into one PR would repeat exactly the "monolithic change" failure mode this epic's own owner directive warns against at the epic level, just one level down. So:
- **Slice 2a** — `EditorLineIndex` wiring (§6.6) + the line-number gutter, the one deliverable that both consumes the wiring directly and gives it a visible, testable surface end-to-end.
- **Slice 2b** — Go to Line/Column and a status bar scoped to fields with no forward dependency on later slices (§6.7), once 2a is merged and proven.
- **Slice 2c** — TextKit-2-safe invisibles rendering (§6.7), split out from 2b for the same "don't bundle differently-risky changes" reason 2 was split into 2a/2b: invisibles is novel, character-glyph-level AppKit drawing with no existing precedent in this codebase, unlike 2b's two precedent-following, low-risk deliverables.

### Slice 3 — Selection foundation and multi-cursor

**Goal:** wire `EditorSelectionSet` as `EditorTextSystem`'s selection source of truth (§7.1); implement Option-click add/remove, add-cursor-above/below, rectangular selection, Escape-collapse, and route typing/paste/delete/indent through `EditorEditTransaction`. Resolve Cmd-D (occurrence selection vs. Folder Duplicate) via the `commandStateRevision`/first-responder pattern (§2.1).
**Dependencies:** Slice 1.
**Tests:** the full multi-cursor adversarial corpus (§15); IME safety tests; the 100-caret/1MB performance budget.

**Split into three sequential PRs, decided at the point this slice began** (same convention as §17's Slice 2 2a/2b/2c split): a fresh baseline read (recorded in §6.9) found `EditorSelectionSet`/`EditorEditTransaction` fully implemented but wholly unintegrated, `EditorTextSystem` still singular-selection-only, no precedent anywhere in this codebase for any of Slice 3's native-interaction deliverables, and a live (not just theoretical) Cmd-D/Folder-Duplicate conflict. Bundling the foundational data-model change, the largest precedent-free AppKit event-handling surface, and the self-contained occurrence-selection feature into one PR would repeat the same "monolithic change" failure mode Slice 2's split addressed:
- **Slice 3a** — Foundation: `selectionSet`/computed `selectedRange`, selection-set-aware selection-change handling, Escape-collapse, multi-selection typing/delete/paste via `EditorEditTransaction` (§6.9). **Landed** (PR #128) — see the Changelog and §6.9's own architecture-correction note: `NSTextView.selectedRanges` cannot hold more than one simultaneous bare caret via any known API, which blocks Slice 3b below from starting at its original one-line scope.
- **Slice 3b** — True multi-caret editing, requiring the custom, AppKit-independent caret tracking/rendering §6.9's architecture-correction note and §6.10's design pass describe — **not** a thin layer over `selectionSet`/`selectedRanges` the way 3a was. Split into sequential PRs once its real shape was known (§6.10): **3b-i** (custom multi-caret rendering foundation) — **landed** (PR #129), see the Changelog and §6.9 for the real cache-invalidation bug found and fixed along the way; **3b-ii** (Option-click add/remove, mouse-driven and self-contained within `EditorCore`) — **landed** (PR #131); **3b-ii-b** (Add/Remove Cursor Above/Below, split out from 3b-ii once that slice began — keyboard-driven, needs app-target menu/shortcut wiring, a different layer/risk profile) — **landed** (PR #132); **3b-iii** (synchronized plain-arrow-key caret movement, explicitly scoped to just the four unmodified arrow keys for its first cut) — **landed** (PR #133), see the Changelog for the cross-slice anchor-consistency bug found in already-merged 3b-ii-b code and fixed as part of this slice; **3b-iv** (rectangular/Option-drag column selection, independent of 3b-ii/ii-b/iii once 3b-i exists) — **landed** (PR #135, originally opened as #134 — see the Changelog's incident note), completing all of Slice 3b.
- **Slice 3c** — Occurrence selection: Select Next/All Occurrence, plus the `keyFolderSelection`/`canPerformOccurrenceSelection` first-responder fix for Cmd-D (§6.9), sequenced last — **landed** (PR #138), completing all of Slice 3.

### Slice 4 — Core text commands + language profiles

**Goal:** the ten built-in transforms (Duplicate/Delete/Move/Join/Sort/Dedupe/Trim/case/indent/comment-toggle); generalize the pairing/indent engine via `LanguageEditingProfile` to every format; implement the fenced-code/front-matter classifier and gate Markdown-specific assists behind it (closing the E10 inherited debt item).
**Dependencies:** Slices 1-3 (needs `EditorEditTransaction` for multi-cursor-aware transforms).
**Tests:** fenced-code/front-matter adversarial corpus; format-neutral pairing/indent tests across every registered `FileFormat`.

**Split into three sequential PRs, decided at the point this slice began** (§6.11): a fresh baseline read found `LanguageEditingProfile` real but wholly unconsumed, none of the ten transforms existing anywhere, and the fenced-code/front-matter classifier needing to be built from scratch with no reusable AST-based prior art (disqualified by E10's own "no full Markdown parse on the hot path" rule). Bundling the foundational profile/gate generalization, the novel classifier, and the ten transforms into one PR would repeat the same "monolithic change" failure mode every prior multi-part slice in this epic has split around:
- **Slice 4a** — `LanguageEditingProfileRegistry` + generalizing the editing-assist gate so every registered format gets a real, profile-driven pairing/indent engine (§6.11). Foundational — 4b and 4c's comment-toggle both depend on real per-format profiles existing. **Landed** (PR #140), see the Changelog for the Return-key regression a hostile review found and fixed for Markdown users with list/quote continuation disabled.
- **Slice 4b** — `FencedRegionClassifier` + gating Markdown-specific assists behind it, closing the E10 inherited-debt item (§2.3, §6.11). Depends on 4a for the correct fallback behavior inside a fence. **Landed** (PR #141), see the Changelog for the EOF/no-trailing-newline P1 a hostile review found and fixed, plus the disclosed fence-character/length simplifications.
- **Slice 4c** — The ten built-in transforms via `EditorEditTransaction` (§6.11). Nine depend only on already-complete Slice 1/3 machinery; comment-toggle also depends on 4a and 4b. Split into sequential PRs once its real shape was known (§6.13): **4c-i** (Duplicate/Delete/Move Line Up/Move Line Down/Join Lines — the multi-selection line-block merge rule shared by all four) — **Landed** (PR #142), see the Changelog for the CRLF/bare-CR line-ending corruption P1 a hostile review found and fixed in Move Line Up/Down; **4c-ii** (Sort/Dedupe/Trim Trailing Whitespace/Convert Case/Increase-Decrease Indent — a simpler, lower-risk "rewrite each selection's own block in place" shape) — **Landed** (PR #143), see the Changelog for the P1 a hostile review found and fixed in Increase/Decrease Indent (a bare caret left the whole line selected instead of a collapsed caret), plus a separate, pre-existing bug in shipped Tab/Shift-Tab discovered along the way and flagged for its own follow-up rather than fixed inline here; **4c-iii** (Toggle Comment, depending on 4a/4b, smallest and most self-contained) — **Landed** (PR #144), see the Changelog for the P1 (a caret inside a line's own leading whitespace teleported past the comment prefix) and two P2s a hostile review found and fixed, across three total review rounds. **All of Slice 4c is now landed — every one of the ten built-in transforms is implemented.**

### Slice 5 — TextSearch + current-document search

**Goal:** real regex/literal engine behind current-document Find/Replace UI, replacing reliance on stock `NSTextFinder` for anything beyond its existing menu-level trigger wrapper (`EditorFind.swift` — which continues to exist for the plain show/hide/toggle-interface calls, but the actual match/replace logic becomes MacDown 2's own per the epic's explicit "stock NSTextFinder is enough" reversal).
**Dependencies:** Slice 1 (`TextSearch` types); Slice 3 (select-matches-into-multi-selection needs `EditorSelectionSet`).
**Tests:** search adversarial corpus; literal-search-in-10MB performance budget.
**Design pass (§6.14):** an inline docked Find bar (deliberately departing from Go to Line/Command Palette's own floating-`NSPanel` precedent — Find is used far more frequently and for far longer per invocation), a new `EditorFindModel` holding the current-match/navigation state `TextSearchEngine` itself deliberately doesn't, and viewport-bounded match highlighting following the invisibles drawing pattern (not secondary carets', since match count is unbounded). Split into sequential PRs once its real shape was known: **5a** (the Find bar itself: live matching, Find Next/Previous, highlighting) — **Landed** (PR #146), see the Changelog for the P1 (regex search ran synchronously on `@MainActor`, freezing the whole app on a catastrophic-backtracking query) a hostile review found and fixed via an off-main `Task.detached` plus a query-generation counter, and two lesser findings (a stale-`text`-binding gap, an O(lines × matches) highlight-redraw cost) fixed alongside it; **5b** (Replace/Replace All via `EditorEditTransaction`) — **Landed** (PR #149), see the Changelog for the P1 (Replace/Replace All not gated on `isSearching`, allowing a transaction built from stale match positions) a hostile review found and fixed; **5c** (Select All Matches via `EditorSelectionSet(ranges:primaryIndex:)`, search-selection-scope via `updateMatches(in:selection:)`) — **Landed** (PR #152), see the Changelog for the real bug (whole-word matching broken at the selection's own boundary, from slicing the text before searching it) a hostile review found and fixed. This closes Slice 5 in full.

### Slice 6 — Workspace index + Quick Open + recents

**Goal:** wire the already-implemented `WorkspaceFileIndex`/`FuzzyPathScore` (Slice 1) to `FileTreeModel.root` and real UI — Cmd-P Quick Open panel (mirroring `CommandPalettePanel`'s origin-window-capture idiom), Open Recent File menu — plus the one genuinely missing engine-side deliverable, the 100k-path performance budget. Corrected from this line's original "`WorkspaceFileIndex` real implementation" framing, which predates the discovery that Slice 1 already fully implemented it; see §6.15.
**Dependencies:** Slice 1 (`TextSearch` fuzzy scoring, `WorkspaceFileIndex` — both already implemented, not started fresh here); `RecentFolderRoots` (`FileTree` package) as the direct architectural template for the new Recent Files store.
**Tests:** 100k-path indexing/query performance budgets (genuinely missing today); symlink-loop safety (already covered by Slice 1's own tests).
**Design pass (§6.15):** one `WorkspaceFileIndex` instance per window (mirroring `FileTreeModel`'s own per-window scope), kept warm via rebuild-on-root-change rather than built lazily on first Quick Open; `QuickOpenPanel`/`QuickOpenModel` mirror `CommandPalettePanel`'s exact ownership/origin-capture/activation-dance idiom (app-wide-scoped, not Go to Line's single-document scope), guarded on `fileTreeModel.root == nil`; a new `RecentFileDocuments` type mirrors `RecentFolderRoots`' bookmark/MRU/cap/staleness-refresh shape for individual documents, not AppKit's own unused `NSDocumentController` recents mechanism. Split into **6a** (engine-side foundation: index wired to `FileTreeModel.root`, 100k-path performance tests) — **Landed** (PR #156), see the Changelog for the real bug (the 100k-path query budget missed by over 10x on a naive implementation, fixed with five genuine algorithmic optimizations, not a relaxed threshold) two independent hostile reviews found and fixed across two rounds; **6b** (`QuickOpenPanel`/`QuickOpenModel` UI + Cmd-P wiring) — **Landed** (PR #157), see the Changelog for the real silent-wrong-file-open bug (Quick Open resolving a stale result against a folder root that had changed underneath it) a hostile review found and fixed across two rounds; **6c** (Recent Files store + menu, plus issue #112's "Recent-file history feeds Quick Open ranking") — **Landed** (PR #161), see the Changelog for the real bug (an `openRecentFile(_:)` symlinked-parent-directory URL mismatch) a hostile review found and fixed. **All of Slice 6 is now closed.**

### Slice 7 — Folder search + safe replace

**Goal:** folder-wide search UI, streaming results, filters, and Replace in Folder with the full preview/revalidate/skip-on-conflict safety model (§9, §4 invariant #6).
**Dependencies:** Slice 6 (`WorkspaceFileIndex`); Slice 5 (`TextSearchEngine` reuse).
**Tests:** replace-in-folder adversarial corpus; conflict/skip evidence reusing `FileStore`'s existing conditional-publication test patterns.
**Design pass (§6.16):** a new `SidebarSection.search` sidebar panel (not a floating panel like Quick Open/Command Palette — folder search is browsed for far longer per session, the same reasoning §6.14 used for the current-document Find bar); new `TextSearch`-package types `FolderSearchFilter`/`WorkspaceSearchEngine`/`ReplacementPlan`/`ReplacementFileOutcome`, reusing Slice 5's `SearchQuery`/`SearchOptions`/`TextSearchEngine` verbatim; safe replace re-reads each file's revision immediately before writing and reuses `FileStore`'s existing `expectedRevision`/`.fileChangedDuringRead` conditional-publication path exactly, never a second write mechanism. Split into **7a** (engine: streaming, cancellation, binary/symlink/permission safety, bounded accumulation), **7b** (sidebar search UI, depending on 7a), **7c** (Replace in Folder preview + safe write, including a resolved decision on excluding open-dirty-tab files from a plan up front).

**7a** (engine-side foundation: `WorkspaceSearchEngine`, `FolderSearchFilter` with glob-style include/exclude and a hidden-file toggle, `GlobPattern`, `TextSearchEngine`'s `matchLimit` early-stop, `WorkspaceFileIndex.allPaths(includeHidden:)`) — **Landed** (PR #167, squash-merged to `master` at `56c699ad`). A follow-up review pass closed four findings before merge: brought `FolderSearchFilter` to the decided glob-based contract (it originally shipped with only an extension list), made the hidden-file toggle real (`WorkspaceFileIndex` now tags hidden entries instead of dropping them, so folder search can opt in without changing Quick Open), closed a cancellation gap (only checked once per file before it was read; now also rechecked after matching and after publishing, so a superseded search can never emit one late result), and made result bounding real at the matcher level instead of truncating an already-fully-materialized array. 69/69 tests passing across 6 suites, `swiftlint lint --strict` clean, full `xcodebuild` gate green. 7b begins next.

**7b** (persistent `SidebarSection.search` panel: `FolderSearchModel` per window, `FolderSearchView`, result-open through `WindowCoordinator.openDocument` + `EditorTextSystem.revealSelection`) — **Landed** (PR #172, merged to `master` at `0b5bfe11`). Debounced (150 ms), generation-guarded, cancellable search streaming `WorkspaceSearchEngine` matches into a per-file results list with match counts; `setRoot` re-searches immediately and clearing the root cancels the in-flight task. An independent review closed four findings before merge: result-open resolved against the *live* tree root instead of the root the results were computed for (now uses the model's captured `root`), adding `SidebarSection.search` broke three MacDownKit test files that pin section order (updated; lesson: re-run the whole package suite after touching a shared enum), no debounce on a disk-backed search, and a misleading retry-loop comment. Two wall-clock flakes in the new tests were root-caused (waiting on `results.count` instead of `outcome`; a fixed sleep) and fixed. **Deliberately carried forward:** per-match line/column/excerpt rows (results are per-file with counts), glob and hidden-file toggles in the UI (the filter already supports both), and reveal into an already-open *non-active background tab* (root cause is `openDocument` not activating an existing tab, shared with Quick Open — issue #173). CI green (`lint`, `build-and-test`, `inventory`). Local note: whole-process `MacDown2Tests` runs on the dev machine stall the main actor and time out unrelated suites that pass in isolation and on CI; CI is the authority for the full suite. 7c begins next.

**7c** (Replace in Folder: `ReplacementPlan`/`ReplacementFileOutcome`/`WorkspaceReplaceEngine` in `TextSearch`, replace state machine in `FolderSearchModel`, Search sidebar Replace UI) — **Landed** (PR #175, merged to `master` at `7d5f2be1`). Per file the engine refuses symlinks (the file or any path component), re-reads and requires the plan revision's sha256+size to match, then publishes through `FileStore.write` with the *fresh* revision as `expectedRevision` (encoding/BOM/EOL preserved, literal replacement with no capture expansion, identical result not rewritten); cancellation leaves the remainder `.notAttempted`. Open-with-unsaved-edits files are excluded up front and re-checked per file right before rewrite; Replace is disabled unless the search outcome is `.completed`, and the sidebar reports files the search could not read. UI: per-file include/exclude, before/after previews (first 5 line regions per file), confirmation dialog stating the disk rewrite is not undoable, progress/Stop, summary with skipped reasons. **Deviations from §6.16:** the executor lives in the `TextSearch` package (testable without the app target); staleness is decided by re-hashing a fresh read rather than trusting the stored revision alone; symlinks are skipped rather than followed; disk rewrites are not undoable (open *clean* documents reload through the existing E18 external-change path). An independent review closed four findings before merge: canonical-equivalence no-op check (`String ==` treated NFC→NFD replacement as unchanged; now compares scalars, regression red-then-green), a write that had already landed being reported as "not modified" when `FileStore`'s post-publication read-back threw, silent partial replace over undecodable files (now surfaced), and an unbounded skipped-file list. **Carried forward / recorded:** `.skippedCannotRepresent` for unrepresentable encodings is unreachable until Slice 8 widens decoding (add coverage there); `FileStore` drops POSIX mode/xattrs/ACLs on every save (issue #174, Slice 10, release-blocking); replacement text is not EOL-normalized for CRLF files (pasted newlines); replace summary is not tied to a root if the folder changes mid-run; failure messages and plural forms are not localized (catalog gap shared with 7b); 7b's per-match rows, glob/hidden UI toggles and background-tab reveal (#173) still open. CI green (`lint`, `build-and-test`, `inventory`). Slice 7 is complete; Slice 8 (Encoding/EOL) begins next.

### Slice 8 — Encoding/EOL controls

**Goal:** Reopen Using Encoding…, Save With Encoding…, broadened `FileEncodingMetadata` validation, `LineEndingProfile`, EOL conversion commands.
**Dependencies:** none from this epic (can proceed in parallel with Slices 2-7 once Slice 1 lands, since it touches `FileCore`/app-target menus, not `EditorCore`'s selection/edit machinery) — sequenced here per the owner's stated preference order, not a hard technical dependency.
**Tests:** full encoding/EOL adversarial corpus; `TextRoundTripFidelityTests` regression.

**Status:** in progress — design §6.17.
- **8a** (FileCore foundation, no UI): implemented — `FileDecodingPolicy`, derived `isAutomaticallyDetectable`/`decodingPolicy`, lossless-or-fail explicit decode, decode-free `readRevision` used by all conditional-publication/verification reads, `canRepresent` + scalar round-trip refusal, `FileEncodingCatalog`, decoding policy plumbed through `DocumentFileMonitor`/probe/reconcile/`ExternalFileController`. 18 `ExplicitEncoding` tests.
- **8b** — implemented: `FileDocument.saving(encodingOverride:)` (metadata adopted only after a successful write), `WorkspaceModel.saveWithoutDestinationPrompt(encoding:)` reusing the normal save/reconcile/retry machinery, `WorkspaceError.textNotRepresentable` + banner, `ExternalFileController.reopenWithEncoding` (explicit lossless decode, dirty-text confirmation, post-confirmation re-check, refused while a save is in flight), File-menu Reopen/Save with Encoding submenus and an actionable status-bar encoding item. Independent review closed: clean-document metadata race silently skipping the encoding retry, reopen racing an in-flight save, tab/epoch change during the recovery drain, `.reopened` reported when recovery cleanup blocked the reload. **Carried forward / known limitations:** (a) a plain Cmd-S issued while an encoding save is still in flight is queued behind it with the *old* encoding and reverts the conversion (latest save wins; file and document remain consistent); (b) edits typed during an encoding save that are unrepresentable in the new encoding surface as `textNotRepresentable` on the next save (nothing lost); (c) after Save with Encoding the monitor may probe once with the old decoding policy before `synchronize` updates it (same shape as Save As); (d) Save As encoding accessory / untitled-document encoding and command-palette entries → Slice 9; (e) 8b strings are English-only in the String Catalog (with the 7b/7c gap).
- **8c** — implemented: `LineEnding`/`LineEndingProfile` (FileCore; classifies none/LF/CRLF/CR/Mixed from UTF-8 bytes, CRLF is one terminator, U+2028/2029/NEL are content), `EditorTextTransforms.convertLineEndingsTransaction` (one replacement from the first to the last differing terminator, so one undo step and cheap on large files; selection remapped in O(log n) through terminator length changes; an offset between a CRLF's CR and LF lands after the replacement terminator), `EditorTextSystem.convertLineEndings(to:)`, a Lines ▸ Convert Line Endings submenu and an actionable status-bar EOL item (shows Mixed/None honestly; conversion never implicit). The status-bar item converts its own pane's text system directly (never via the key window), and both entry points focus the editor only after a conversion actually happened. Independent review found no scan/transform defects; its robustness, performance and test-gap findings are closed. **Carried forward → Slice 10:** typing Return, paste and editing-assist inserts in a CRLF/CR document insert `\n` and make the file Mixed (the status bar flags it and offers conversion, but new-line insertion should follow the document's dominant EOL); the status-bar profile is recomputed O(n) per render like the existing character count; menu-bar Convert Line Endings items are enabled even when nothing would change and show no checkmark; `keyLineEndingTextSystem` duplicates `keyTextTransformController`'s lookup; EOL strings are English-only (catalog gap).

### Slice 9 — Syntax override + snippets + `.tex`/`.latex` + `MarkdownParseOptions`/#117 disposition

**Goal:** per-document Syntax Mode override; native snippet system; `.tex`/`.latex` format registration per the evidence-based §9.6 decision; remove/recast the five inert `MarkdownParseOptions` fields; close #117's command-palette consistency item.
**Dependencies:** Slice 4 (`LanguageEditingProfile` needed for TeX mechanics and snippet syntax-scoping).
**Tests:** snippet expansion corpus; `FormatRegistryConsistencyTests` extension for `.tex`/`.latex`; the new palette-consistency test.

**Status (Slice 9 is split into sub-slices, one concern per PR):**
- **9a** — implemented: `MarkdownParseOptions` recast to its single real flag, `blockDirectives` (tables/task lists/strikethrough are always on in swift-markdown 0.8.0; GFM extended autolinks and footnotes have no engine support, so the four inert fields were removed rather than left as switches that do nothing). The Markdown settings pane footer now states what always parses and what is unsupported (English text replaced; fr/ja/pl translated). Test changes: options defaults/equality, and removal of the inert-fields wiring test.
- **9b** — implemented: `CommandDescriptor.catalog` (every literal-titled menu item in the `Commands` sources, each either bound to a palette id or excluded with a named reason), `CommandRegistryConsistency.validate(...) -> Report`, and `CommandRegistryConsistencyTests`, which scans `WorkspaceCommands*.swift`/`TextFilterCommands.swift` for their literal `Button`/`Menu`/`CommandMenu` titles so a menu item added, renamed or removed without touching the catalog fails, plus drift-injection tests for each failure kind. Exclusions are typed (`menuContainer`, `dynamicItems`, `debugOnly`, `paletteItself`, `notYetWired`). **Carried forward → Slice 10:** the `notYetWired` commands (editing/line transforms/encoding/EOL/find/go-to-line/tabs/export) are palette-eligible in principle but need explicit origin-window capture to be added; the scan cannot see titles supplied through label closures (Layout choices) or variables (theme and text-filter names), which sit inside catalogued container menus.
- **9c** — implemented: `tex` `FileFormat` (extensions `tex`, `latex`; source-only, no preview capability, so it can never enter the Markdown parse/preview path), `FormatManifest`/`project.yml` `public.source-code` Alternate entries, and a `tex` `LanguageEditingProfile` (`%` line comment, no block comment, no auto-indent triggers). **§9.6 decision — plain-text-styled fallback, no local highlighter for 1.0:** the only maintained tree-sitter grammar (`latex-lsp/tree-sitter-latex`, MIT) does not check in its generated `src/parser.c` (upstream deliberately gitignores it), so it cannot be consumed as a normal SwiftPM dependency the way the other grammars are, and there is no published "with-generated-files" tag. Vendoring a generated parser would add an unreviewed multi-megabyte artefact plus a build-time generation step; that is disproportionate to a lightweight-source-editing requirement. `highlightLanguageID` is therefore `nil` (the registry-consistency test treats `nil` as always acceptable) and the editor renders TeX with the theme's plain text style — documented, tested, safe. Tests: format resolution for `tex`/`latex`/upper-case, no-preview invariants, open→edit→save byte-exact CRLF round-trip, Save As to/from `.tex` re-routing the format, and Toggle Comment with the TeX profile. **Carried forward:** a TeX highlighter if upstream publishes generated sources (or as a Slice 10 vendoring decision).
- **9d** — implemented: per-document Syntax Mode override. `SyntaxModeOverride` (mode format id + the base format id it was made against) lives on `WorkspaceTab`/`TabRecord` (persisted, legacy sessions decode as no override), `TabStore.setSyntaxMode(_:for:)` sets/clears it, and `WorkspaceTab.syntaxFormat` resolves the effective format. Only editing behaviour follows the override (highlighter language, `LanguageEditingProfile`, Markdown-specific assists); the file's real `document.format` still drives preview, outline, Save panel type, and never marks the document dirty. Recording the base format id makes a stale override (Save As / rename to another format, including on a background tab) inert without any view-level reset. A status-bar Syntax Mode menu ("Auto-detect (<format>)" plus every registered format) applies it. Independent review closed: the coordinator's real session writer (`sessionSnapshot`) and restore path (`applyRestoredState`) dropped the override — and, the same pre-existing defect, `previewMode` — so neither survived relaunch (both fixed with an app-level save/restore round-trip test); Markdown formatting commands now follow the effective Syntax Mode; an override is cleared when `document.format` changes so Save As back to the original format cannot revive it. **Carried forward:** Syntax Mode command-palette/menu entry (with the palette `notYetWired` set → Slice 10); the 9d strings are English-only in the String Catalog (with the 7b/7c/8b/8c gap); no app-level UI test of the menu itself.
- **9e** — implemented: native snippet system. `SnippetTemplate` (`${selection}`, `${clipboard}`, `$0` final caret, `\$`/`\\` escapes; anything else literal; single-pass so substituted text is never re-expanded; missing clipboard expands to empty), `EditorSnippetInsertion` (one `EditorEditTransaction` across all selections, so one undo step and one publication; per-caret indentation, `${selection}` and line ending; IME composition and read-only editors fail open), `Snippet`/`SnippetLibrary`/`SnippetCatalog`/`BuiltInSnippets` (format-scoped, user entries override built-ins by id, lossy per-entry decode), `SnippetStore` (versioned JSON in Application Support, atomic write; a missing, unreadable or newer-version file is never overwritten). App layer: Insert Snippet picker panel (mirrors Quick Open; targets the captured origin window), Snippets menu, palette `insertSnippet`/`editSnippets`. Review findings closed: a failed insert (IME composition, read-only, in-flight assist) no longer dismisses the picker silently, an insert is refused if the active tab changed since the list was scoped, `createIfMissing` uses an exclusive create so it cannot replace a file that appeared first, the origin-close observer is never registered without a window, and an unreadable/newer-version/partially-skipped `snippets.json` is explained in the picker. **Decision (revised in 10u):** `${selection}` is the document's own text and is inserted verbatim; `${clipboard}` comes from outside the document, so its line breaks follow the insertion point's own line ending (as the snippet body's do) — no mixed endings from a foreign-ending paste (invariant #5). **Carried forward → Slice 10:** tab stops / multi-placeholder navigation (only `$0` in this slice), a snippet-management UI beyond editing the JSON file, localisation of built-in snippet names and the new UI strings, continuation-line indent that also copies a `> `/list prefix, and panel-lifecycle tests (origin close, double present).

### Slice 10 — Orthogonal hardening

**Goal:** the full adversarial/performance/accessibility pass across every slice's surface together (interactions between multi-cursor + search, multi-cursor + snippets, encoding conversion + external edit, etc.), plus the corrected E05 Release performance evidence (§11).
**Dependencies:** Slices 1-9.

**Status (Slice 10 is split into sub-slices, one concern per PR; routing source of truth is the #183 audit):**
- **10a** — implemented (#174): the conditional publication path (every ordinary save of a loaded document, and Replace in Folder) now carries the destination's permission bits, owner/flags, ACL and extended attributes onto the staged file *before* the atomic exchange, so the existing rollback/recovery ownership is untouched and no post-publication chmod exists. Characterized natively: the unconditional `replaceItemAt` path already preserves mode and xattrs, so only the conditional path changed. Review findings closed: `COPYFILE_STAT` is deliberately **not** used (it copies timestamps, so a save would stop advancing the modification date, and `UF_IMMUTABLE`, which would make the staged file unswappable and undeletable); instead mode bits (mandatory), `UF_HIDDEN`/`UF_NODUMP`, ACL and xattrs are carried, ACL/xattr copying is skipped only for `ENOTSUP`/`ENOATTR` and any other failure is surfaced and the staged file cleaned up. Known limitation: the owner is not carried (the saving user owns the new file). Regression tests: mode matrix (0700/0600/0644/0755), xattr, ACL, mtime advances, unconditional overwrite, new file default, read-only destination, immutable destination leaves no staged file, and Replace in Folder keeping mode.
- **10b** — implemented (line-ending fidelity for inserted text, invariant #5): Return in a CRLF (or bare-CR) document no longer lets AppKit insert a bare `\n` when no Markdown assist applies (assists on, assists off, or the last line, which has no terminator of its own) — `EditorView.Coordinator.handleLineEndingAwareNewline` inserts the document's separator, failing open for IME composition, read-only editors and multiple selections, and never pre-empting a list/indent continuation (the assist outcome is applied directly, so it is computed once), covering every Return-family selector and every range of a real multi-selection; `lineSeparator(ofLineContaining:)` now falls back to the nearest terminator instead of `\n`. Multi-line replacement text (Find bar Replace/Replace All and Replace in Folder) adopts the target document's dominant line ending via `LineEnding.adaptingLineBreaks(in:toMatch:)`; text with no line break, or a document with no terminator, is left verbatim. Known limitations: Return is its own undo step in a CRLF document (the assist path breaks undo coalescing), and a lone trailing `\r` in an otherwise-CRLF document makes the next line follow that `\r`.
- **10c** — implemented (#183 F17): re-selecting the already-current "Reopen with Encoding" is a complete no-op at both the window entry point and `ExternalFileController.reopenWithEncoding` (new `.unchanged` result: no recovery drain, disk read, prompt, recovery mutation, undo reset or focus change); a genuinely different encoding keeps the existing confirm-before-discard and supersession guards. Adds the carried-forward app-level `reopenWithEncoding` tests (clean redecode, dirty declined, no-op when dirty, window entry point).
- **10d** — implemented (#183 F12): Sort Lines / Remove Duplicate Lines no longer reject the whole command when the selection set mixes multi-line groups with single-line ones — single-line groups are left untouched and only shifted by earlier groups' edits; the command stays a no-op only when no group spans more than one line.
- **10e** — implemented (#183 F23): the session writer records the editor's true primary selection (`selectionSet.primaryRange`) instead of AppKit's topmost range, so a multi-selection no longer restores the wrong primary. `SessionViewStateRoundTripTests.sessionSavesTheEditorsPrimarySelectionNotTheTopmostRange` sets a nonzero primary index and checks the persisted cursor and selection length; it does not itself relaunch the app. The test was reported failing without the fix in [merged PR189](https://github.com/Joncallim/macdown_2/pull/189) (source `40784e30313b9474bb77bca9927d17a98cfeadc8`, merge `1a18a71a2477a75f88c81a9c25f271f6eaff95aa`); its [build-and-test](https://github.com/Joncallim/macdown_2/actions/runs/36881332797), lint and inventory checks passed. The source and test remain ancestors of MostlyText main `f62efb7a4e639c9eb206944d5cc92dec3d9db661`; issue #17's native journey is still open.
- **10f** — implemented (#183 F16): status-bar content metrics (character count, word count, line-ending profile) are cached on `EditorTextSystem` by content revision (`documentMetrics()`), and the bar reads the live text as an uncopied `NSString`, so a selection-only change performs zero whole-document scans (a content change rescans exactly once). Tests assert the scan counter does not move across selection changes and does across typing, same-length edits and whole-document replacement. Final Release large-file latency evidence remains #115 E05-A.
- **10g** — implemented (#183 F03): "In Selection" keeps the search domain it was established with. `EditorFindModel.searchDomain` is sampled from the live selection only when Find opens or the option is turned on (never from the selection Find itself changes to the current match or a caret), is kept in step with edits (see 10w), and is cleared on close or when the option is turned off, so a Replace can no longer admit matches outside the originally selected range. A late refresh after Find closed no longer restores highlights. **Carried forward:** retaining the domain through ordinary user typing while Find stays open, and attaching transactions to an exact document/query/domain/session identity (the broader F03 hardening).
- **10h** — implemented (#183 F14, root-change half): changing or closing the folder root retires a running Replace in Folder (`retireReplaceRun`: cancels the task, clears `isReplacing`/summary, bumps `replaceGeneration`), and the run's late progress/summary are discarded unless its generation is still current, so an old-root run can no longer publish into a model now representing another root. Re-setting the same root does not retire it. Regression tests fail without the fix. **Carried forward:** the rooted physical-object/symlink-subtree write authority and cancellation-during-awaited-protection halves of F14, and F08.
- **10i** — implemented (#183 F11): recovery artifacts are read back exactly. `String(contentsOf:encoding: .utf8)` (and `String(data:encoding:)`) strip a leading `EF BB BF`, so an authored leading U+FEFF was deleted on recovery — reproduced natively on macOS. `RecoveryBuffer.readExactUTF8(at:)` decodes the bytes verbatim (`String(validating:as:)`, invalid UTF-8 rejected) and replaces every recovery-file read/verification. Scalar-for-scalar round-trip tests (fail without the fix); the UTF-16 variant from the audit was not reproduced and is not claimed.
- **10j** — implemented (#183 F04, distinct from #163): `JSONAnalysisSession` publishes only for the request that is still current — publication is guarded by the request generation, not just the calling task's cancellation, so `cancelPending()` and newer requests (an `analyzeNow` caller's own task is not the stored pending task) can no longer be overwritten by an older, late result; an already-cancelled caller's result is computed off the main actor and returned without publishing. The public API is unchanged (an internal analyzer seam is test-only). Regression tests fail without the fix.
- **10k** — implemented (#183 F22): Save As publishes against a destination baseline captured when the user authorises it — `DestinationBaseline.absent` (published with `RENAME_EXCL`, so a file created in the meantime is never replaced) or `.revision(r)` (ordinary conditional publication, rollback/recovery machinery unchanged, #174 metadata carried). Save As onto the document's own file keeps ordinary Save's external-writer protection while the document is healthy (backing available, no pending external change, known revision; recognised by path or physical identity, so a case-variant spelling cannot drop it); when the backing file is missing/unavailable or externally changed — the user resolving that by choosing the same name — the destination's current state is captured fresh, so recreating a deleted file and the explicit "replace" resolution still work. Symbolic-link destinations get no baseline (their behaviour is unchanged). On volumes without `RENAME_EXCL`, publication falls back to re-check plus `moveItem`. A refused publication surfaces as the usual save error and leaves the destination and the document untouched. Regression tests (file-store level and through `WorkspaceModel.saveAs`; the model-level race test fails without the fix).
- **10l** — implemented (#183 F10, writer half): `DocumentWriter` remembers the encoding each accepted output revision was written in, and an ordinary queued save whose snapshot predates an accepted encoding/BOM save inherits that encoding instead of silently writing the old one back (an explicit encoding on the later save still wins; entries are compacted with the lineage). Regression tests (stale-snapshot save fails without the fix). **Carried forward:** F10's error-label half (name the effective encoding attempted) belongs with F15.
- **10m** — implemented (#183 F02, persistence/identity boundaries): `String.isExactlyEqual(to:)` (UTF-8 scalar-for-scalar) replaces Swift's canonical `==` at the decisions that choose persistence, dirtiness or what to publish — `FileDocument.updatingText`/`rebindingSavedDestination`, external-snapshot reconciliation, successful-save descendant admission, Save As snapshot identity, recovery verification/migration, session-restore recovery comparison, the editor model→view admission guard, external-file reload/move comparisons and JSON-format verification — and Remove Duplicate Lines keys on bytes. Display/name comparisons are unchanged; no text is normalised. Regression tests use the audit's U+212B/U+00C5 pair (equal UTF-16 length, canonically equal) and fail without the fix. **Carried forward:** none identified; any further exact-source site found by the final review is an addition, not a gap.
- **10n** — implemented for fingerprinted records (#183 F21): Recent Files refuses a different physical file after path reuse. Verified natively that bookmarks resolve path-first (once an unrelated file sits at the old path they resolve to *it*), so each record now also stores a stable fingerprint (inode + creation time) of the recorded file and `resolve` refuses — and drops — an entry whose resolved object no longer matches. For a file that merely moved, `resolve` and `pruneMissingFiles` follow the bookmark and adopt the new path as the alias (`RecentFolderRoots`' keep-the-alias-only-while-it-identifies-the-object rule) instead of discarding a bookmark that still finds the file. Legacy raw and wrapped bookmarks without a fingerprint are preserved as unresolved and excluded from actionable Open Recent entries, including during whole-install relocation, until the user chooses the file again; that explicit open records a fresh fingerprint and retires the matching old bookmark. This prevents silent path-first reuse while preserving the stored bookmark for reauthorization. During relocation, a fingerprinted record is rebound only while its original lexical path still identifies the recorded object; a reused source path leaves the old record intact. The legacy regression cases fail before and pass after this source policy, and a mapped path-reuse control passes; native Open Recent and migration UI acceptance remain unverified. `RecentFileDocumentsTests` covers path-reuse refusal, moved-file adoption, working aliases and pruning; [merged PR198](https://github.com/Joncallim/macdown_2/pull/198) reports the path-reuse test failing without the fix (source `ab631ab372716b104e8f8d6832ba944cc41d35f3`, merge `78065580394b54e960fce4b4b32966b1b8ac5053`). Its [build-and-test](https://github.com/Joncallim/macdown_2/actions/runs/36910507184), lint and inventory checks passed. The source and tests remain ancestors of MostlyText main `f62efb7a4e639c9eb206944d5cc92dec3d9db661`; issue #17's full native and release gates are not inferred.
- **10o** — implemented (#183 F09): `EditorView.dismantleNSView` is now the `static` `NSViewRepresentable` requirement (the instance method was never the protocol witness, so SwiftUI's teardown never ran its observer/delegate cleanup). Because the text system outlives a mount, teardown only detaches what the dismantled mount still owns — the shared text view's delegate and the system's scroll view are left alone if a newer mount already took them. Tests dispatch through the generic `NSViewRepresentable` constraint (a direct instance call would have missed the defect) and cover current-mount teardown and older-mount-must-not-detach-newer; the existing real-mount lifecycle test now dispatches the same way. Coordinated with #173 (reveal lifecycle), which keeps its own scope.
- **10p** — implemented (#183 F05): a same-tab Save As that moves unchanged text into a format with a parser (e.g. `.txt` → `.json`/`.md`) now starts that format's initial analysis — the `document.format.id` change handler runs the same load path as a fresh open instead of only re-reading outline state that had never been computed. A mounted-view regression (`DocumentEditorSplitView` in a hosting view, format switched with identical text) fails without the fix; it proves the mounted symptom the audit left native-qualified. A syntax-only override still does not enable another format's preview.
- **10q** — implemented (#183 F15, both directions): (1) a Save/Save As failure for the latest save of the same document lifetime is now surfaced even if the user typed — or edited and undid — while the write was in flight (`shouldSurfaceSaveFailure` no longer requires the attempted text to still be current; newer-save/other-lifetime/closed-owner suppression and the recovery-location error are unchanged; text, undo and recovery are untouched and the document stays dirty). (2) Error publication/clearing is operation-owned: `WorkspaceModel.errorRevision` is bumped on every `lastError` assignment, `SaveContext` captures it when a save begins, and awaited-cleanup/retry success only clears the displayed error if it is unchanged since (`clearLastError(ifUnchangedSince:)`); user dismissal and an operation's own failures still assign directly. Regression tests (visible failure after an edit and after edit+undo; a late success does not erase another operation's error — fails without the fix). **Carried forward:** naming the *effective* encoding in an encoding failure after F10 inheritance, and extending the token to the open/close domains if the final review finds a path.
- **10r** — implemented (test hardening, recurring CI flake): `WindowCoordinatorPaletteOriginTargetingTests.newFileFromAnExplicitOrigin…` failed on three PRs with 70–164 s runs — the test waited only 3 s for the new window and then asserted on state (selected URL, loaded document) that had not finished yet. It now waits (up to 60 s, returning the moment the condition holds) for the whole post-creation sequence — new window, selection under the origin root, opened document loaded — before asserting. No product code changed; the stalls themselves (main-actor starvation on loaded runners) remain tracked under #155/#159/#163.
- **10s** — implemented (E05 Release performance evidence, §11, option (b) — local-Mac measurement; no CI pipeline change): `HighlightPerformanceTests` now enforces Release ceilings when built with `-c release` (the Debug configuration keeps its documented 8 s ceilings). Exact command: `cd MacDown2/Packages/MacDownKit && swift test -c release --filter HighlightPerformanceTests --no-parallel`. Measured 2026-10-02 on Apple silicon, 1 MB Markdown, three serial runs (stable to <2%): **full highlight 0.25 s** (E05 budget 500 ms — met), **full parse 0.196 s**, **incremental reparse after a one-character edit 0.194 s** — the same cost as a full parse and linear in document size (0.016 s at 100 KB, 0.087 s at 500 KB, 0.17 s at 1 MB, regardless of edit position), i.e. the old tree is not reused. **E05's 50 ms keystroke budget is therefore NOT met for 1 MB Markdown at the parser level** (Neon schedules it off the main actor; ordinary-size documents are well inside it). The 8 ms main-thread budget concerns viewport-slice work, not a whole-document parse, and is covered by `EditorPerformanceTests`. The other package benchmarks also pass in Release with large margins (`swift test -c release --filter "EditorPerformanceTests|WorkspaceFileIndexPerformanceTests|EditingAssistPerformanceTests" --no-parallel`: gutter/status caret update 0.35 s for the 1000-op loop, i.e. well under 1 ms/op, 100k-path index query 0.26 s for its trial set). Package-benchmark evidence only — complete app-path Release measurement stays with #115 E05-A. The unmet keystroke budget is an orthogonal finding recorded as an issue (#203); the CI Debug perf tests that intermittently miss their budgets on loaded runners are a load artefact, not a regression.
- **10t** — implemented (#183 F18, freshness half): the workspace file index no longer stays a root-open snapshot. `FileTreeModel.onDidMutate` fires after every successful create/rename/duplicate/move/trash and the window re-walks its index (`WindowController.refreshWorkspaceIndex`), and the index also refreshes when a window becomes key (throttled to once per 5 s) so changes made by other programs while the app was inactive are picked up; a newer walk supersedes an in-flight one and the previous snapshot stays queryable until it lands. No extra work on selection/search keystrokes (still disk-free). Tests: hook fires only for successful mutations; external add/delete appear after refresh; own operations refresh without an explicit call; no-folder refresh is a no-op; the interval throttle. **Carried forward:** root disappearance/unavailability invalidating Quick Open and Folder Search immediately, distinguishing an indexing failure from an empty workspace, re-running an active folder search when the index lands, and F19 (immutable navigation origin).
- **10u** — implemented (cross-feature interaction audit, line-ending fidelity): found by an independent adversarial pass over feature pairs, each with a reproducing test. Duplicate Line on a block starting at line 1 (e.g. Select All) no longer inserts a bare LF into a CRLF/CR document (the separator now comes from the document's own first terminator); Toggle Comment and Increase Indent now work on every line of a bare-CR document (split on the document's own separator) and Toggle Comment no longer treats blank lines in CRLF documents as non-blank (so a block containing one can be uncommented again); `${clipboard}` adapts to the insertion line ending. `String.contains("\n")` is false for a "\r\n" grapheme, so these checks scan UTF-8.
