Remaining audit findings are dispositioned in later sub-slices: stale In-Selection domain after non-Replace edits, Move Line Down out-of-bounds cached selection, transforms during IME composition, primary-selection flip on a touching caret, Move Up/Down fusing CR+LF in mixed-ending documents.
- **10v** — implemented (cross-feature audit, selection/IME robustness): editing commands that rewrite text (line transforms, text transforms incl. Convert Line Endings, Toggle Comment) now fail open during an IME composition and while an assist/programmatic update is running (`canApplyCommandEdit`) instead of committing the marked text — the same contract snippet insertion has; a transform's resulting selection is clamped to the post-edit text length, so Move Line Down with whole-line selections no longer leaves an out-of-bounds cached multi-selection that made later multi-cursor edits fail closed; and a touching zero-length caret no longer steals primary-ness from the real selection it abuts (`EditorSelectionSet.indexClosest` prefers exact, then non-empty containing, then caret). Remaining audit findings: stale In-Selection domain after non-Replace edits and Move Up/Down fusing CR+LF in mixed-ending documents.
- **10w** — implemented (cross-feature audit, Find "In Selection" follow-up to 10g): the retained domain now follows *every* text change, not just Replace. `EditorTextSystem.textChangeObserver` reports each atomic edit (pre-edit range + replacement length; multi-range transactions one edit per range, highest first) and each change without geometry (undo/redo, whole-document replacement); `EditorFindModel.noteTextChange` shifts/resizes the domain for typing, snippets, transforms, Convert Line Endings and Replace alike (an edit before the domain shifts it, inside resizes it, at its end leaves it, straddling a boundary covers the new text). A change with no geometry drops the domain and marks the scope lost — In Selection then matches nothing until the user re-establishes it (toggling the option / reopening Find) rather than silently widening to the whole document. The Find bar installs the observer while open. The explicit Replace-only remap is replaced by this single mechanism. Tests: model-level adjustments and loss/re-establish, and through a real text system (Convert Line Endings keeps the domain; undo loses it) — the audit's three repros. Remaining audit finding: Move Up/Down fusing CR+LF in mixed-ending documents.
- **10x** — implemented (test hardening, perf budgets): the two package benchmarks that kept flaking on loaded CI runners (gutter/status caret update 8 ms; 100k-path index query 30 ms) assert Release budgets against *unoptimized Debug* code (5–10 ms/op on a quiet machine). They now enforce the product budgets in Release (`swift test -c release …`, where they measure 0.35 ms/op and ~1 ms/query) and keep a 5x documented ceiling in Debug, the same policy the highlighting benchmarks follow (10s). No product code changed; no budget was loosened where it is enforced.
- **10y** — implemented (#183 F08, regex half): a superseded or cancelled regex search is now actually stopped. `TextSearchEngine` passes `.reportProgress` so ICU calls back while a pathological pattern backtracks (measured ~8k callbacks/s on `(x+x+)+y` against `xxxx…!`, which otherwise runs effectively forever), handles cancellation *before* `guard let match` (a progress call carries no match), and reports `SearchQueryError.cancelled` — partial output is never returned as a complete result. `EditorFindModel` keeps its in-flight search task and cancels it when a newer search starts (and treats a cancelled-but-current result as "no update", leaving the previous matches), so rapid edits cannot pile up unbounded regex work; Folder Search's existing per-file cancellation checks now terminate mid-regex. Tests: a cancelled pathological regex stops promptly with `.cancelled` (hangs without the fix), ordinary regexes unaffected, and a superseded search through the Find model. **Carried forward:** a hard wall-clock deadline for a non-cancelled pathological regex (none measured on macOS), and bounding *queued* work beyond the single in-flight search.
- **10ab** — implemented (#183 F19, navigation-origin half): results opened from Quick Open and Folder Search now carry the identity and context a sidebar open would. Quick Open opens `fileTreeModel.root` (the folder as the user opened it — a symlink root stays a symlink path) joined with the result, passing the folder root and physical access root to `openDocument` so the new window stays in the workspace instead of dropping folder context; Folder Search opens through `FolderSearchModel.lexicalRoot` (new; set with the index root) while still searching the physical root. `setFileTreeRoot` closes Quick Open before its first suspension, not only after the root changed, so rows from the old root are never actionable against the new one. Tests: lexical root kept/cleared, and a root switch closes the open panel. **Carried forward:** an explicit workspace-root generation token on every result (the audit's full "immutable origin" contract), root disappearance invalidating Quick Open/Folder Search immediately, indexing failure vs empty.
- **10z** — implemented (#183 F20, autosave half): the debounced session autosave no longer publishes an obsolete snapshot. `scheduleSaveSession` cancels the previous task, but a task already past its debounce kept running through the awaited recovery work and then published its stale session over the newer one. `saveSessionResult(isAutosave:)` — used only by the debounced path — now re-checks cancellation immediately before publication and yields; explicit callers (termination, Save As, rename publishers) always publish. Tests (a cancelled autosave publishes nothing — fails without the fix; a live autosave and an explicit save still publish). **Carried forward:** an explicit call that began earlier overwriting a newer autosave's snapshot (needs a publication sequence owned by the session store) and the direct Save As/rename/termination publisher races.
- **10ad** — implemented (test hardening, root cause of the recurring New File flake traced): a diagnostic CI run of `WindowCoordinatorPaletteOriginTargetingTests.newFileFromAnExplicitOrigin…` showed the *main actor blocked for ~110 s at a time* right after the new document window is created (two such stalls, ~220 s total, test then passes with the file selected, the new window present and no error) — opening a real window on the CI runner builds its WebKit preview, matching the known WebKit-on-CI hang (#155; the logs show WebContent/pboard sandbox errors). So this is runner/WebKit starvation, not a product race and not a lost operation. The test's condition wait (returns the moment it holds) is raised to 600 s accordingly; nothing else changed. Issue #155 remains the owner of the underlying stall.
- **10ae** — implemented (#183 F07, shared source-map line model): `SourceMap` treated only `\n` as a line terminator and measured a CRLF line's end before only the LF, so on CRLF documents every block range kept a trailing `\r` and bare-CR documents collapsed to a single line. It now recognises `\n`, `\r\n` and lone `\r` (CommonMark's three endings), records each line's terminator length, and ends line ranges before the whole terminator. D2/Graphviz/Mermaid fence-content stripping shares the new `String.markdownLines()` (UTF-8 scan, since `"\r\n"` is one Character) so CRLF fences no longer keep a trailing `\r` and bare-CR fences are found. Regression tests cover SourceMap line ranges, the `[TOC]` marker and the three fence scanners for LF/CRLF/CR. F06 remains with #118. A pre-existing `TextFilterProcessGroupTests` descriptor-reuse flake seen while verifying is tracked in #215.
- **10af** — implemented (#183 audit finding 9, Move Line Up/Down fusing CR+LF): in a document that mixes `\r` and `\n` terminators, swapping lines could leave a moved `\r` directly before an unrelated `\n` (an empty line between them), which the next parse reads as one `\r\n` — a line vanished and terminators were rewritten. The move now detects a created CR-before-LF seam at every replacement edge (including touching multi-group replacements) and declines the whole command instead (invariant 5: no silent rewriting of line endings). An exhaustive test over every 2–4 line document built from empty/non-empty lines and all three terminators proves every applied move is a pure permutation (fails without the guard), plus explicit decline/apply cases.
- **10ag** — implemented (#183 F01, recovery-registry payload identity): `PendingRecoveryCleanupAction` is identified by kind + exact lifetime (migration/acknowledgement now include the source lifetime too) but carries a payload (text, mutation, state). Registration used `Set.insert`/dictionary assignment, which keep the OLDER equal member, so a second failure for the same lifetime left a stale payload (and stale Save-As continuation) to be retried. All registrations now go through `registerPendingRecovery(_:continuation:)`, which replaces the member and its continuation; completion goes through `completePendingRecovery(_:)`, which clears only if the registered payload is the one that was replayed, and the retry loop replays the CURRENT registration — so a newer failure registered while an older replay awaits is never dropped. Regression tests (newer payload wins and is what retry persists; stale completion leaves the newer pending; migrations from different sources stay distinct) fail without the change.
- **10ah** — implemented (#183 F20, publication ordering): the cancel check covered only the debounced autosave; two NON-cancelled session saves (e.g. an explicit termination/Save As/rename publisher and a later autosave or another explicit save) could still complete in inverted order, the older snapshot rolling the canonical session back. Every `saveSessionResult` now takes a snapshot sequence when its snapshot is built (`SessionPublicationOrder`), and skips publication — returning `.saved`, since a later snapshot of the same documents already published — if a newer snapshot has published first; a successful publication records its sequence. A test seam (`afterSessionRecoveryPersisted`) lets the regression force snapshot A to suspend, move selection state, complete B, then resume A and prove the newer session survives (fails without the guard). `focusOutline` moved from `WindowCoordinator.swift` to `WindowCoordinator+Workspace.swift` to stay under the file-length limit.
- **10ai** — implemented (#183 F10 error-label half, closes F10): a save whose text the encoding cannot hold failed with the generic `encodingDetectionFailed` and the model labelled it from `encodingOverride ?? document.encoding` — wrong for a queued ordinary save that inherited an accepted encoding change (10l), which would name the stale snapshot's old encoding. `FileStore.write` now throws a dedicated `FileStoreError.textNotRepresentable(attempted:)` carrying the encoding+BOM it actually attempted, and `workspaceError(for:)` (shared by every save path, including Save As) maps it to `WorkspaceError.textNotRepresentable(encodingName:)` from that value. Replace-in-Folder still reports `skippedCannotRepresent`; exhaustive switches updated. Regression test (a stale UTF-8 snapshot inheriting Latin-1 fails naming Latin-1) plus the existing explicit-override label test.
- **10aj** — implemented (#183 F14, symlink-subtree write authority): FileTree mutators validated only the LEXICAL path, so with `root/link -> /outside` every create, rename, duplicate, move, copy-in and trash below the link was accepted and landed outside the approved tree (a failing test performed real writes outside the root before the fix). `validate` now also requires each member's containing directory to RESOLVE under the root's physical location; the member itself may be a link (renaming/trashing the link acts on the link, and navigation/reads through links are unchanged). `create` also validates outside its `do/catch`, so a refusal is the typed `.outsideCurrentRoot` instead of being stringified into `.underlying`. Tests cover all seven mutators plus the still-allowed cases. **Carried forward:** this is check-then-act (a link inserted between validation and the filesystem call is not anchored by `openat`-style fd authority); true anchoring is shared low-level work with #118/#121, and F14's cancellation-during-awaited-protection half and F08 remain.
- **10ak** — implemented (#183 F19, query-generation half): Quick Open kept the previous query's rows on screen — and actionable — while a newer query was pending, so Return/click in that window opened a file the current text no longer asked for. `QuickOpenModel.selectedResult` is now `nil` while `isSearching`, so Return and row clicks are inert until the response for the current query generation lands (the rows stay visible to avoid flicker). Folder Search already clears its results when a new search starts (verified, no change). Regression test with a held query seam (fails without the guard). **Carried forward:** an explicit workspace-root-generation token on every result (root switches already close Quick Open, 10ab) and root-disappearance invalidation (F18).
- **10al** — implemented (#183 F18, root-unavailability half): a workspace root that vanished or became unreadable walked to an empty list, so `WorkspaceFileIndex` reported `.ready(count: 0)` — indistinguishable from a genuinely empty folder — and (until the walk landed) the previous snapshot's rows stayed queryable. `rebuild` now checks the root after the walk and, if it is missing, not a directory or unreadable, drops the snapshot and reports `.failed`; an existing-but-empty folder is still `.ready(count: 0)`. Quick Open reads that state (`QuickOpenModel.isIndexUnavailable`) and shows "Folder Unavailable" instead of "No Matching Files". Tests: vanished root fails and drops the stale snapshot, empty root is ready, the model distinguishes the two. An existing walk-gate test now uses a real directory root. **Carried forward:** Folder Search surfacing the same failure and re-running an active search when the index lands, and the untranslated Quick Open strings (no catalog entry exists for any Quick Open text — part of the known localisation gap).
- **10am** — implemented (#183 R01): the status-bar encoding menu (Reopen with Encoding / Save with Encoding) resolved its target through `NSApp.keyWindow`, so activating it acted on whichever window was key — not the window that owns the bar (a different window, or none when the app is not active, would be targeted or silently ignored). The bar now routes through `WindowCoordinator.reopenDocument(in:withEncoding:)` / `saveDocument(in:withEncoding:)`, which resolve the controller from the bar's own `WorkspaceModel`; the menu-bar commands keep key-window routing, which is correct for them. Regression test: with two windows and no key window, saving with an encoding from B's bar changes only B. Native proof stays with #88.
- **10an** — implemented (#183 F14, cancellation-during-protection half): `WorkspaceReplaceEngine.replace` checked `Task.isCancelled` BEFORE awaiting the caller's protection query, so a run retired while that await was suspended (root change, 10h) continued into `apply` and wrote the file for a root the user had left. The cancellation check is repeated after the await (`outcome(for:root:isProtected:)`), marking the file `.notAttempted`; a file already being published is still finished. Regression test cancels the task from inside the protection query (fails without the second check, with the file rewritten). **Carried forward:** F14's anchored-fd authority (see 10aj), F08's native deadline, Folder Search surfacing an index failure.
- **10ao** — implemented (#183 F18, Folder Search half): with a failed workspace index (10al) Folder Search read zero indexed paths and reported "No Results". `WorkspaceSearchEngine.search` now returns `FolderSearchOutcome.indexUnavailable` before searching when the index is `.failed`, and the Folder Search status line shows "Folder Unavailable". Test: a vanished root yields `.indexUnavailable` and no results. **Carried forward / not claimed:** re-running an active folder search when a rebuilt index lands (a user re-typing or the next search picks it up), the F08 native deadline proof, F14's anchored-fd authority (shared with #118/#121) and #173's native activation/reveal proof — each needs native/shared work outside this slice series.
- **Slice 10 closure (2026-10-02):** every #183 finding is fixed with regression coverage or explicitly delegated (F06/F13 → #118, R02 → #121, L01 → #17). **Carried forward, not claimed:** F14's descriptor-anchored (`openat`) authority (shared with #118/#121), F08's native deadline proof, re-running an active folder search when a rebuilt index lands, #173's native activation/reveal reproduction, and #203 (O(n) incremental reparse vs the 50 ms budget at 1 MB). The cross-feature interaction pass and corrected E05 Release evidence (10s/10x) are done. The deep orthogonal product review that follows the re-gate is tracked outside this slice series.

### Slice 11 — Release re-gate

**Status:** E22 acceptance criteria are dispositioned in `planning/RELEASE_EVIDENCE.md` ("E22 (#112) re-gate", 2026-10-02): implemented with automated evidence, Release-app/manual evidence unverified until #115, E16 post-E22 delta recorded as a partial update (E23 not complete; final freeze deferred), no known E22-owned P0/P1.

**Goal:** re-run affected E15 evidence rows, produce E16's post-E22 localisation delta (extraction + fr/pl/ja translation + pseudo-localisation, per `epic-16-implementation.md`'s established workflow) **only if** the owner has by then also completed E23 per the current sequencing (E22 → identity re-freeze → E23 → #115 → final E16 freeze) — otherwise this slice records E22's own string/UI delta honestly as a partial update to the interim baseline and defers the *final* freeze declaration to after E23/#115, per §12 above. Confirm zero unresolved P0/P1 introduced by E22 before considering the epic release-complete.

### Post-gate orthogonal review (pass 1, 2026-10-02)

Independent adversarial reviewers (read-only, separate checkouts) audited editing, file lifecycle, Markdown/preview/export and workspace/UI. Every reported finding is reproduced by this session before it is accepted; accepted findings are fixed one concern per PR with regression tests. Lifecycle-reviewer findings and their disposition:

- **R1a** — implemented (P0, recovery): `RecoveryBuffer` judged every non-current managed lifetime at or below the GLOBAL generation high-water mark as retired, but epochs are minted when a document is created, not when it first persists. With two documents open, persisting the newer-minted one first made the older-minted one's recovery writes refuse forever (unsaved edits unprotected, session not published, Quit reports "Recovery Could Not Be Secured"). Lifetimes this process minted (`mintRecoveryEpoch`) or adopted from a restored session (`adoptRecoveryEpoch`, called by `TabStore.restoreTab`) and has not retired are now judged only against their own document's history; the global mark still guards lifetimes the process has no claim on, and retiring a live lifetime removes the exemption. Tests: older-minted lifetime of another document can persist after a newer one (fails without the fix), retired older lifetime stays refused, restored lifetime needs adoption.
- **R1c** — implemented (P1, session fidelity): the canonical session writer (`WindowCoordinator.sessionSnapshot`) built each `TabRecord` without the document's encoding/BOM (only the test-only `TabStore.currentSession` set it). A file reopened as Latin-1/Shift-JIS or BOM-less UTF-16 therefore failed strict UTF-8 on relaunch (a clean tab vanished, a dirty one came back "unavailable"), and an untitled document created with a non-UTF-8 default restored as UTF-8 so the first save silently changed the encoding (invariant #5). The record now carries `encoding`. Test: a Latin-1 document's session record round-trips its encoding.
- **R1b..** — pending, accepted for reproduction: dirty documents dropped on a file-open/new-document launch (session not restored, recovery orphaned); canonical session record omits encoding/BOM; restored dirty tab overwrites a file changed while the app was quit; conditional publication has no fallback on volumes without `RENAME_SWAP` (exFAT/SMB); move probe rebinds the document to a `.bak` sibling; symlinked file cannot be opened/saved; recovery restore/close use canonical-equivalence `==`; over-long file names fail to save (temp name); mode-0444 file is overwritten; Save As onto an already-open path yields two windows with one document id. Product decisions to record rather than fix blindly: opening a non-UTF-8 file (there is no open-with-encoding path).

### Post-gate orthogonal review (pass 2, 2026-10-03)

Six fresh adversarial reviewers re-audited master after pass 1 ("assume material defects remain"). Findings were reproduced before acceptance and fixed one concern per PR with regressions. Regressions this session's own pass-1 fixes introduced are listed first.

**Regressions from pass-1 fixes (fixed):** Edit ▸ Undo/Redo did nothing after the per-tab undo manager (#261, P0); a normal full restore left the launch session live so the next Finder open duplicated dirty windows (#262); Open Recent dropped any file once saved (#263).

**Merged in this pass:** #260 frame-height sync no longer quadratic; #263 Open Recent follows saves; #264 Open With Encoding on an already-open file re-reads it; #265 Save As refuses a name another window has open; #266 restore reads recovery from the coordinator's buffer; #267 export no longer overflows the stack on deep nesting (P0) and the YAML alias-bomb guard covers non-alphanumeric anchor names (P0); #268 footnote/free-text lines no longer leak into Preview and only referenced link definitions are prefixed; #269 math is not typeset inside URLs, HTML tags or front matter; #270/#271 timing-flake margin and blank-line-after-`---` is a thematic break; #272 contributed diagram HTML is sanitised (`javascript:` links, scripts, handlers); #273 inline raster `data:` images survive export; #274 diagram timeout bounds the caller and replaces a hung page; #275 Go to Line trims/saturates; #276 Increase Indent honours tabs; #277 Sort/Dedupe decline when they would fuse CR+LF; #278 a dirty tab restored with its file missing keeps its baseline; #279 deleted-file discard retires recovery and a clean close rechecks after the async retire; #280 opens serialise by physical file identity; #281 Save As onto a symlink name writes through to the target; #282 Save As destination baseline is captured off the main actor.

**Evaluated and not changed (recorded, not silently dropped):**
- `WindowCoordinator+Workspace.swift:192` / `WindowController+Close.swift:164` URL comparisons: both sides are standardized URLs of the same document lifetime; the check exists to detect a rename. Not a defect.
- Regex `$1` capture-group replacement: Replace is literal-only by design today; supporting captures is a feature (needs capture ranges in `SearchMatch`), out of scope for this tranche.
- Bare-CR documents in the Markdown editing assists (`lineStart`/`lineContentEnd` treat CR as part of one line): invariant #5 holds (separators follow the document), the behaviour for classic-Mac files is degraded; deferred.
- Restoring a tab whose path has since become a symlink: resolving it would change the document id its recovery record is keyed by; today a clean tab is dropped and a dirty one comes back "unavailable" (no text lost). Deferred.
- Every save replaces the file's creation date and breaks hard links (Foundation-style atomic replace); deferred, documented limitation.

**Still open from the Markdown/Preview report:** block-placed derived content (display math, diagram fences) inside lists/block quotes is hoisted out of its container; setext-heading range repair with a following table; unserialised/uncancellable parse; front-matter `lines()` allocation; lone-CR front matter; YAML date values/duplicate keys; unterminated-fence content loss; scroll-sync controller/map gap disagreement; export copies any local file as an "image"; diagram cache byte accounting; `performExport` snapshot ordering; math scanning of escaped backticks; remaining export/CSS polish. UI, localisation and accessibility items (Open Folder with no document window, ⌘⌫/⌘D in text fields, window-size restore, plurals, untranslated strings, VoiceOver labels) are queued last per the stated priority order.

### Post-gate orthogonal review (pass 3, 2026-10-04)

Four fresh reviewers (editor/IME, lifecycle/data integrity, Markdown/preview/export, security/performance/UI) re-audited master after pass 2, again told to assume material defects remain and to look hardest at bugs introduced by earlier fixes. Roughly half of the pass-3 findings were defects in this session's own pass-2 changes, which is why a further pass was worth running.

**Shipped bugs found and fixed:** the HTML preview pane rendered blank on current macOS because scheme responses carried no `Content-Type` (#292, reproduced in a real `WKWebView`; the existing UI test only checked that the pane existed); a conflict restored at launch was cleared to plain dirty by the monitor's first probe so Save overwrote the external change (#293); a SwiftUI update during IME/dead-key composition crashed the app and compositions left the line index stale (#294, P0); the outline kept the previous tab's headings after a tab switch (#297); inline math in a GFM table cell exported as an empty cell, via a failed `cmark_node_replace` followed by freeing a still-linked node (#299); a dirty tab restored with its file missing kept no baseline (#278, pass 2) and the restored-conflict analogue above.

**Defects in my own pass-2 fixes, repaired:** the derived-HTML sanitizer corrupted math `alt` text and could be reassembled into a blocked tag (#296, rewritten as a linear, tag-aware, idempotent scanner); math LaTeX reached the renderer containing masking characters and the link-destination scan was quadratic (#295); link definitions were never found in CRLF files and prefixing was still O(blocks × definitions) (#298); display math mid-line and fences on list-marker lines split their container (#299); typing three backticks produced four (#301); Save As cancelled silently when typed into during the off-main baseline read and a case-only Save As failed (#302); the other close paths lacked the post-retire recheck (#306); duplicate windows from a Finder open racing the launch restore (#309).

**Also fixed:** Trim Trailing Whitespace, Decrease Indent and Move Line could fuse CR+LF in mixed-ending files (#300); one undecodable tab dropped the whole session and an unreadable `session.json` was overwritten (#303); a dead WebKit content process left diagram pools and the HTML preview broken (#304); test-only launch arguments were honoured in release builds (#305); three quadratic paths (TOC markers, inline sentinels, outline remap, #307); the folder index walk had no bound (#308, capped at 250,000 entries rather than reversing the documented decision to follow linked directories); file-tree duplicate naming for `.tar.gz` and rejection of `.`/`..` (#310).

**Known limitations carried to the release report (not fixed):** hardening very large HTML (the CSP-injection tokenizer is security-critical; the 5 MB timing was a debug build and is unmeasured in release); `webView` page-load timeouts in `DiagramHarnessPage.make`; `OutlineIdentityMap` label/Unicode folding corners; standalone HTML export deliberately keeps authored raw HTML (self-contained and PDF do not); PDF export runs `NSPrintOperation` on the main thread; `application(_:openFiles:)` does not reply to unreadable files; regex `$1` replacement, bare-CR Markdown assists, restore through a path that has become a symlink, creation-date/hard-link behaviour on save, and recovery lifetimes that persisted once then retired not being re-rotated after a cancelled close (all recorded in pass 2 / above); plus the native-proof-owed items (first-run window, Dock reopen, menu enabled-state for Close and Folder commands, tab order, live key-equivalent behaviour, French lookup in an Xcode build, undo menu in the built app).

### Post-gate orthogonal review (pass 4, 2026-10-04)

Four further reviewers re-audited master after pass 3 with the same brief. Again roughly half of the findings were defects in the previous wave's own fixes (derived-content placement, the sanitizer, close/restore paths), so a fifth pass follows this wave.

**Fixed:** a sentinel leaking into exported HTML when a contribution sat inside a construct the placement rules could not honour now falls back to the author's source text with a diagnostic (#319); IME/multi-cursor state and absolute selections for Tab/Heading (#317, #318); quit-during-restore, window-size restore and the font fallback (#314, #315); sanitizer parser-differential (#316); PDF navigation policy (#312); outline/preview identity now a per-session UUID, single-pass deferred-sentinel substitution, linear `](…)` scanning, setext-heading placement (#320); Replace All/Trim no longer patch the line index once per range (#321, ~17.8 s to ~4.7 s for 20k matches); a restored dirty tab whose file reappears unchanged is no longer a conflict (#322); fence closers must match their opener and a backtick info string is not a fence (#323); a second ⌘W no longer stacks close sheets (#324, no automated test possible in the unit host).

**Deliberately not changed:** Open Recent entries still drop when a file is atomically replaced by another tool, because the bookmark resolves by path afterwards and cannot be told from an unrelated file at that path (reverses the #183 F21 decision, so left for a product call); a transient recovery-read error during restore still drops that tab from the restored session (the recovery file stays on disk; testing needs a failing-read seam in `RecoveryBuffer`).

**Still queued (lower priority, per the stated order):** case-only Save As does not rename the directory entry; `"""`/`'''` and `x <` pairing; Capitalize via Foundation `capitalized`; directory-walker cap truncates silently; localisation (numeric format keys, missing `bundle: .module`, plurals, incomplete fr/pl/ja app catalog, unlocalised "Untitled"), VoiceOver labels and Reduce Motion, naming inconsistencies ("MacDown 2"/"macdown2"/"MostlyText"), Recent-file menu labels, font-family picker for an uninstalled default; distribution blockers (no LICENSE/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES in the bundle, no About/licences screen, `compliance.py check --release` open items).

### Post-gate orthogonal review (pass 5, 2026-10-04)

Four reviewers (editor, lifecycle/data integrity, Markdown/export, security/performance/UI) re-audited master after pass 4. The lifecycle reviewer found nothing above P3; the others found one P0 and a handful of P1/P2s, two of which were regressions from pass-4 fixes (#321, #320).

**Fixed:** front matter with a sequence/mapping/alias YAML key (`[ref]: x`, `? [a, b]`) crashed the process on open, typing and restore via a Yams force-unwrap (#326, P0); opening a CJK/emoji document under 100 KB froze the main thread for seconds through one whole-document `boundingRect` (#329, P1); the #321 rebuild-once change reported `.untracked` and so dropped Find's "In Selection" scope on Replace All (#327); the #320 setext guard wrapped a diagram fence followed by `---` in an `<h2>` (#328); a `$` in code flipped math pairing and `$$` paired across blank lines (#330); an HTML file that repeatedly killed WebContent reloaded in a tight loop (#331); ⌃F left the Folder menu's enabled state stale so Return/⌘D could rename/duplicate from the Find field (#332); symlink fan-out could run the folder index walk for hours (#333, directory budget); Replace All is applied as one native edit above 64 ranges (#334, 4.7 s to under a second for 20k matches); Toggle Comment CRLF fusion and Join Lines blank-line handling (#335); folder search skips files over 32 MB (#336); the session records the visible tab as active when the app is inactive (#337); TOML is a declared document type (#338).

**Deliberately not changed:** two recovery-lifetime edge cases — retiring a lifetime that never persisted does not fence it (a late debounced write leaves an orphan recovery file), and a restore whose session still names an already-retired epoch cannot persist edits until Save As (needs a crash inside the 300 ms session debounce). Both sit in the fence protocol that earlier passes hardened, are low severity, and a change there risks more than it fixes.

**Still queued (P3):** zero-width regex matches can split a CRLF in Replace All; pasted text is not adapted to a CRLF document's ending; `*it*` at line start produces `*it**`; self-contained HTML export keeps remote fetches inside diagram HTML; diagram fences nested in a quote/list hand their container prefixes to the renderer; the preview scheme handler reads whole subresources on the main actor; Math preview preprocessing is not memoised across caret moves; preview base URL is not refreshed by an unedited Save As; the Commands menu discovers filters from disk inside `body`.

### Post-gate orthogonal review (pass 6, 2026-10-04)

Four reviewers re-audited master after pass 5 (fuzzing their areas: ~3.5M editor inputs, ~250k export pipelines, 1.5M sanitizer inputs, 700k YAML inputs, 4,000-document lifecycle model runs). No P0. The recent fixes held up under fuzzing; the findings were one P1, a set of P2s in code older than this session, and P3 edge cases.

**Fixed:** YAML front matter within the alias *count* budget still expanded ~256x (a ~20 KB file cost 3.5 s and 324 MB per parse) — the expanded size is now budgeted (#340, P1); a corrupt `recovery-fences.json` stopped every file open and new document until deleted by hand — now quarantined (#341); Shift-Tab could leave a selection end between a CR and its LF and typing there ate the CR (#342); Return at the end of a non-last list item inserted a bare blank line instead of a bullet, reversing a pinned test that described the bug (#343); math delimiters and export sentinels were matched per `Character`, so a `$` or sentinel glued to a ZWNJ/ZWJ/VS16/combining scalar (Persian text) mispaired and swallowed prose — the scanner is now UTF-16 with a differential test against the old one (#344); the sidebar drag token expired after 30 s and was consumed by a failed drop, and its UTI was undeclared (#345); the preview reload budget no longer resets on `didFinish` (#346); huge documents and huge paragraphs are sized by estimate instead of re-measured after every edit pause (#347); transaction ties at one offset are ordered deterministically (#348); multi-caret Join Lines with a caret on the last line (#349); ⌘Q before the launch restore no longer publishes an empty session (#350); a leading U+FFFE in BOM-less UTF-16 is refused (#351); Replace in Folder keeps Open Recent entries valid (#352); block-quote `>` markers are stripped from multi-line display math in Export and Preview (#353); a child block's line range is clamped to its parent (#354).

**Still queued (P3):** inline math inside image alt text vanishes without a diagnostic; the leaked-sentinel retry is not re-checked (only reachable with overlapping contributions); multi-selection transforms are O(selections × lines); a clean document that sees a transient read failure is forced `.dirty` and later reconciles as a spurious conflict; `_`/`*` auto-pairing after a word at end of line leaves a stray delimiter; Open Recent bookmarks resolve with mounting/UI allowed and drop entries on an unmounted volume; the Dock icon does nothing when every window is minimized (unverified); `ParseEngine` stack overflows at ~900k nested `>` (hostile only); front-matter `title` scalar coercion (`1.10` → `1.1`); `MathContribution` is O(spans × exclusions) on multi-MB inputs; self-contained export keeps remote fetches inside diagram HTML; a failed open re-reads the whole file on the main actor for encoding detection.

### Post-gate orthogonal review (pass 7, 2026-10-09)

Four reviewers re-audited master after pass 6. Two P1s and one P1 regression of mine, plus P2/P3s; the lifecycle reviewer again found nothing above P2. Most findings were one class: Foundation/`Character`-level string operations treating a delimiter plus a grapheme-extending scalar (U+0301, ZWNJ/ZWJ, VS16, U+1885) as a different unit.

**Fixed:** opening an `.html` file ending in an unterminated `<head`/`<html` hung the main thread in the CSP-injection scan, on every open and every relaunch of the restored tab (#356, P1); a `"` followed by a combining mark stayed unescaped in sanitized diagram HTML — script injection into self-contained/standalone exports — and in math `alt` text (#357, P1/P2, `.literal` and per-scalar escaping); my #347 height estimate under-estimated CJK/emoji and ignored the line-height multiple, leaving the end of very large documents unscrollable — now pessimistic, with a larger paragraph cache (#358, P1); the unrepresentable-text error now explains an invisible leading U+FEFF/U+FFFE (#359); regex `\n` matches the whole CRLF and empty matches inside a pair are dropped (#360); Return and Tab/Shift-Tab apply to every selection of a multi-selection (#361); Preview link-definition labels are scanned per scalar (#362); files with `:` in their name can be moved/copied in the sidebar (#363).

**Still queued (P3):** Heading/Paragraph on a blank CRLF line rewrites its terminator; multi-caret Join Lines merges touching groups; Find "In Selection" is lost on IME/dead-key commits; pasting a single `(`/`*`/`` ` ``/`_` over a selection wraps it; Move Line can leave a selection end between CR and LF; whole-word literal search misses an overlapping match; the fence classifier counts parity over a 5,000-line window ignoring opener character/length; text-filter output is not adapted to the document's line ending; "Use Disk Version" discards edits without confirmation (may be intended); an unreadable recovery file for a file-backed tab silently restores the disk version (adjacent to the recorded transient-read item); the preview CSP `<meta>` can be injected into a script body (the authoritative response header still applies); `MathContainerPrefix.isInsideQuote` is quadratic on a very long single line; Preview math preprocessing does not mask URLs/HTML like Export does; `OutlineIdentityMap.nearestUnclaimed` is O(n²) for many identical headings.

### Post-gate orthogonal review (pass 8, 2026-10-09)

Three Opus reviewers (editor, lifecycle, Markdown/export) re-audited master after pass 7 and swept their areas for the pass-7 class (Character-level string operations beside grapheme-extending or Unicode *Prepend* scalars). The first attempt failed on the spend limit and returned nothing; it was re-run. Two P1s, three P2s, three P3s; the class also reached guards, not just escapes.

**Fixed:** Replace in Folder wrote through a symlinked directory when the file name started with a combining mark — `relativePath.split("/")` merged the slash with the mark, so the directory above was never symlink-checked (#365, P1); `HTMLEscaping.escape` (the self-contained export `<title>`/`<h1>`) skipped a `<`/`>`/`"` after a Prepend scalar, defeating the no-raw-HTML rule (#365, P1); sentinel collision/leak checks, the sanitizer's early return and the typed-name separator check were Character-level (#365); my #360 regression dropped the LF half of a line ending when a pattern matched the CR and LF separately (`\s`, `[\r\n]`) (#366); line-break adaption for Replace/snippets added or kept the wrong breaks next to combining marks (#366); the huge-document height estimate was still too short for U+2028/NEL lines, tabs and long words — now a greedy-wrap simulation that errs long (#367); glob include/exclude matching, Toggle Comment's inserted space and a recovery-migration key containing `|` now work per scalar / at the right separator (#368).

**Still queued (P3):** carried from pass 7 (see above) plus: `PreviewContributionAdmission` and `FencedCodeBlockScanner` treat U+2028 as a line break where cmark does not (shifts only where Preview math / export placement lands); `HTMLEscaping`-adjacent TOC escaping is per `Character` (formatting only, sanitised downstream).

## 18. Definition of Done and residual risk

Per `EPIC_STANDARD.md` §4, plus the epic issue's own acceptance criteria (both the original ~19-item list and the "Additional acceptance criteria" from the 2026-09-21 inherited-debt amendment) — not restated verbatim here to avoid drift between two copies; this document's binding completion gate is: **every checkbox in the epic issue is either checked with linked evidence, or explicitly, individually dispositioned (FIXED/PROVED/REJECTED-FOR-1.0) in `RELEASE_EVIDENCE.md`, before E22 is considered release-complete for the #115 gate.**

**Consciously deferred / residual risk to track as follow-up issues, not silently dropped:**
- Same-document split view remains explicitly out of scope (architectural — one `NSTextView` per document).
- The remaining priority locales beyond fr/pl/ja (per E16's existing disposition) are not this epic's responsibility to add.
- If §9.6's evidence-based review concludes no safe `.tex`/`.latex` highlighter dependency is currently integrable, the plain-text-styled fallback is the recorded, tested, deliberate 1.0 disposition — not a placeholder for later work, unless the architecture review itself identifies a concrete follow-up path.
- Exact fuzzy-ranking formula, gutter/status SwiftUI-vs-AppKit ownership split (§5.2), and the precise Cmd-D/first-responder event set (§2.1's "purely-keyboard focus transfer is not currently in `shouldRefreshCommandState`'s switch" open question) are intentionally left to their owning slice rather than pre-decided here, per `EPIC_STANDARD.md`'s Layer 2/Layer 3 split — each will be recorded as an addendum to this document when that slice lands.

## Changelog

- 2026-09-21: Initial architecture, authored against baseline `95e4f60` before any implementation slice began.
- 2026-09-21: Slice 1 landed (PR #123). Reconciled this document against three real bugs an external automated review of that PR found and that were fixed, not merely re-documented: `EditorLineIndex`'s incremental rescan lacked a leading margin (§6.1), `EditorSelectionSet.indexClosest` used inclusive rather than half-open containment (§6.2), and `EditorEditTransaction`'s overlap/bounds validation used `precondition` (traps in Release too, contradicting this document's own "Release drops, doesn't crash" contract) with zero test coverage of the invalid-input path (§6.3). Also reconciled §17's Slice 1 "no changes to `EditorView`/`EditorTextSystem`" boundary with §6.3's already-documented, narrow, required exception for `isApplyingMultiRangeTransaction`, and closed a `WorkspaceFileIndex`/`DirectoryWalker` gap where symlinked directories were misclassified as files (§2 baseline).
- 2026-09-21: Slice 1 owner review corrected `EditorEditTransaction`'s validation from a partial "valid prefix" fallback to true all-or-nothing rejection of the whole transaction (§6.3's third correction), fixed a crash in the rewritten validator (`1 ..< 0` on an empty transaction) and a flaky supersession test in `WorkspaceFileIndexTests`, and closed the requested test matrix (empty/single/multi-element, overflow, first/middle/last invalid-position, mounted-`NSTextView` zero-mutation regressions). PR #123 merged to `master` at `338f800` with 1401/1401 package tests passing and the complete GitHub CI matrix green. Slice 1 is closed; Slice 2 (editor chrome: gutter, status bar, Go to Line/Column, invisibles) begins next per §17.
- 2026-09-24: Slice 2a landed (PR #124, four review rounds against real bugs, each fixed rather than merely re-documented). `EditorTextSystem.noteIncrementalEdit`/`rebuildLineIndex` (§6.6, new `EditorTextSystem+LineIndex.swift`) read `assistTextSource` (the live `NSTextStorage`-backed string) instead of `text`/`textView.string`, avoiding the O(document length) materialization the codebase's own `EditorTextSystem+EditingAssists.swift` already documents as "last resort only." `EditorGutterView` (new `NSRulerView` subclass) shipped with accessibility metadata (identifier `editorGutter`, role `.group`, a localized label) from the same slice per §12, and its viewport-bounded draw path (`EditorTextSystem+Gutter.swift`'s `enumerateVisibleLineFragments`) is now covered against a 10 MB document, not just a 50-line crash-safety smoke test. Three separate sites hit the same undo-manager-identity bug class — `EditorTextSystem.undoManager` resolves to a throwaway `fallbackUndoManager` until the text view is mounted in a real window, then switches identity to the window's real manager — in `EditorTextSystem`'s own observer, `EditorView`'s gutter-redraw observer, and (found by a hostile review of the fix itself) a test that could not have detected any of this because `dismantleNSView` never actually fires via SwiftUI's view-identity-switch teardown in a headless test process; the final test calls `dismantleNSView(_:coordinator:)` directly on the real `scrollView`/`coordinator`, which exercises the identical production code path deterministically. Also fixed: `updateNSView` recomputed gutter thickness before applying model text instead of after; `EditorTextSystem.init` scanned `initialText` twice (once directly, once again inside `setText`'s rebuild); and `EditorGutterView.updateThickness` measured only "0"'s glyph width, which could clip a wider digit in the proportional/display fonts the Editor settings' unrestricted font picker allows (fixed by measuring the widest of "0"-"9", regression-tested against "Bradley Hand"). Added a dedicated `<8 ms` gutter/caret-update benchmark isolating `EditorLineIndex.applying` + `updateThickness()` from TextKit layout, per §11's "Gutter/status caret update" row (a distinct, narrower budget than the existing 50 ms whole-keystroke tests); made it a best-of-5-trials minimum after one CI run flaked at 9.1 ms/op on shared runner hardware against a consistent ~5-6 ms/op locally — the 8 ms threshold itself was not weakened. Invisibles drew correctly for a from-scratch `(0, 0)`-origin full-window paint — the only scenario any test exercised — but silently drew nothing for an ordinary, genuinely scrolled dirty rect, meaning invisibles would have reliably failed to draw for almost any incremental/scrolled repaint in the shipped app. Fixed by converting `dirtyRect` to container space for the lookup/bounds check and back to view space for each marker's draw position (the x-coordinate had the identical gap, missing `inset.width`). - 2026-09-25: **Slice 3a landed (PR #128), with a real, disclosed architecture correction found during its own implementation.** `EditorTextSystem.selectionSet` (§6.9) is a computed property, backed by a `storedSelectionSet` cache that survives a read-after-write as long as AppKit's live `selectedRanges` still match it, falling back to a fresh from-AppKit reconstruction (`primaryIndex: 0`) once they diverge — `selectedRange` itself is unchanged, both stay consistent because both ultimately reflect the same live AppKit state. Escape (`cancelOperation:`) collapses a multi-selection to its primary range. Typing/delete/paste across multiple active selections route through one `EditorEditTransaction` (one undo group, one publication) via new `EditorTextView.insertText(_:replacementRange:)`/`deleteBackward(_:)`/`deleteForward(_:)`/`paste(_:)` overrides — **not** `NSTextViewDelegate`'s array-based `shouldChangeTextInRanges:replacementStrings:`, as §7.2's original diagram assumed: that delegate method was tried first and found, empirically against a real mounted `NSTextView`, to never be invoked by ordinary typing/backspace regardless of `selectedRanges.count`. §7.2 was corrected to record the actual mechanism.

  The more significant finding, directly probed with real AppKit calls rather than assumed: **`NSTextView.selectedRanges` cannot hold more than one simultaneous zero-length (bare-caret) range, via any known API** — confirmed with both the property setter and `setSelectedRanges(_:affinity:stillSelecting:)`, at any spacing, and even mixed one-for-one with a real selection. This means genuine multi-*caret* editing (Option-click to add a bare caret, Add/Remove Cursor Above/Below — Slice 3b's stated scope) cannot be built as a thin layer over `EditorSelectionSet`/`selectedRanges` the way this slice's own multi-*selection* work is; it needs custom caret state and drawing kept entirely outside AppKit's selection model, closer in kind to Slice 2c's own custom invisibles drawing than to anything in Slice 3a. This is recorded in §6.9 as a scoped architecture-correction flag for a design pass before Slice 3b's implementation begins, not silently redesigned around. Slice 3a itself ships exactly what is achievable and tested: multi-*selection* (real, non-zero-length, non-touching ranges) typing/delete/paste — exactly what Select-All-Occurrence (Slice 3c) will produce.

  Codex's review service showed no comments or reviews on this PR at any point (consistent with this epic's account-level quota exhaustion throughout); two independent fresh-context agent hostile reviews substituted for it. The first (against the initial implementation) found six genuine issues, all fixed: (1) a test asserted only the publication count and undo behavior for a 3-selection keystroke, never the actual post-insert text, so it would have passed even if only the primary selection were affected — added the missing assertion; (2) Escape/`cancelOperation:` had no IME guard unlike every sibling branch in `doCommandBy` — added `!textView.hasMarkedText()`; (3) `applyMultiCursorDeleteSelection()` trusted, rather than checked, its "every range in a multi-selection is non-empty" invariant — added an `allSatisfy` check, falling through to native handling if it's ever violated; (4) `applyMultiCursorTransaction` hardcoded the resulting selection's `primaryIndex` to `0` — threaded the real pre-edit `primaryIndex` through instead; (5) multi-selection paste was untested, and once a real, pasteboard-backed test was written (save/restore the developer's actual clipboard around the call), it failed: `NSTextView.paste(_:)` performs its edit as two separate `insertText` calls — delete the selection(s), then insert the pasted text — and the first call's own multi-range delete collapses the selection to one caret (per the finding above) before the second call ever runs, silently losing the paste at every selection but one; fixed with a dedicated `EditorTextView.paste(_:)` override that reads the pasteboard directly and routes it through `applyMultiCursorInsert(_:)` in one step, ahead of AppKit's own two-step sequence; (6) §7.2's diagram was stale, as described above. A second, focused re-review of that fix commit confirmed all six were genuinely resolved and found two further, minor, test-only issues in the new paste test's pasteboard backup — it saved only the first UTI type per pasteboard item (risking silent fidelity loss on restore for a multi-representation clipboard item) and called `writeObjects` once per saved item in a loop (each call replaces pasteboard contents, so only the last of several saved items would have survived) — both fixed by saving every type per item and restoring all items in one `writeObjects` call.

- 2026-09-25: **Slice 3b-i landed (PR #129), with a real correctness bug found and fixed mid-implementation, not just at review time.** `EditorTextView` gained a second custom-drawing pass painting a solid, non-blinking bar at every caret beyond the primary, using `NSTextInputClient.firstRect(forCharacterRange:actualRange:)` — confirmed empirically (a direct probe against this exact TextKit 2 stack) to return correct, already-converted view-space rects, avoiding a repeat of the hand-rolled paragraph-relative line-fragment math Slice 2c's invisibles pass needed. While implementing this, `EditorTextSystem.selectionSet`'s own Slice 3a getter/setter turned out to have a real, pre-existing gap: its staleness check discarded any caret AppKit's `selectedRanges` cannot represent on the very next read, meaning a bare secondary caret could never actually survive being cached at all. Fixed by having the setter verify its write against AppKit and, on a detected collapse, revert AppKit's visible state to just the primary while `storedSelectionSet` retains the full model.

  Codex's review service again showed no comments on this PR (consistent with this epic's account-level quota exhaustion throughout); two independent fresh-context agent hostile reviews substituted for it, plus a live follow-up from the first. The first review found the setter/getter fix above was itself incomplete: a purely reactive staleness check is not enough, since a LATER, completely unrelated selection change that happens to coincide with the cache's own primary (a click away and back, an outline jump-back, undo) would satisfy the check and wrongly resurrect a stale secondary caret with nothing to do with the user's current intent — `applyMultiCursorInsert` would then fan an ordinary keystroke out to that phantom location. Fixed with proactive invalidation: a new `isUpdatingSelectionSet` flag and `invalidateStoredSelectionSetIfStale()`, called from `EditorView.Coordinator.textViewDidChangeSelection` for every selection change the `selectionSet` setter did not itself post; `setText`/`replaceTextFromExternal` also now explicitly clear the cache, since a whole-document replacement must never resurrect offsets from a previous document. A second, focused re-review of that fix empirically confirmed it closes the hole (checked out the pre-fix commit, ran the new regression test against it, watched it fail; ran it again post-fix, watched it pass) but found one of the two new regression tests didn't actually exercise its own claimed fix (`documentReplacementClearsAStaleMultiSelectionCache` passed even with the fix removed, because `setText`'s own post-replacement caret never coincidentally matched the stale primary in the first place) — rewritten using `replaceTextFromExternal`'s explicit snapshot range to make the coincidence deterministic, and reconfirmed by removing the fix again and watching the corrected test genuinely fail.

- 2026-09-25: **Slice 3b-ii landed (PR #131).** `EditorTextSystem.toggleSecondaryCaret(at:)` adds a bare secondary caret alongside whatever selection is already active if none exists at that offset, removes one if it does, and never removes the last remaining caret. `EditorTextView.mouseDown(with:)` intercepts a plain (single-click) Option-click, resolves the click point via `NSTextInputClient.characterIndex(for:)`, and routes it through that method; anything else falls through to `super.mouseDown(_:)` unchanged, and not calling `super` for a handled Option-click means AppKit's own drag-tracking loop never starts, so Option-*drag* correctly does nothing until Slice 3b-iv.

  Codex's review service again showed no comments (consistent with this epic's account-level quota exhaustion throughout); an independent fresh-context hostile review substituted for it and found two genuine issues, both fixed: (1) `toggleSecondaryCaret(at:)` only checked for an existing bare caret at the exact offset — an Option-click landing *strictly inside* an existing real selection fell through to `addRange`, which `EditorSelectionSet.normalize`'s own overlap-merge rule then silently merged away (a genuine overlap, not a touch), consuming the click while doing nothing visible: no new caret, selection unchanged, none of the ordinary click behavior the user would otherwise get. Fixed by detecting this case and failing open to native click handling, leaving the boundary case (exactly at a selection's start/end, a genuine touch) unaffected. (2) The "plain click falls through to native handling" branch had no test coverage, since a real `mouseDown` call for a non-Option click was found (during this slice's own development) to hang indefinitely — `NSTextView.mouseDown(with:)`'s real implementation enters an internal modal event-tracking loop waiting for `mouseUp`/drag events a synthetic single event never supplies. Fixed by extracting the guard into a pure, testable `EditorTextView.isPlainOptionClick(_:)` predicate. A follow-up, focused re-review hand-traced the boundary-condition fix against `EditorSelectionSet.normalize`'s exact merge rule for every relevant offset (exactly at start/end, one past start, one before end, length-0 and length-1 ranges, mixed real-selection-plus-bare-caret scenarios) and confirmed no off-by-one in either direction, and confirmed the new tests weren't vacuous (they assert full range-array equality, not just `isMultiple`).

- 2026-09-25: **Slice 3b-ii-b landed (PR #132).** `EditorTextSystem.addCursorAbove()`/`addCursorBelow()` add a caret one line above/below the top-most/bottom-most existing caret, at the same column (clamped to that line's actual length via `EditorLineIndex`'s existing clamping convention), extending from the top-most/bottom-most caret rather than the primary — matching the "grow the column outward from its current edge" convention every real multi-cursor editor uses for repeated invocations. App-target wiring (`WindowCoordinator+MultiCursor.swift`, mirroring `WindowCoordinator+Editing.swift`'s Markdown/JSON command-bridge pattern but deliberately not format-restricted) adds the commands to the Format menu on `⌃⌘↑`/`⌃⌘↓` (Xcode's own convention for the same feature), gated on the editor text view being first responder. A background research pass confirmed neither this nor the alternative `⌥⌘↑`/`⌥⌘↓` binding collides with any existing shortcut in the app. Verified against the full local build matrix this PR's CI runs (package tests, app+CLI Debug/Release builds, 212/212 `MacDown2Tests`), not just the package suite.

  As a deliberate, disclosed scope decision (not a bug): the target column is recomputed fresh from the reference caret's current column on each call rather than carried as persistent "desired column" state across repeated invocations through an intermediate shorter line — genuine sticky-column tracking needs its own cache-invalidation discipline (mirroring Slice 3b-i's `storedSelectionSet` fix) that is separable complexity.

  Codex's review service again showed no comments; an independent fresh-context hostile review found one genuine bug and one disclosed-but-unfixed gap. The bug: `addVerticalCursor` used only `referenceRange.location`, never `.length` — when the top-most/bottom-most entry in `selectionSet` is a genuine multi-line selection rather than a bare caret (nothing gates this command to bare carets), the computed target point could land inside that same selection's span, and `EditorSelectionSet.normalize`'s own overlap-merge rule (the same class of bug PR #131's own review found once already) would silently absorb it — yet the method still unconditionally reported success. Fixed by checking the range count actually grew before returning `true`. A follow-up, focused re-review proved this fix is exact, not merely "usually correct": since the added range is always zero-length, absorbing it into an existing range can never extend that range's own end, so it can never bridge two other ranges together as a side effect — meaning the count can only ever be `unchanged` (absorbed) or `+1` (placed distinctly), with no possible false positive or false negative. The disclosed gap: `NSRange` carries no anchor/active-end distinction, so when the reference is a real selection, which end the user's caret was actually on cannot be recovered — `.location` (the lower bound) is used regardless, now stated plainly in the doc comment rather than left silent.

  PR #132 merged (squash) to `master` at `71bfe5f` with 314 package tests passing (304 pre-existing + 10 new in `EditorVerticalCursorTests.swift`, zero regressions) and CI green on the exact final SHA. Slice 3b-ii-b is closed. Remaining Slice 3b work — 3b-iii (synchronized plain-arrow-key caret movement) and 3b-iv (rectangular/Option-drag column selection) — continues next per §6.10/§17.

- 2026-09-25: **Slice 3b-iii landed (PR #133).** `EditorTextSystem.moveAllCaretsLeft/Right/Up/Down()` (`EditorTextSystem+SynchronizedMovement.swift`) synchronize every active caret across a plain, unmodified arrow-key press — explicitly scoped, per §6.10, to just these four selectors; word/line/paragraph jumps and any `AndModifySelection:` variant remain untouched and continue to move only the primary caret via native handling. Interception is via `EditorView.Coordinator.doCommandBy(_:)` (a direct empirical probe confirmed `NSTextView.doCommand(by:)` invokes the delegate first for movement selectors, and a delegate returning `false` correctly falls through to native movement — the originally-planned `shouldChangeTextIn`-based interception from an earlier draft of §7.2 was never viable, corrected there). Left/Right collapse a real selection to its near edge or move one composed character sequence (`NSString.rangeOfComposedCharacterSequence(at:)`); Up/Down collapse toward the direction of travel (start for up, end for down) and then move one line at the same column via `EditorLineIndex`, clamping rather than crashing at document boundaries. `handleSynchronizedMovement` was extracted to a new `EditorView+Coordinator+Movement.swift` (mirroring the established per-feature-file split, `EditorView+Coordinator+Selection.swift`) to keep `EditorView.swift`'s `doCommandBy` under the cyclomatic-complexity limit; `EditorView+Coordinator+Observers.swift` is a second, purely mechanical extraction for the same file's 400-line limit.

  Codex's review service remained at its account usage limit; an independent fresh-context hostile review found no P1s, one real P2, and two P3s. The P2: a genuine cross-slice consistency bug — `addVerticalCursor()` (already-merged, PR #132) anchored on a reference selection's `.location` for *both* `addCursorAbove` and `addCursorBelow`, while this slice's own `movedVertically()` anchors on `.location` for up but `.location + .length` for down ("collapse toward the direction of travel"), so "Add Cursor Below" and a synchronized Down-arrow press starting from the same real selection landed at visibly different columns — the same user-facing feature disagreeing with itself depending on which command triggered it. Fixed by changing `addVerticalCursor` to match `movedVertically`'s convention (start anchor for above, end anchor for below), which required touching already-merged Slice 3b-ii-b code as part of this PR rather than as a separate follow-up, per this epic's own "fix don't just flag" discipline. That fix broke PR #132's own regression test `addCursorBelowFailsClosedWhenTheTargetPointFallsInsideAnExistingRealSelection`; a geometric hand-trace (later independently re-derived and *strengthened* by the follow-up review below) proved the test's scenario is now structurally unreachable — under the end-anchored convention, every other range in `EditorSelectionSet` is necessarily confined to lines at or before the reference line, so the new target (one line past it) can never fall inside any existing range, not just the reference range itself. Replaced with `addCursorBelowFromARealSelectionAnchorsOnItsEndNotItsStart`, pinning the corrected behavior; the original `updated.count > selection.count` guard remains as defense in depth for a case this codebase can no longer construct, not because the guard itself was ever wrong. The two P3s — untested-but-verified-correct order-crossing (`upwardMovementCanReorderCaretsAndStillRelocatesThePrimaryCorrectly`, confirming `EditorSelectionSet.init(ranges:primaryIndex:)`'s `indexClosest` correctly relocates the primary when movement crosses caret order) and vertical-convergence (`convergingCaretsAfterVerticalMovementMergeIntoOne`) scenarios, and a doc comment overclaiming exact equivalence between horizontal "collapse and stop" and vertical "collapse then move one further line" semantics — were both addressed: two new tests added, the doc comment softened.

  Given the fix touched already-merged code and carried real cross-slice risk, a second, focused independent review of just that fix commit ran before merging (this epic's own established precedent for substantive fix rounds). It found no P1/P2 issues, and went further than the original hand-trace: it proved the "no other range can be positioned in the direction of travel from the reference" property holds for the *whole* selection set (not just adjacency to the reference range) by the sort/disjointness invariant alone, hand-re-verified every new test's arithmetic independently against `EditorLineIndex`'s actual column/offset conventions, and grepped the full tree confirming no other code depends on the old `.location`-for-both-directions behavior (all pre-existing `EditorVerticalCursorTests` cases use zero-length carets, for which the fix is a no-op by construction). It also noted, as an unrelated aside, the same pre-existing `TextFilterProcessLifecycleTests`/`TextFilterFourthPassRegressionTests` nondeterministic flakiness PR #128's own verification pass first surfaced — confirmed again as untouched by this diff, not investigated further here.

Slice 3b-iii is closed. Slice 3b-iv (rectangular/Option-drag column selection) — the last remaining piece of Slice 3b — begins next per §6.10/§17.

- 2026-09-25: **Slice 3b-iv landed (PR #135, originally opened as #134 — see the incident note below).** `EditorTextView.mouseDragged(with:)`/`mouseUp(with:)` (new) build, on an Option-drag past a 4-point activation threshold, one selection range per text LINE (an `NSTextLineFragment` — a wrapped continuation line counts as its own line here, matching the gutter/invisibles per-line-fragment iteration convention already established, not `EditorLineIndex`'s source-`\n`-delimited granularity) the drag rectangle vertically spans, each column-clamped to that line's own visible length, replacing `selectionSet` wholesale (`EditorTextSystem+RectangularSelection.swift`, new). Confirmed empirically, via two direct probes (a plain multi-paragraph document and a genuinely wrapped single paragraph) rather than assumed from documentation: `NSTextLineFragment`'s `characterRange`/`characterIndex(for:)`/`locationForCharacter(at:)` all operate in an index space local to the enclosing `NSTextLayoutFragment` (accumulating across wrapped continuation lines, not resetting per line), while each line's own point geometry resets to `x = 0` at that line's own left edge — and `characterIndex(for:)` clamps gracefully at a line's own boundaries for an out-of-range `x`, giving the required "column-clamped to line length" behavior with no additional manual clamping. A design bug was caught and fixed during this slice's own development, before anything shipped: an early draft's `mouseUp` unconditionally recomputed a degenerate one-point "rectangle" on every plain Option-click's own `mouseUp` (which always fires, even with zero mouse movement), silently discarding `mouseDown`'s own toggle-caret result the instant the button was released. Fixed with a `didDragRectangularSelection` flag plus the drag-activation threshold, so a stationary click's toggle survives. Also found empirically while writing tests: `NSTextView.mouseUp(with:)`'s real implementation, like the already-documented `mouseDown(with:)` case, can hang a headless test past its timeout against an isolated synthetic event with no live `NSApplication` event queue behind it — `mouseUp`'s own logic was extracted into `finishRectangularSelectionGesture(atViewPoint:)`, which never itself touches `super`, so tests exercise it directly instead of driving the real override.

  Codex's review service remained at its account usage limit; an independent fresh-context hostile review found no P1s, one real P2, and one P3. The P2: `EditorTextSystem.applyMultiCursorInsert(_:)` (already-merged Slice 3a code) was missing the same `allSatisfy`-non-empty-range guard `applyMultiCursorDeleteSelection()` already had — a gap that predates this slice but was only reachable in practice once this slice's own ragged rectangular selections (a spanned line shorter than the drag's own left edge produces a zero-length range) could put a real selection and a bare synthetic caret in the same `selectionSet` outside of Select-All-Occurrence's own always-non-empty output. The result: a ragged rectangle's typing behavior silently acted on every line while its delete behavior declined entirely and dropped every line but the primary — the identical selection behaving inconsistently between the two operations. Fixed by adding the matching guard to `applyMultiCursorInsert` (matching delete's already-correct, more conservative behavior, rather than teaching delete to intelligently handle a mix — disclosed as the lower-risk interim call, not a claim that ragged-rectangle typing/deleting is now fully supported), correcting that method's now-stale Slice-3a-era doc comment, and adding a regression test reproducing the exact ragged selection. A second, focused independent review of just that fix commit (this epic's own established precedent for substantive fix rounds touching already-merged code) found nothing further: it independently hand-traced the regression test's geometry, confirmed no other call site assumed the old permissive behavior, and confirmed the existing `EditorMultiSelectionTests` suite never exercised a mixed-length selection to begin with (by inspection, not just by the tests still passing). The P3 — the geometry's per-line X-origin math assumes every wrapped line's local `x = 0` coincides with its enclosing fragment's own `minX`, true today only because this codebase applies no paragraph styling beyond `lineHeightMultiple` anywhere — was documented as disclosed, current fragility a future hanging indent or RTL paragraph support would need to account for, not fixed (there is nothing to fix against today's actual styling).

No commits were actually lost — this session's own local checkout still held the correct `master` at `69454a5`, which was also already an ancestor of PR #134's still-live head branch on GitHub — and `master` was restored by force-pushing that local commit back to `origin/master` (confirmed via `git merge-base` before and after). PR #134 itself, however, was auto-closed by GitHub as a side effect and could not be reopened (`gh`'s own API returns "branch has no history in common with master," a stale snapshot from the moment of closure that does not clear even after the shared ancestor is restored and `git merge-base` proves otherwise) — refiled as PR #135 from the identical branch/commits, with a comment on #134 pointing to its replacement. - 2026-09-25: **Slice 3c landed (PR #138), closing all of Slice 3.** `EditorTextSystem.selectNextOccurrence()`/`selectAllOccurrences()` (new `EditorTextSystem+OccurrenceSelection.swift`) implement Sublime Text/VS Code's own Cmd-D convention, the only real precedent for this exact gesture, per J1's own literal walkthrough ("press once to select the word, repeat to reach all three occurrences" — the incremental, one-at-a-time convention, not an immediate multi-select on the first press): from a bare caret, select the word at/touching it (word-boundary detection reuses `MarkdownEditingAssistEngine`'s existing surrogate-pair-safe scalar helpers, same module, rather than a third duplicate of that logic); from an existing selection, find the next occurrence of its own literal text (case-sensitive substring match, not whole-word-restricted), wrapping to the document start, skipping anything already selected. Select All Occurrences shares the same text-to-match derivation but replaces the whole selection with every occurrence in one call.

  Also fixes the Cmd-D conflict §6.9 already documented as live: `keyFolderSelection` (`WindowCoordinator+FileTree.swift`) now returns `nil` whenever the editor's text view is the current first responder, via a new `canPerformOccurrenceSelection` (`WindowCoordinator+OccurrenceSelection.swift`, mirrors the already-shipped `canPerformCursorCommand` pattern exactly). Fixed at `keyFolderSelection`'s own definition rather than only at the "Duplicate" button's call site, since ALL THREE Folder commands read this one property: "Rename" (plain Return, colliding with the editor's own E10 list/blockquote-continuation assists) and "Move to Trash" (Cmd-Delete, colliding with `NSTextView`'s own delete-to-beginning-of-line binding) had the identical live conflict, just never separately reported — the Cmd-Delete one was found only by an independent hostile review, not by this slice's own initial tracing, and is disclosed honestly as a fix this slice's own commit message initially failed to call out. `WorkspaceCommands.swift`'s `.textFormatting` command group was extracted into `WorkspaceCommands+TextFormatting.swift` (mechanical split, no behavior change) to stay under swiftlint's type-body-length limit once the two new commands were added.

  Codex's review service remained at its account usage limit; an independent fresh-context hostile review of the initial commit found no P1s, one real P2, and one P2-disclosed side effect. The P2: `selectNextOccurrence()`'s original "find the next occurrence" step called the whole-document, non-overlapping `allOccurrenceRanges(of:in:)` tiling (built greedily from offset 0) and looked for the CURRENT PRIMARY's own range inside that precomputed list to decide "already selected, skip." For a MANUALLY selected (not word-selected) self-overlapping search string — the middle `"aa"` at offset 1 of the document `"aaaa"` — that tiling never reports `(1,2)` at all, only `(0,2)` and `(2,2)`; the primary silently failed to be recognized as already-selected, a different, genuinely OVERLAPPING range got added via `addRange(_:makePrimary:)`, and `EditorSelectionSet.normalize`'s own overlap-merge rule silently collapsed the result into one larger, no-longer-matching range while the method still reported success. Fixed by replacing the tiling-lookup approach with a `firstOccurrence(of:in:searchingFrom:notOverlapping:)` helper that scans directly from the primary's own end (never via a separately precomputed tiling the primary might not itself appear in) and skips on OVERLAP rather than exact match, advancing the scan by one UTF-16 unit past a skipped candidate (not past its own length) so a self-overlapping pattern is still searched correctly; if every remaining occurrence genuinely conflicts, as in the `"aaaa"` case, it now correctly declines instead of silently corrupting the selection. `allOccurrenceRanges(of:in:)` itself is unchanged and remains exactly right for `selectAllOccurrences()`, where a non-overlapping tiling is the only workable semantic — `EditorSelectionSet` cannot represent overlapping ranges under any circumstance. A second, focused independent review of just that fix commit found no P1s or P2s: it independently hand-traced the exact `"aaaa"` scenario plus an additional self-overlapping case of its own construction (`"ababa"` searching `"aba"`) to confirm the fix generalizes, confirmed the "advance by one" loop cannot pathologically degrade for realistic document sizes, and flagged two documentation-only nits (a stale parameter-name reference from the pre-rename version of the helper, and an overbroad claim that the ranges reaching `overlaps(_:_:)` are always non-zero-length — true only for the fresh search match, not for an existing zero-length caret from another cursor, though the formula is still correct for that case) plus one disclosed, non-corrupting edge case in `selectAllOccurrences()`'s own already-existing, unchanged primary-index derivation (a manually selected self-overlapping primary can end up replaced by a different, still-correct-for-the-search-text occurrence as the new primary) — all three addressed in a final doc-only commit, verified against the unchanged 359-test count.
