- 2026-09-25: Wrote the Slice 4 contract (§6.11) before implementation began, per a fresh baseline read against `master` at `645edc8`. Confirmed `LanguageEditingProfile` (§6.4) is real, tested, but consumed nowhere (repo-wide grep finds only its own declaration); none of the ten built-in transforms exist anywhere in the codebase; and the fenced-code/front-matter classifier has no reusable prior art — `MarkdownEngine/FrontMatter.swift`'s extractor is whole-document-only, and the `Diagrams*` package fence scanners all walk the debounced swift-markdown AST via `SourceMap`, both disqualified by E10's own binding "no full Markdown parse on the hot path" rule this classifier inherits. Confirmed `FileFormatRegistry` has 16 registered formats with no comment-syntax field anywhere, and no comment-delimiter table exists in `Highlighting`/`FileCore` to reuse — Slice 4 builds this from scratch. Split Slice 4 into 4a (`LanguageEditingProfileRegistry` + generalizing the editing-assist gate to every format), 4b (`FencedRegionClassifier` + gating Markdown-specific assists behind it, closing the E10 inherited-debt item), and 4c (the ten built-in transforms via `EditorEditTransaction`) — same "don't bundle differently-risky deliverables" reasoning already used for every prior multi-part slice — see §17. Slice 4a begins next.

- 2026-09-25: **Slice 4a landed (PR #140).** A new `LanguageEditingProfileRegistry` (`EditorCore`) holds one hand-authored `LanguageEditingProfile` per registered format (comment delimiters, paired-delimiter list, indent-after-trailing triggers) across all 16 formats, falling back to `.plainText` for anything unregistered. `EditingAssistConfiguration` gained `completesMarkdownDelimiters`, split out of the existing `completesMatchingCharacters` (kept under its original name — a persisted user setting — to avoid an unrelated settings-key migration): the old field now gates only general structural (bracket/quote) pairing, for every format; the new one gates Markdown's own `*`/`_`/backtick symmetric delimiters. `MarkdownEditingAssistEngine.outcome(...)` gained a required `profile: LanguageEditingProfile` parameter, threading `pairedDelimiters` into structural pairing/type-over/backspace (replacing the always-global `structuralPairs` table) and `defaultIndentWidth` into Tab/Shift-Tab's effective width. A new `generalNewlineOutcome` gives every non-Markdown format a genuinely new behavior: Return now maintains the current line's indentation, plus one further level via a profile's `indentAfterTrailing` set (e.g. `{` for C-family languages). `DocumentEditorSplitView`'s gate changed from "Markdown or disabled" to "every format gets a real, profile-driven configuration." Two purely mechanical extractions (`MarkdownEditingAssistEngine+Pairing.swift`, splitting `EditingAssistSafetyTests` into its own file) kept the main engine file and an existing test file under swiftlint's line-count limits.

  Codex's review service remained at its account usage limit; an independent fresh-context hostile review of the initial commit — briefed explicitly on the regression risk, since this slice touches already-shipped, production Markdown editing behavior, not just new surface — found no P2/P3s but one genuine **P1**: `newlineOutcome`'s Markdown-vs-general dispatch used `configuration.continuesMarkdownPrefixes` as its only signal. That flag is ALSO a real, persisted user preference ("Continue lists, quotes, and indentation on Return," `EditorSettingsPane.swift`) a Markdown user can turn off independently of their document's format — before this slice, turning it off made Return a pure no-op for Markdown, full stop. With only that one flag to dispatch on, a Markdown document with the preference off became indistinguishable from a genuinely non-Markdown document at the dispatch point, so it silently fell into the new `generalNewlineOutcome` and resurrected indentation-carrying for exactly the users who had explicitly asked to turn it off — a real, user-visible regression to already-shipped behavior that a normal test pass didn't surface, since the existing regression test's own fixture (`"- item"`, no leading whitespace) happened to pass either way. Fixed by adding a new, independent `EditingAssistConfiguration` field, `isMarkdownFormat`, set once by `assistConfiguration(from:isMarkdown:)` from the document's own format id and never re-derived from `continuesMarkdownPrefixes`; `newlineOutcome` now checks `isMarkdownFormat` first, restoring the preference's exact pre-slice contract while keeping the new general behavior correctly scoped to non-Markdown formats. Strengthened the existing test with an indented sibling and added a direct unit-level regression test reproducing the exact scenario. A second, focused independent review of just that fix commit found no P1s or P2s — it independently hand-traced all four `(isMarkdownFormat, continuesMarkdownPrefixes)` combinations, confirmed every `EditingAssistConfiguration` construction site in the test suite was unaffected by the new field's default value, verified the quoted Preferences label and the pre-slice `.passthrough` behavior directly against the pre-95cbdc8 code, and flagged one harmless doc-comment wording imprecision, fixed in a final doc-only commit.

  PR #140 merged (squash) to `master` at `3900498` with 376 package tests passing (360 pre-existing + 16 new across `EditingAssistGeneralFormatTests.swift`/the P1 fix's own regression tests, zero regressions) plus 214 `MacDown2Tests` app-target tests (212 pre-existing + 2 new, after fixing a compile break the app-target suite's own `assistConfiguration(from:)` call site had from the added `isMarkdown` parameter), verified against the full local build matrix, and CI green on the exact final SHA. Slice 4a is closed. Slice 4b (`FencedRegionClassifier` + gating Markdown-specific assists behind it, closing the E10 inherited-debt item) begins next per §6.11/§17.

- 2026-09-26: **Slice 4b landed (PR #141).** A new `FencedRegionClassifier` (`EditorCore`, §6.12) closes the E10 inherited-debt item named in §2.3: a bounded, synchronous, local scan that classifies a UTF-16 offset as `.prose`, `.frontMatter`, or `.fencedCode(languageID:)`, never touching `MarkdownEngine`/`SourceMap`. Front matter is detected by a small forward scan from the document's first line (capped at 1,000 lines); fenced-code detection is a backward parity scan from the caret's own line (capped at `maximumFenceLinesScanned`, see below), counting fence-delimiter lines crossed. Wired into `MarkdownEditingAssistEngine.outcome(for:...)` itself, immediately after the existing `isEnabled` guard: for a Markdown document whose caret sits inside a fence or front matter, an adjusted configuration (Markdown-specific flags forced off) and the fenced language's own `LanguageEditingProfile` (Slice 4a) are substituted for that one call only, never persisted. `.markdownCommand` (explicit Bold/Italic/Heading menu commands) is deliberately not gated by this classifier — a disclosed, different-interaction-model scope decision, not an oversight.

  An independent fresh-context hostile review of the initial commit (`703ef0f`) — after one retry past an Agent-side session rate limit (the first attempt failed with an HTTP 429; per this epic's own review-loop discipline, the correct response was to wait and retry for a genuine independent review, not substitute a self-review) — found no issues with the `.prose`-stays-unmodified and `.markdownCommand`-bypass invariants, correct parity counting for well-formed multi-fence documents, and correct 3-vs-4-space fence-indent boundary enforcement, but one genuine **P1** and two **P2**s. **P1:** `classify()`'s backward scan only ever counted fence-delimiter lines strictly BEFORE the caret's own line (starting from `currentLineStart - 1`), never checking whether the caret's OWN current line was itself a fence delimiter. A document ending in an unterminated closing fence with no trailing newline has its closing `` ``` `` line AS the caret's own line, so that closer was silently dropped from the parity count, leaving only the opener (odd) and wrongly reporting `.fencedCode` for a caret that had actually moved back into prose. Verified independently via a disposable probe test before fixing (confirmed the exact wrong output, then deleted the probe). Fixed by checking the caret's own current line for a fence delimiter it has moved past (`caret >= fence.markerEnd`) before the existing backward scan, which required promoting `fenceDelimiter`'s private return type from a tuple to a small `FenceDelimiter` struct (swiftlint's `large_tuple` rule rejected the resulting 3-member tuple) exposing that marker-end offset. **P2 #1:** a closing line's fence CHARACTER (backtick vs. tilde) was never checked against its opener's, unlike real CommonMark — disclosed in a doc comment and pinned by a new test rather than fixed with a full stack-based rewrite (judged low-impact; mixed-fence-character documents are rare). **P2 #2:** this classifier runs on every keystroke, and its cost at the (then 20,000-line) cap was never measured or tested; reduced the cap to 5,000 lines and added a near-cap performance-budget test, once the review's own measurement (~0.5ms/1,000 lines) showed 20,000 could reach ~10ms per keystroke in the pathological case.

  A second, focused independent review of just that fix commit (`c7864d9`) found no P1s — it independently hand-traced the fix against the exact regression fixture, confirmed the current-line check and the backward loop operate on disjoint line ranges (no double-count or skip), verified the tuple-to-struct refactor was behavior-preserving field-by-field, and confirmed the new near-cap performance fixture (4,900 filler lines, under the 5,000 cap) was not fragile the way an earlier `linesPastFence = 5000` attempt had been (that attempt used up the entire scan budget finding only the closing fence, before ever reaching the opener, and was caught and corrected during this same fix round before ever being committed). It surfaced four honest, non-blocking **P2** gaps instead: (1) an inaccurate claim already present in §6.12's own prose — "if the cap is reached, the scan gives up and reports `.prose`" — when the actual code applies the same parity-based decision regardless of whether the scan reached document start or merely hit the cap, proven via a constructed counter-example; (2) the P1 fix's own current-line check also correctly resolves two related, untested cases (a caret past a closing-fence marker on a line with more document after it, and an unterminated OPENING fence at end-of-document, which CommonMark says should extend to EOF); (3) the closing-fence-length CommonMark rule (a 4-backtick opener needs 4+ backticks to close, not just 3) was already disclosed in §6.12's own prose but missing from the source file's own doc comment; (4) an unused `character` field on the new struct (harmless, predates this fix). All four addressed in a doc/test-only follow-up commit (`43ebee2`): corrected §6.12's own cap-reached description above, extended the source doc comment's disclosure to cover fence length too, and added the two missing regression tests. No further review round was run for this doc/test-only commit — matching this epic's own established practice of scoping re-review effort to commits with actual behavior changes.

One CI `build-and-test` failure during this slice (`parentVanishedLatchSurvivesAChangedEventDuringDebounce`, `DocumentFileMonitorTests`/`DocumentFileMonitorRecoveryTests`) was diagnosed via `git diff --stat` showing zero file overlap with this slice's `EditorCore`-only diff, confirmed as a pre-existing, occasionally-flaking test independent of this work (master's own recent CI history shows the same test passing on most runs), and re-enqueued rather than treated as this slice's own failure. Slice 4b is closed. Slice 4c (the ten built-in transforms via `EditorEditTransaction`) begins next per §6.11/§17.

- 2026-09-26: Wrote Slice 4c's own design pass (§6.13) before implementation began, per §6.11's own deferred "whether 4c needs a further split is decided when it begins" note. Baseline read against `master` at `75b78e8` re-confirmed none of the ten transforms exist, but found `MarkdownEditingAssistEngine+Indentation.swift`'s existing per-line indent/outdent engine (`indentSelectedLines`/`unindentSelectedLines`/`transformSelectedLines`) is reachable only through the single-selection E10 assist dispatch — so "indent-adjust, multi-cursor aware" is a new command reusing that same logic once per active selection, not a from-scratch algorithm — and confirmed comment-toggle's two stated dependencies (4a's `LanguageEditingProfile`, 4b's `FencedRegionClassifier`) are both already landed. Fixed the exact per-transform scope (line-block computation, the multi-selection conflict/merge rule for the four line-reordering transforms, selection-requirement rules for Sort/Dedupe/case conversion, Trim Trailing Whitespace's deliberate whole-document scope, comment-toggle's fence-aware profile resolution) and the full command list with shortcuts, checked against every existing `keyboardShortcut` declaration for conflicts before picking them. Split Slice 4c into 4c-i (Duplicate/Delete/Move Up/Move Down/Join Lines — share the line-block merge rule), 4c-ii (Sort/Dedupe/Trim/Convert Case/Indent — simpler, independent-per-selection shape), and 4c-iii (Toggle Comment — smallest, most self-contained, own dependency) — see §17. Slice 4c-i begins next.

- 2026-09-26: **Slice 4c-i landed (PR #142).** New `EditorLineTransforms` (`EditorCore`) implements Duplicate Line, Delete Line, Move Line Up, Move Line Down, and Join Lines as pure, `NSTextView`-independent logic operating on `NSString`/`EditorLineIndex`/`EditorSelectionSet` — the `MarkdownEditingAssistEngine` precedent applied to whole-line commands. Each active selection's own touched line-block is computed, then blocks that overlap or are merely adjacent are merged into one group before any transform runs (§6.13's own multi-selection conflict rule), producing N `TextReplacement`s in one `EditorEditTransaction` — the same shape Slice 3a's own multi-selection typing/delete already established. Move Up/Down swap the block with the single adjacent line via one pure content rearrangement over their combined span. Join Lines' single-line groups implicitly extend to include the next line (Ctrl-J's own Xcode/BBEdit convention), re-merging afterward since extension can create new overlaps. Delete Line swallows the preceding line's own terminator when deleting the document's last line, so no dangling blank line is left behind. All four line-reordering transforms are all-or-nothing across multiple active selections at a boundary (Move Up at document start, Move Down at document end), matching this codebase's established "the whole command disables/no-ops rather than silently half-applying" precedent. `EditorTextSystem+LineTransforms.swift` adapts this to the live text system; `WindowCoordinator+LineTransforms.swift`/`WorkspaceCommands+LineTransforms.swift` add a new "Lines" menu (⌘⇧D/⌘⇧K/⌥⌘↑/⌥⌘↓/⌃J), not format-restricted, checked against every existing `keyboardShortcut` declaration for conflicts.

  An independent fresh-context hostile review of the initial commit (`4e17fdb`) found no issues with the offset/delta arithmetic across multiple simultaneous groups (hand-traced for non-adjacent 2-group Duplicate/Delete/Move), the merge rule's own chain-merging of 3+ selections regardless of input order, the 3-consecutive-carets Join scenario, the all-or-nothing Move boundary semantics, or the app-level command bridge/shortcut wiring, but one genuine **P1** and three **P2**s. **P1:** Move Line Up/Down's `swapReplacement` rejoined bare line content with a literal `"\n"`, silently downgrading a CRLF or bare-CR document's own line endings to LF within the swapped span — confirmed as real, silent document corruption for any non-LF-terminated file, with zero test coverage for that class of input anywhere in the slice. Fixed by preserving every terminator inside the swapped span verbatim via a new shared `terminatorText(afterLine:)` helper: the block's own internal terminators never change position and are copied through unchanged, and only the ONE terminator between the block and the adjacent line relocates to the opposite side, with its own exact text preserved. **P2s:** Duplicate Line's synthesized last-line separator had the same hardcoded-`"\n"` root cause on a narrower path, fixed to reuse the terminator already used before the block; Duplicate Line was not a no-op on a truly empty document unlike its four siblings, fixed for consistency; Join Lines' post-extension re-merge can combine two carets separated by exactly one unclaimed line into a single whole-span join rather than two independent joins — a faithful, uniformly-applied consequence of the merge rule, disclosed via a doc comment and a pinning test rather than changed.

  A second, focused independent review of just that fix commit (`f281463`) found no P1s — it hand-traced the terminator-preservation fix against a 5-line CRLF document moved both directions, confirmed `terminatorText`'s own offset math is correct for all three terminator kinds, and confirmed the pre-existing LF-only Move tests still test what they originally claimed. It flagged four honest, non-blocking gaps: Move Line Down across a CRLF boundary with a genuine multi-line block had no test (exactly the direction most likely to expose a residual off-by-one in the fix's own length-aware caret math); Duplicate Line of a multi-line last block under CRLF had no test; bare-CR Move Line Down had no test; and `terminatorText(afterLine:)` enforced its own "not the document's last line" precondition only in a doc comment, not in code. All four addressed in a doc/test-only follow-up commit (`e798c3c`): added the three missing regression tests (including a resulting-selection assertion, not just resulting text) and an explicit `precondition`.

  PR #142 merged (squash) to `master` at `2adc37a` with 448 `EditorCoreTests` passing (438 at the initial commit + 10 new across both fix rounds: 7 adapter-integration tests plus 3 CRLF/CR-coverage tests from the second review round; zero regressions) and 214 `MacDown2Tests` app-target tests unaffected (confirmed via a full `-only-testing:MacDown2Tests` run against the initial commit), CI green on the exact final SHA. Slice 4c-i is closed. Slice 4c-ii (Sort/Dedupe/Trim Trailing Whitespace/Convert Case/Increase-Decrease Indent) begins next per §6.13/§17.

- 2026-09-26: **Slice 4c-ii landed (PR #143).** New `EditorTextTransforms` (`EditorCore`) implements Sort Lines, Dedupe Lines, and Trim Trailing Whitespace, reusing `EditorLineTransforms`' own line-block merging (Sort/Dedupe need the identical "expanding a selection to whole lines can make two disjoint selections overlap" handling Duplicate/Delete already solved, Slice 4c-i) and its `terminatorText(afterLine:)` helper, applying that slice's own CRLF/CR-preservation discipline from the start rather than repeating the bug class: Sort reuses terminators POSITIONALLY (reordering content never changes the separator structure between positions), Dedupe reuses each surviving line's own real terminator, correctly dropping the trailing separator when the block's own original last line is itself removed as a duplicate. Trim Trailing Whitespace is deliberately NOT selection-scoped (a one-shot whole-document cleanup, matching BBEdit/Sublime's own convention). Convert Case (`EditorTextTransforms+Case.swift`) requires every active selection to be real and non-empty, computing each conversion's own UTF-16 length rather than assuming it matches the original (e.g. German "straße" uppercases to "STRASSE", 6 UTF-16 units to 7). App-level wiring extends the SAME "Lines" `CommandMenu` Slice 4c-i already created rather than each sub-slice getting its own file (two identically-named `CommandMenu`s produce two separate sibling menus in the real menu bar, not a merge — the one deliberate exception to this epic's usual per-sub-slice file-split convention); new shortcuts ⌘]/⌘[ for Increase/Decrease Indent, checked against every existing `keyboardShortcut` declaration for conflicts; Sort/Dedupe/Trim/Convert Case are menu-only.

  An independent fresh-context hostile review of the initial commit (`d87de3b`) confirmed the CRLF/CR-preservation discipline was genuinely carried through (no hardcoded `"\n"` anywhere in the new Sort/Dedupe code, verified by hand-tracing a mixed-terminator scenario and a triplicated-duplicate-line fixture), confirmed the shared line-block-merge/overlap-safety reasoning, and confirmed Convert Case's delta accumulation across multiple length-changing selections — but found one genuine **P1** in Increase/Decrease Indent (`EditorTextTransforms+Indent.swift`): generalizing Tab/Shift-Tab's existing per-line indent engine to multi-selection fed each merged group's WHOLE line-block range into `MarkdownEditingAssistEngine.indentSelectedLines`/`unindentSelectedLines` (needed for a correct text edit) but then also propagated THEIR OWN `resultingSelection` — necessarily the whole block, since that's the range they were given — straight through to every member, so even the ordinary single-caret, single-line case left the ENTIRE re-indented line selected instead of a collapsed caret: the very next keystroke after ⌘]/⌘[ would have replaced the whole line. Confirmed end-to-end against a real mounted `NSTextView`. No test in the initial commit asserted on the resulting selection for this command, only the resulting text — the exact gap that let it through.

  Fixed (`2c3e191`) by reimplementing the per-line transform inline (duplicating `indentSelectedLines`/`unindentSelectedLines`'s own tiny closures rather than calling them), so each GROUP MEMBER's own original position could be remapped independently via `MarkdownEditingAssistEngine.remappedSelection` — an existing, unmodified, internal primitive — rather than reusing the group's own single whole-block result for every member. While writing this fix, a second, closely related bug was found and fixed in the FIRST DRAFT of the fix itself, before ever being committed: `remappedSelection` returns a position RELATIVE to its own `rangeLocation` parameter, not an absolute document offset (confirmed by a direct probe against the real, already-shipped `MarkdownEditingAssistEngine.outcome` path — a selection starting at document offset 4 produced a `resultingSelection` of `{0, 13}`, not a value based at offset 4) — the first draft added only the cross-group `delta` accumulator and forgot to add the group's own `groupRange.location` back, caught by hand-tracing the fix's own test expectations against the real remap arithmetic before trusting them, rather than after. This investigation also surfaced a THIRD, genuinely separate, pre-existing bug in already-shipped single-selection Tab/Shift-Tab (the same `remappedSelection` helper, used via `transformSelectedLines`, has the identical missing-offset defect for any multi-line selection not starting at document offset 0 — masked in every existing test by every one of them happening to use a selection starting at offset 0) — explicitly out of scope for this PR, disclosed here rather than silently fixed inline, and flagged via a spawned follow-up task for its own dedicated fix/test/review cycle rather than scope-creeping into this slice.

  A second, focused independent review of just the fix commit (`2c3e191`) found no P1s or P2s — it hand-traced all three required multi-selection scenarios (a bare caret not at document start, two disjoint one-line groups where the second doesn't start at offset 0, two carets merging into one multi-line group) against the real `remappedLocation` algorithm directly rather than trusting the fix's own test comments, confirmed the `IndentContext`/`IndentAccumulator`/`applyIndent` extraction (needed to stay under swiftlint's function-body-length and parameter-count limits) preserved behavior exactly, confirmed `MarkdownEditingAssistEngine+Indentation.swift` was restored to be byte-identical to master (the earlier access-widening fully reverted, no longer needed once the fix stopped calling those two functions), and additionally wrote and ran a throwaway test covering a 3-group decrease-indent case with a genuine no-op sandwiched between two real edits, confirming the delta accumulator survives an untouched middle group correctly.

  PR #143 merged (squash) to `master` at `f5ea015` with 486 `EditorCoreTests` passing (484 at the initial commit + 2 new regression tests pinning the collapsed-caret result from the fix round; zero regressions), CI green on the exact final SHA, app target build confirmed clean by both review rounds. Slice 4c-ii is closed. Slice 4c-iii (Toggle Comment, depending on 4a/4b) begins next per §6.13/§17 — the last piece of Slice 4c.

- 2026-09-26: **Slice 4c-iii landed (PR #144) — the tenth and final built-in transform, closing all of Slice 4c.** New `EditorCommentToggle` (`EditorCore`) implements Toggle Comment: "smart toggle" semantics (VSCode/Sublime/Xcode's shared convention), reusing `EditorLineTransforms`' own line-block merging and `makeTransaction`. A profile with no `lineComment` (Markdown, HTML, XML, CSS in this codebase's own registry — a real, common case for this app specifically, not a rare edge case, since Markdown prose has no line-comment syntax of its own) falls back to wrapping the whole block once with `blockComment`'s own delimiters. A profile with neither declines the whole command, including for a mixed merged-group selection. Inside Markdown, the effective profile is resolved per merged group via each group's own anchor line, mirroring `MarkdownEditingAssistEngine`'s own `effectiveConfigurationAndProfile` (§6.12): the fenced language's own profile inside a fence, `.plainText` inside front matter, Markdown's own block-comment fallback in prose. App-level wiring adds "Toggle Comment" to the existing "Lines" menu on ⌘/.

  This PR went through three independent review rounds, each finding one genuine issue — matching the exact track record of both sibling PRs in this slice (#142's CRLF corruption, #143's full-line-selection bug). **Round 1** (initial commit `b353163`) found a **P1**: `toggleOutcome`'s line-comment branch reused `MarkdownEditingAssistEngine.remappedSelection` directly, whose own "at column 0, unaffected" special case is correct for Indent (which edits at column 0) but wrong here, since the comment prefix is inserted/removed AFTER each line's own leading whitespace — any caret sitting strictly inside a line's indentation (an entirely ordinary position) got the whole line's delta applied, teleporting it past the prefix instead of leaving it untouched. The same round found two P2s: blank lines were folded into the "already commented?" decision, so a block fully commented except for one blank line wrongly registered as "not all commented" and got double-commented instead of uncommented; and a wide selection spanning from inside a fence through its own closing delimiter into surrounding prose comments the closing fence line itself, corrupting it as a fence marker — disclosed as an accepted consequence of the design contract's own "scope to the anchor line's profile" rule, not a bug.

  **Fixed in `bf24f00`**: gave comment-toggle its own remap (`remappedCommentTogglePosition`/`remappedCommentToggleSelection`), aware of each line's own real insertion column rather than assuming column 0; excluded blank lines from the "already commented?" decision while still leaving them untouched by the action itself; disclosed the fence-boundary consequence via a doc comment and a pinning test. `lineCommentToggle`'s return type became a small `LineCommentToggleResult` struct (swiftlint's `large_tuple` rule rejected the resulting 3-member tuple).

  **Round 2** (re-review of `bf24f00`) found no P1s — independently hand-traced the boundary case, the leading-whitespace-interior case, the after-prefix case, and a multi-line group with different per-line indentation, confirming the fix's own test expectations were correct, not just passing — but found one new **P2**: the new remap function didn't clamp a position that fell STRICTLY INSIDE a just-removed comment prefix (e.g. a caret between the two `/` characters of `//` at the moment of uncommenting) — it applied the removal's full negative delta uniformly, landing the caret BEFORE the line's own leading whitespace, further from correct than doing nothing. Confirmed not a regression from `bf24f00` — the identical defect already existed, dormant for this feature, in the reused `MarkdownEditingAssistEngine.remappedLocation` primitive before `bf24f00` stopped calling it.

  **Fixed in `410ef4b`**: clamped a position inside the removed span to the insertion point itself. Also replaced a test whose name/doc comment claimed to verify "a second-line caret through both lines' own deltas" but never actually asserted on the resulting selection (only text) — matching this exact PR's own recurring theme that missing `.selection` assertions are what let each of these bugs through undetected.

  **Round 3** (final re-review of `410ef4b`) found no P1s or P2s of substance — independently re-derived the clamp arithmetic algebraically across every boundary (exactly at the insertion column, exactly at the end of the removed span, one unit inside it, with and without a trailing space after the prefix), confirmed the positive-delta/commenting path is untouched by the new clamp branch, and confirmed the new two-caret merge test's expected values by hand-trace. It noted one purely cosmetic inaccuracy (the fix commit's own message said an old test was "replaced" when it was actually left in place alongside the new one — harmless redundant coverage, not wrong coverage, corrected here rather than via a history-rewriting amend) — verdict "ready to merge as-is."

  PR #144 merged (squash) to `master` at `f58147c` with 510 `EditorCoreTests` passing (501 at the initial commit + 9 new across all three fix rounds: 7 from round 1's fix, 2 from round 2's fix; zero regressions), CI green on the exact final SHA, app target build confirmed clean across all three review rounds. **Slice 4c is now closed in its entirety — all ten built-in transforms (Duplicate/Delete/Move Up/Move Down/Join Lines, Sort/Dedupe/Trim Trailing Whitespace/Convert Case/Increase-Decrease Indent, Toggle Comment) are implemented, tested, and merged.** Slice 5 (TextSearch + current-document search) begins next per §17's own dependency order.

- 2026-09-26: Wrote Slice 5's own design pass (§6.14) before implementation began. Baseline read confirmed `TextSearchEngine.matches` (from Slice 1) was already fully implemented and tested but had zero consumers anywhere in the app target — current-document Find was, and until this slice remained, 100% stock `NSTextFinder`, with `EditorFind.swift` a thin, unused wrapper around `performTextFinderAction:`. Fixed the architecture: an inline docked Find bar (deliberately departing from Go to Line/Command Palette's own floating-`NSPanel` precedent, since Find is used far more frequently and for far longer per invocation than either), a new `EditorFindModel` holding the current-match/navigation state `TextSearchEngine` itself deliberately doesn't (first real consumer of `SearchOptions.wraps`), and viewport-bounded match highlighting following the invisibles drawing pattern rather than secondary carets', since match count is unbounded. Split into 5a (the bar itself: live matching, Find Next/Previous, highlighting), 5b (Replace/Replace All via `EditorEditTransaction`), and 5c (Select All Matches via `EditorSelectionSet`, search-selection-scope). Slice 5a begins next.

- 2026-09-26: **Slice 5a landed (PR #146) — the current-document Find bar.** New `EditorFindModel`/`EditorFindModelStore` (`EditorCore`) hold pure find/navigate state per tab identity, mirroring `EditorTextSystemStore`'s own per-identity caching exactly. New `FindBarView` (app target): an inline bar docked above the editor pane, not a floating panel, per §6.14's own design decision — query field, case/whole-word/regex toggles, match count/error display, Find Next/Previous, Escape to close. Viewport-bounded match highlighting (`EditorTextView+FindHighlights.swift`) follows the exact same bounded `enumerateTextLayoutFragments` walk and `textContainerInset` coordinate conversion the invisibles-drawing pass already established (and was hostile-review-corrected for in PR #127); `EditorTextSystem.setFindHighlights(ranges:currentIndex:)` forwards to it. `drawSecondaryCarets` was also extracted into its own `EditorTextView+SecondaryCarets.swift` file, purely to keep `EditorTextView.swift` under the file-length lint budget after adding the highlighting pass — no behavior change. Wired `findStore` through `WindowController` → `WorkspaceShellView` → `ContentAreaView` → `DocumentEditorSplitView`, plus `WindowCoordinator.toggleFind()` and a "Find in Document…" menu command bound to **⌃F**, deliberately not ⌘F: AppKit's own stock Find/Find Next/Find Previous/spelling menu (`.textEditing`) is left untouched for this slice, matching "Go to Line/Column…"'s own ⌃G precedent rather than risk breaking the unrelated spelling/substitutions items `.textEditing` also covers — replacing it outright is a separate decision left for a future slice.

  An independent fresh-context hostile review of the initial two-commit PR found no issues with `EditorFindModel`'s `advance(by:)`/`nearestIndex`/`updateMatches` branch logic (hand-traced against all 16 initial tests), the highlight-forwarding no-op-on-no-change guard, the `toggleFind()`-vs-the-bar's-own-close-button symmetry, or `EditorFindModelStore` eviction (confirmed to use the exact same `evictAll()`-on-window-close convention every other per-tab store in this app already uses, not a regression) — but found one genuine **P1** and two lesser issues. **P1:** `EditorFindModel.updateMatches` called `TextSearchEngine.matches` synchronously on `@MainActor`. A catastrophic-backtracking regex query froze the ENTIRE app, not just the Find bar, with no recovery short of force-quit — exactly the adversarial case §15 lists by name ("a deliberately slow/catastrophic-backtracking regex, cancellation must actually free the main actor") and directly contradicting this document's own §8 commitment for this exact feature ("runs off-main... with a query-generation counter so a stale query's results are never published over a newer one's"), neither of which existed anywhere in the initial commits. **P2s (not bugs, but real gaps):** `FindBarView` searched its own SwiftUI `text` prop rather than the live `EditorTextSystem.text`, the same staleness class this codebase already hit and fixed for the status bar (`DocumentEditorSplitView+EditorPane.swift`'s own `EditorStatusBarView` comment) — narrow (only reachable during an external file reload with Find open) but structurally the exact pattern already flagged as risky; and the highlight-drawing pass rescanned the ENTIRE match array per visible line fragment (O(visible lines × total match count)), a real, unaddressed performance gap for exactly the "match count is unbounded" risk the code's own doc comment already named without actually mitigating.

  **Fixed:** `updateMatches` is now `async`, running the actual `TextSearchEngine.matches` call inside a `Task.detached`, with a `searchGeneration` counter so a slower, now-superseded call's result is discarded rather than published over a newer call's — this does not stop a pathological regex's own computation (`NSRegularExpression` has no cancellation hook mid-call) but does keep the main actor and UI free while it runs, the literal requirement. Added `SearchQueryError: Sendable` (`TextSearch`) so it can cross the `Task.detached` boundary — a Swift 6 strict-concurrency compile requirement, not just a style choice, so the successful build is itself evidence the fix is real. Added an `isSearching` flag surfaced as a small `ProgressView` in the bar. `FindBarView` now takes a `resolvedText: () -> String` closure (`editorStore.existingSystem(for:)?.text`) used only when actually searching, keeping its own `text` prop as nothing more than the `.onChange` reactive trigger. The highlight scan became a two-pointer walk: since `TextSearchEngine.matches` always returns matches in ascending document order and the fragment enumeration is also top-to-bottom, a single forward-only cursor shared across every visible line finds each line's own overlapping matches in amortized O(1) rather than rescanning the whole array. All 16 existing `EditorFindModelTests` (plus the one call site in `EditorFindModelStoreTests`) converted to `async`/`await`; 2 new tests added.

  A second, focused independent review of just the fix commit found all three fixes genuinely correct, not merely plausible-looking: confirmed the `Task.detached` closure captures only value types (never `self`), hand-traced the generation-counter guard across multiple out-of-order completion interleavings and found no path where a stale result could win, confirmed the `Sendable` conformance is compiler-enforced (this package's Swift 6.2 tools version means an unsendable type crossing the `Task.detached` boundary would fail to *compile*, not just misbehave), hand-traced the two-pointer highlight scan against a constructed 3-line-fragment example with a match spanning two fragments and confirmed no skipped or duplicated draws, and independently re-ran `swift test --filter EditorCoreTests` itself rather than trusting the commit message's claim — confirming 538 tests passing. It noted one trivial, non-blocking commit-message wording inaccuracy (claimed "both call sites" in a test file that has only one) not worth a fixup commit for.

  PR #146 merged (squash) to `master` at `69581e4` with 538 `EditorCoreTests` passing (510 at Slice 4c's close + 28 new: 16 `EditorFindModelTests` initially, growing to 18 after the fix round, plus 7 `EditorFindModelStoreTests` and 3 `EditorTextViewFindHighlightTests`) and the full 214-test `MacDown2Tests` app-target suite passing, both confirmed clean before and after the fix round. **Slice 5a is closed.** Slice 5b (Replace/Replace All via `EditorEditTransaction`) begins next per §6.14/§17.

- 2026-09-26: **Slice 5b landed (PR #149) — Replace and Replace All.** `EditorFindModel` gained `replacementText` and two pure transaction builders, `replaceCurrentTransaction(with:)`/`replaceAllTransaction(with:)`, both routing through the existing `EditorEditTransaction` unchanged per §6.14's own design ("N == 1 is a valid, and encouraged, use of this type") — Replace All builds exactly one transaction covering every match (§4 invariant #10), never N separate ones, using the pre-existing `resultingCaretRanges(for:)` to compute the resulting caret. `FindBarView` gained a second row (replace field, Replace/Replace All buttons); `DocumentEditorSplitView+EditorPane.swift`'s new `applyFindReplacement` applies the transaction to the live text system and re-runs the search against the post-edit text. An independent review found one real gap — Replace/Replace All were gated on `matchCount`/`currentMatch` but not on `isSearching`, so firing Replace while a previous async search was still resolving could apply a transaction built from stale match positions — fixed by gating both the buttons and the underlying methods (not just the buttons, since Return in the replace field bypasses `.disabled`) on `!model.isSearching`. 9 new `EditorFindModelReplaceTests`; `EditorCoreTests` reached 547. **Slice 5b is closed.**

  **Aside, discovered during this slice's own CI investigation, not part of Slice 5b's own scope:** a local `MacDown2Tests` red run prompted a full investigation (owner-directed, since an earlier characterization of it as "known flakiness" lacked adequate evidence) that root-caused two distinct, previously-only-worked-around classes of chronic test flakiness — tracked and fixed as issue #150/PR #151, entirely orthogonal to Find/Replace's own code. See #150's own comment thread for the full investigation and `RELEASE_HARDENING.md` §12 for the resulting durable test-evidence policy. Slice 5c (Select All Matches, consuming `searchesSelectionOnly`) begins next per §6.14/§17.

- 2026-09-26: **Slice 5c landed (PR #152) — Select All Matches and search-in-selection, closing Slice 5 in full.** `EditorFindModel.selectionSetForAllMatches` is a pure conversion of the current match list into an `EditorSelectionSet` via `EditorSelectionSet(ranges:primaryIndex:)` directly — deliberately **not** `EditorTextSystem.selectAllOccurrences()`, a different, already-shipped Slice 3c feature, per §6.14's own explicit instruction. `updateMatches(in:selection:preferringLocationNear:)` gained a `selection` parameter implementing `SearchOptions.searchesSelectionOnly` — the first real consumer of that field anywhere in this codebase — falling back to whole-document search for a `nil` selection or a bare caret. `FindBarView` gained a "Sel" toggle and a "Select All Matches" button; `DocumentEditorSplitView+EditorPane.swift`'s new `applySelectAllMatches` installs the selection via `system.selectionSet =` and scrolls the primary match into view via `scrollToVisible(utf16Range:)`, deliberately not `revealSelection(utf16Range:)`, which collapses to a single range internally and would destroy the multi-selection just installed.

  An independent hostile review of the initial implementation found one real, empirically-reproduced bug and two minor issues. **The bug:** search-in-selection sliced the live text down to just the selection's own substring and searched within that substring, then offset results back to document coordinates — but `TextSearchEngine`'s whole-word boundary check only ever examines characters inside whatever string it is given, so a match truncated exactly at the selection's own edge could be wrongly reported as word-bounded. Reproduced concretely: text `"precat and cat"`, a selection starting right after `"pre"` (its own substring reading `"cat and cat"`), whole-word search for `"cat"` wrongly returned 2 matches instead of the true 1. Fixed by searching the FULL text first (so the whole-word check always sees true document context) and filtering the resulting matches down to only those fully contained within the clamped selection range, eliminating the substring-extraction/offset-remapping path entirely — immune to this bug class by construction, not merely patched around it. A regression test pins the reviewer's own repro case. The two minor issues: a misleading test name for `selectionSetForAllMatches`'s defensive, in-practice-unreachable `currentIndex ?? 0` fallback (documented and the test renamed to describe what it actually verifies), and a pre-clamp ordering edge case rendered moot by the same redesign. A second, focused independent review of just the fix commits confirmed both correct by hand-tracing the filter predicate against the repro case and several constructed boundary scenarios (a match straddling either edge of the selection, an empty selection, a selection exactly equal to one match), verified the `currentIndex`-non-nil invariant claim by tracing every mutation path, and re-ran the full suite itself rather than trusting the commit message.

  PR #152 merged (squash) to `master` with `EditorFindModelSelectionTests` (13 tests, including the regression test) and the full 560-test `EditorCoreTests` suite passing, swiftlint/swiftformat clean. **Slice 5 is now closed in its entirety — Find, Replace/Replace All, and Select All Matches/search-in-selection are all implemented, tested, and merged.**

  **Aside, discovered while landing this slice, not part of Slice 5c's own scope:** PR #152's CI run failed once on `replacementWatcherProbesAReappearedFileWithoutAnotherEvent()` in `DocumentFileMonitorRecoveryTests.swift` — an area this PR's diff never touched. Investigated per `RELEASE_HARDENING.md` §12 rather than waved off: a CI rerun of the identical job passed cleanly, the test passed 5/5 and 8/8 in isolated local repro attempts, and `waitUntil`'s own doc comment (added during #150's own investigation) already named this exact test as independently documented flaking in `planning/issue-57-findings.md` over a month before #150, with #150's own timeout widening (2s → 10s) recorded at the time as a partial mitigation, not a fix. Root-caused: `DocumentFileMonitor.emit(_:generation:sequence:)` calls each test's observation callback synchronously from within its own actor-serialized execution — already correctly ordered — but the affected tests' own recorder types were `actor`s, forcing each callback to spawn `Task { await recorder.append(observation) }`, an independently-scheduled unstructured task per observation with no ordering guarantee under scheduler contention. Fixed by converting every affected recorder to a lock-based `@unchecked Sendable` class (matching this suite's own already-correct `HealthRecorder`), repairing the whole class of usages across the `DocumentFileMonitor` test family, not just the one name already caught flaking. Tracked and fixed as issue #153/PR #154, entirely orthogonal to Find/Select's own code. Slice 6 (Workspace index + Quick Open + recents) begins next per §17.

- 2026-09-27: Wrote Slice 6's own design pass (§6.15) before implementation began, per a fresh baseline read against `master` at `fdbb783e`. Corrected §17's own stale Slice 6 goal line: `WorkspaceFileIndex` and `FuzzyPathScore` (both Slice 1, PR #123) are already fully implemented and tested, not new work this slice builds from scratch — confirmed by reading `WorkspaceFileIndex.swift`/`FuzzyPathScoreTests.swift` directly and finding recursive indexing, symlink-loop safety, and fuzzy ranking all already covered. What is genuinely missing: `WorkspaceFileIndex` has no wiring to `FileTreeModel.root` (an already-committed §2.1 baseline item never closed), no owning store anywhere in the app target, and no 100k-path performance test despite §11/§17 already committing to that budget. Decided the open architecture questions the baseline research surfaced: one `WorkspaceFileIndex` instance per window (mirroring `FileTreeModel`'s own per-window scope, kept warm via rebuild-on-root-change, not lazily built on first Quick Open) rather than a single app-wide instance like `RecentFolderRoots`; `QuickOpenPanel`/`QuickOpenModel` mirror `CommandPalettePanel`'s exact ownership/origin-capture/activation-dance idiom (app-wide-scoped, guarded on `fileTreeModel.root == nil`), not `GoToLinePanel`'s single-document scope; a new `RecentFileDocuments` type mirrors `RecentFolderRoots`' own bookmark/MRU/cap/staleness-refresh shape for individual files, not AppKit's unused `NSDocumentController` recents mechanism. Split into 6a (engine-side foundation: index wired to `FileTreeModel.root`, 100k-path performance tests), 6b (`QuickOpenPanel`/`QuickOpenModel` UI + Cmd-P wiring), 6c (Recent Files store + menu) — the same "foundation, then UI, then smaller addition" shape already used for Slice 3's own a/b/c split. Slice 6a begins next.

- 2026-09-27: **Slice 6a landed (PR #156) — `WorkspaceFileIndex` wired to `FileTreeModel.root`, meeting the 100k-path performance budget with real evidence for the first time.** `WindowController` gained a `workspaceFileIndex` property, one per window per §6.15's own decision; a new `WindowController.setFileTreeRoot(_:accessURL:)` (`WindowController+WorkspaceIndex.swift`) wraps `fileTreeModel.setRoot(_:accessURL:)` and keeps the index in sync — rebuilding it against `fileTreeModel.rootAccessURL` after a root is set, or clearing it (new `WorkspaceFileIndex.clear()`) when the root goes nil, so a stale snapshot from a previously-open folder can never leak into a later query. Named distinctly from `WorkspaceModel`'s own pre-existing `setFolderRoot` to avoid confusion between the two, since most call sites already call both back to back. All 5 existing call sites that used to call `fileTreeModel.setRoot` directly now route through it.

  The 100k-path query budget (issue #112's own flat, non-hedged "<30 ms top-result refresh for 100k paths") had zero test evidence before this slice and, once actually measured, missed by more than 10x (~413 ms on a naive first implementation). This was root-caused and fixed with real algorithmic work across two independent hostile-review rounds, not by relaxing the number — the epic's own §6.15 design pass had already flagged this exact discipline in advance ("if 100k paths cannot meet 30 ms on real hardware, that is a finding to act on, not a threshold to quietly loosen"), and it held: fold the query once per `query()` call instead of once per candidate (`FuzzyPathScore.score(foldedQuery:foldedPath:foldedBasename:)`); precompute each `IndexedPath`'s own folded form once at construction (`FuzzyPathScore.FoldedText`, bundling a folded `String` with its `[Unicode.Scalar]` decomposition and a `UInt128` ASCII-presence bitmask); compare `Unicode.Scalar`s instead of `Character`s, since grapheme-cluster boundary detection is real, measurable overhead this doesn't need after folding; gate all basename-level checks (exact, prefix, and subsequence) behind one O(1) bitmask test before doing any string work; and build a genuine inverted postings-list index (`postingsByASCIIByte: [UInt8: [Int]]`) so `query()` only ever scans candidates containing every one of the query's distinct ASCII characters, never the full path list.

  **First independent hostile review** found the app-target wiring and the fuzzy-matching algorithm's own soundness (the single most important property — could the postings/bitmask filtering ever produce a false negative, silently hiding a real file from Quick Open?) both correct, verified via adversarial property tests directly against the real actor, not just hand-tracing. It also found two real problems in the fix's OWN implementation at that point: the performance test itself was flaky (~2 failures in 18 runs, up to 88 ms observed) because `candidateIndices(for:)` picked only the query's single rarest ASCII character's postings list, which wasn't always rare enough alone for reliable margin; and a `WalkGate` semaphore-based test helper (added for a separate, now fully deterministic rebuild-progress test — see below) had no timeout, a theoretical hang risk under thread-pool contention. It also reported the app target failed to build — independently reproduced and traced to the reviewer's own local, stale `MacDown2.xcodeproj` (gitignored, always regenerated via `xcodegen generate` from `project.yml`'s whole-folder `sources: [MacDown2]` inclusion, which CI already runs before every build): not a defect in the commit.

  **Fixed:** `candidateIndices(for:)` now intersects ALL of the query's distinct ASCII characters' postings lists (smallest-first, the standard multi-way set-intersection ordering), not just the rarest one, via a new merge-based `intersectSortedAscending` helper. The performance test now takes the best of 5 trials rather than a single measurement — the same response this project already gave PR #124's own gutter/caret-update benchmark for the identical class of problem (real OS scheduling noise on shared hardware, not an algorithmic defect): `query(_:)` is a pure function of its already-built snapshot, so a genuine regression would still fail every trial including the minimum, while transient noise would not. `WalkGate.waitUntilBlocked()` gained a 5-second timeout as a generous outer safety net, matching `AsyncBarrierWaiting.swift`'s own established convention, with the test now asserting the gate actually signaled rather than assuming it always will.

  Also fixed along the way, before either review round: a self-inflicted flaky test in the qualitative "100k-path index build: off-main, visible progress/ready state" budget's own coverage — the original version raced a real directory walk against `Task.yield()`, hoping the walk was slow enough to still be running when `state` was checked, and failed intermittently on a fast disk. Replaced with a fully deterministic version: `WorkspaceFileIndex` gained an internal, test-only injectable `walk` closure (mirroring `DocumentFileMonitor`'s own established dependency-injection shape for testing actor-based async work), and the test uses a `DispatchSemaphore`-based gate to block the injected walk until the test has synchronously confirmed `state == .building` — zero dependency on real disk-I/O timing.

  **Second, focused independent review of just the fix commit** re-verified the postings-intersection algorithm (correct on empty/single-element edge cases; the postings-list precondition — strictly ascending, duplicate-free per byte — still holds), reconfirmed false-negative soundness with 4 more of its own adversarial tests against the real actor (including a match split unusually across directory vs. filename among 5,000 decoys, and a non-ASCII/CJK query exercising the zero-ASCII-scalar fallback path), ran the performance test 20/20 times itself with zero failures (and empirically caught one real run where OS noise pushed 4 of 5 trials to 63-200ms while the 5th stayed at 26.7ms — direct, first-hand confirmation that best-of-5 does exactly what it claims), and confirmed Swift's per-process (not per-call) hash-seed randomization means the 5 trials within one test invocation cannot see meaningfully different algorithmic costs from each other, so best-of-5 cannot mask a genuine probabilistic regression.

  PR #156 merged (squash) to `master` with `TextSearchTests` (37 tests, including the new performance suite) passing 25+ repeated runs with zero failures after the fix, CI green (`build-and-test` ~20-26 min including the full app+CLI Debug/Release builds and `MacDown2Tests`, `lint` clean) on both the initial and fix-commit CI runs. **Slice 6a is closed.**

  **Aside, discovered while landing this slice, not part of Slice 6a's own scope:** a local full-package-suite run could not be completed on this dev machine across four separate attempts — each confirmed via `sample` to be the identical WebKit/JavaScriptCore thread-idle hang signature already seen earlier in this session's #150/#153 investigation, recurring even after excluding the three diagram-renderer test targets most directly touching `WKWebView`. CI's own fresh-runner execution of the identical commands passed cleanly both times this slice needed it, and this hang's own signature (real progress for tens of seconds, then a complete, consistent stall, with zero overlap to any diff under test) is the same local-environment-degradation pattern already root-caused, not a new one. Tracked as issue #155 for real investigation (not just continued reliance on "CI is green") since a genuine WebKit-touching regression could otherwise hide behind this same excuse. Slice 6b (`QuickOpenPanel`/`QuickOpenModel` UI + Cmd-P wiring) begins next per §6.15/§17.

- 2026-09-27: **Slice 6b landed (PR #157) — the Cmd-P Quick Open panel, closing the fuzzy-navigation UI.** `QuickOpenPanel`/`QuickOpenModel`/`QuickOpenView` mirror `CommandPalettePanel`'s exact ownership/origin-capture/activation-dance idiom per §6.15's own explicit decision, not `GoToLinePanel`'s single-document scope: an `NSPanel` held strongly by `WindowCoordinator`'s new `quickOpen` property for exactly as long as it's open, released via `quickOpenDidClose`, force-closed by `removeController` if its origin window closes. `QuickOpenModel` is the async equivalent of `CommandPaletteModel` — since `WorkspaceFileIndex.query` is an actor call, each query edit spawns a `Task` and bumps a generation counter, discarding any now-superseded in-flight result, the same discipline `EditorFindModel.updateMatches` already established (§8). `toggleQuickOpen()` refuses to open when the key window has no folder root, matching `GoToLinePanel`'s own "nothing useful to do" precedent. Opening a result routes through the existing `WindowCoordinator.openDocument(at:relativeTo:)` dedupe/open path, captured against the origin window rather than `NSApp.keyWindow`. "Quick Open…" (⌘P) added to the File menu.

  An independent hostile review found one real, empirically-confirmed correctness bug and one confirmed-vacuous test. **The bug:** `onOpen` resolved a selected result against the origin's *live* `fileTreeModel.rootAccessURL`, justified by a comment claiming the root "cannot change while this floating panel is open" because closing the folder would already force-close the panel via `removeController`'s own guard — the review proved that false: `removeController` only fires when the *window* closes, never when an already-open window's root changes underneath it (Open Folder…/Open Recent Folder/session restore all switch a window's root via `WindowController.setFileTreeRoot` without ever closing it). A Quick Open panel left open across such a switch could resolve an already-selected, now-stale result against the *new* root — silently opening the wrong file if one happened to share the same relative path, or failing silently otherwise. Fixed by adding `WindowCoordinator.closeQuickOpenIfOrigin(_:)`, called from `setFileTreeRoot` whenever the root changes, mirroring `removeController`'s own "just close it" choice for the equivalent window-closing case. **The vacuous test:** the review proved `rapidTypingDiscardsSupersededQueryResults` couldn't detect a broken generation guard at all — deleting the guard entirely from `QuickOpenModel.scheduleQuery()` still left all 6 existing tests passing, since `WorkspaceFileIndex` is a single actor whose calls always complete in submission order, so the test's natural timing could never produce the inversion the guard exists to prevent. Fixed by adding an injectable `performQuery` seam to `QuickOpenModel` (mirroring `DocumentFileMonitor`'s own established injectable-dependency shape) and a new test, `lateArrivingStaleQueryResultIsDiscarded`, using controlled continuations to force the actual completion-order inversion — verified non-vacuous the same way the review found the old one wasn't: temporarily deleting the guard, confirming only the new test failed, restoring it, confirming green again.

  A second, focused independent review of just the fix commit re-verified both fixes with its own throwaway tests (switching/closing/an-unrelated-window's-root-change scenarios; deleting-and-restoring the generation guard itself) and found both genuinely fixed, correctly, with one minor coverage gap: the shipped diff's own permanent tests only covered the query-generation-guard fix, not the root-change scenario the correctness bug was actually about. Closed before merging: three new tests added to `QuickOpenStaleOriginTests.swift` covering exactly that (switching the origin's root closes the panel; closing it to `nil` closes the panel; an unrelated controller's root change leaves this window's panel alone).

  PR #157 merged (squash) to `master` with the full `MacDown2Tests` suite passing 226/226 (up from 214 before Slice 6 began), CI green (`build-and-test`/`lint`) on the initial and both fix-commit runs. **Slice 6b is closed — this closes the fuzzy-navigation half of Slice 6 in full (engine + UI); only Slice 6c (Recent Files) remains.**

  **Not completed this session, disclosed honestly rather than silently skipped:** a live, on-screen manual click-through of the panel opening/typing/opening-a-file journey. Computer-use/screen-automation access was granted but the screen-takeover approval this app requires timed out waiting for interactive confirmation on two separate attempts; retrying indefinitely without the owner's active attention was judged not a good use of autonomous time. The build/lint/test evidence above is real and extensive (226 passing tests including dedicated origin-lifecycle and root-change regression tests, two hostile review rounds), but a genuinely executed on-screen interaction has not happened yet — recorded here as an open item, not claimed as done.

  **Aside, discovered while landing this slice, not part of Slice 6b's own scope:** the full `MacDown2Tests` suite hit the same already-tracked (#155) local WebKit-flakiness pattern once more, this time as a concrete assertion failure rather than a full hang — `D2ExportRegistryTests.aRealD2FenceComposesIntoSelfContainedHTMLWithNoExternalReferences()` failed with "rendering timed out" during a full-suite run, unrelated to this slice's own diff (zero file overlap), passed cleanly in isolation immediately after (2.12s), and CI's own fresh-runner execution of the identical commit passed cleanly. Added as further evidence to #155 rather than treated as a new issue, since it fits the same "WebKit-touching test fails only under a long, loaded local session" pattern that issue already tracks. Slice 6c (Recent Files store + "Open Recent File" menu) begins next per §6.15/§17.
- 2026-09-28: **Slice 6c implemented, PR #161 opened, closing all of Slice 6.** `RecentFileDocuments` (`FileTree` package) mirrors `RecentFolderRoots`' bookmark/MRU/cap-at-10/staleness-refresh shape for individual documents; `pruneMissingFiles()` (issue #112's "missing-path pruning") runs from `reload()` and from every real file open rather than the SwiftUI `Commands` menu body, which has no reliable per-open lifecycle hook. Recording is centralized in `WindowCoordinator.openDocument(at:...)`, the single choke point every real file open already routes through, so session restore (which bypasses that method) never re-promotes already-open documents on relaunch. Also implements issue #112's "Recent-file history feeds Quick Open ranking": `WorkspaceFileIndex.query` gained a `recentRelativePaths` parameter adding a small (+10), tier-safe bonus — smaller than every gap between `FuzzyPathScore`'s own score tiers — verified via tests that re-derive the actual tier math by hand, confirming it can only break ties within a tier, never promote one across tiers.

  An independent hostile review found one real bug: `openRecentFile(_:)` opened `resolution.accessURL` (the security-scoped bookmark's physical, symlink-resolved target) instead of `resolution.lexicalURL`, contradicting its own doc comment's claim to mirror `openRecentFolder(_:)`. Investigating the reviewer's own proposed repro (a symlinked *file*) found it was actually unreachable — `FileStore`'s pre-existing `metadata(at:)` uses `FileManager.attributesOfItem(atPath:)`, which does not traverse a symlink at the final path component, so a symlinked file already fails to open at all (`.notRegularFile`) via any path, unrelated to this fix. The underlying code defect was still real and worth fixing (a symlinked *parent directory*, e.g. an aliased folder, is the reachable case where lexical/access URLs genuinely diverge for an openable file) — fixed, with `WindowCoordinatorRecentFileTests.swift` covering that corrected scenario directly rather than the reviewer's own unreachable one.

  Package-level verification: 85/85 `FileTree`/`TextSearch`/`RecentFileDocuments` tests passing, including the 30ms/100k-path Quick Open performance test (unaffected by the new ranking bonus — measured directly, A/B, with and without the bonus code path, confirming negligible overhead). App-level: 228/228 `MacDown2Tests` passing across three separate local runs, aside from the already-tracked #155 WebKit pattern recurring a third time (this time as system-wide `WebContent` process exhaustion severe enough to stall even non-WebKit async tests to 140+ seconds before a crash) — new evidence added to #155 rather than re-investigated here, per this project's own established precedent for that issue. **PR #161 merged (squash) to `master` at `adb7a9b3` with CI green (`build-and-test` 21m16s, `lint`) — Slice 6c is closed, completing all of Slice 6 (engine + UI + recents) in full.**
- 2026-09-28: **Fixed a real, independently-reported raw-editor defect alongside Slice 6c, kept in its own separate PR (#162) per this epic's own "one coherent concern per PR" discipline:** the line-number gutter drew each label at its `NSTextLayoutFragment`'s own top edge, not the real text's baseline — `EditorConfiguration.lineHeightMultiple` (default 1.2) makes each line's box taller than the font's natural line height and pushes the baseline down within it, so a label drawn at the naive top rendered measurably higher than the text it numbers. Fixed by having `EditorTextSystem.enumerateVisibleLineFragments` report each fragment's own real, TextKit-computed baseline (`frame.minY + firstLine.typographicBounds.minY + firstLine.glyphOrigin.y`, verified empirically to be a pure font/paragraph-style metric, identical across every line including an empty one) and drawing via CoreText's own baseline-relative `textPosition`/`CTLineDraw` rather than `NSString.draw(in:withAttributes:)`, whose positioning-within-a-rect has no way to reproduce `lineHeightMultiple`'s effect. `GutterLineLabel.minY` renamed to `baselineY`. Five new deterministic geometry tests (`EditorGutterBaselineAlignmentTests.swift`) independently re-derive TextKit's own expected baseline through a different API entry point than the fix itself uses, rather than re-running the same code path — first-line correctness, 50-line no-drift, empty-line parity, and that the offset genuinely scales with `lineHeightMultiple`/font size rather than a hardcoded constant. All 565 `EditorCore` package tests pass unchanged; the app build succeeds; a clean full local `MacDown2Tests` run could not be obtained due to the same severe, already-tracked (#155) local-session degradation noted above (new evidence added there too), unrelated to this diff (zero file overlap with anything WebKit/QuickOpen/process-related). No pixel-level on-screen check was possible in this environment — disclosed as a residual limitation rather than claimed. **PR #162 merged (squash) to `master` at `2c720ac1` with CI green (`build-and-test` 30m16s, `lint`).**

Also records two items owned by other, dedicated sessions, neither touched by this epic's own work: the LC-05 SwiftTreeSitter `Package.resolved` pin (reverted after it failed to resolve; owned by #148) and a `FileCore` red-`master` test, `parentVanishedLatchSurvivesAChangedEventDuringDebounce()`, being root-caused separately. Confirmed this epic's own CI runs (PRs #161/#162, both green) are unaffected by either.
- 2026-09-28: Wrote Slice 7's own design pass (§6.16) before implementation began, per a fresh baseline read confirming `TextSearchEngine`/`WorkspaceFileIndex`/`FileStore`'s conditional-publication write already exist and are reusable verbatim, while `WorkspaceSearchEngine` and any replacement-plan type do not exist at all yet. Decided the open architecture question this slice raises: folder search gets a new, persistent `SidebarSection.search` panel (`SidebarSection`'s own `reconcile(_:)` already supports a new case cleanly), not a floating `NSPanel` like every prior E22 panel — the same "used for far longer per session" reasoning §6.14 already used to depart from that precedent for current-document Find, applied one level further. Split into 7a (engine: `WorkspaceSearchEngine`/`FolderSearchFilter`, streaming, cancellation, binary/symlink/permission safety, bounded accumulation with explicit truncation), 7b (sidebar search UI), 7c (Replace in Folder preview + `FileStore`-backed safe write, reusing its existing `expectedRevision`/`.fileChangedDuringRead` conditional-publication path exactly per invariant #6) — see §17. Slice 7a begins next, once PRs #161/#162 are reconciled.
- 2026-09-29: **Slice 7a landed (PR #167, squash-merged to `master` at `56c699ad`), closing all four findings a review pass raised against the first implementation before merge.** (1) `FolderSearchFilter` originally shipped with only an `includedExtensions: Set<String>?` — replaced with glob-style `includeGlobs`/`excludeGlobs` (new `GlobPattern` type, `*`/`**`, exclude-wins precedence) matching §6.16's actual decided contract; the binary-skip policy is documented as an unconditional engine invariant rather than a second stored flag, since there is no byte-level search path a toggle could meaningfully switch to. (2) Issue #112's hidden-file toggle was structurally impossible, since `DirectoryWalker` dropped every hidden entry at walk time before it ever reached the index — `IndexedPath` now carries `isHidden` (propagated to every descendant of a hidden directory, not just dotfile basenames), `WorkspaceFileIndex.allPaths(includeHidden:)` is the new opt-in, and `query(_:)` (Quick Open) keeps filtering hidden entries out unconditionally, verified by tests on both sides. (3) Cancellation had a real gap: `Task.isCancelled` was checked once per file, before it was read, so a cancellation landing while a (possibly slow) file's matches were being computed or published could still let one more result through and turn `.cancelled` into a late `.completed`/`.truncated` — now rechecked at both of those points too, proven by two new deterministic tests (a second test seam, `afterMatchingFile`, pausing the engine exactly where the gap was) rather than a timing race. (4) Result bounding was cosmetic — the engine called `TextSearchEngine.matches` unbounded and only sliced the returned array afterward, so a single pathological file could still force an unbounded allocation; `TextSearchEngine.matches` now takes an early-stop `matchLimit`, verified directly and end-to-end. A follow-up commit fixed the `swiftlint --strict` violations this produced (a few functions/files over their line/complexity/parameter limits) by extracting `DirectoryWalker` to its own file, splitting `WorkspaceSearchEngine.search` into `search`/`apply`/`processFile`, and splitting the now-oversized test file into two — no behavior change. Final state: 69/69 tests passing across 6 suites, `swiftlint lint --strict` clean, full `xcodebuild` gate green, CI green (`lint`, `build-and-test`, `inventory`). Cleaned up two unrelated but real hazards found along the way: a stray iCloud "conflicted copy" duplicate Swift file in the working tree that could have caused a duplicate-symbol build error, and the underlying cause of a long stretch of environmental build/git flakiness this session hit — `~/Documents/GitHub` was itself inside iCloud Drive's "Desktop & Documents Folders" sync, racing the compiler on unrelated files (`FileFormat.swift`, `Themes.swift`, etc., reproduced 5 times, never once on this diff's own files); relocated to `~/GitHub` with every git worktree repaired (`git worktree repair`), confirmed by full rebuild/retest/CI all going green immediately after. Slice 7b (sidebar search UI) begins next.
